**Why:** v3 shows raw numbers and the v4 plan shows three-level labels, so neither tells a user how far a result is from good or what to fix first. This finding supplies concrete THRESHOLDS values for all four presets, a smooth score that A/B compare and the benchmark can average, and per-metric 'points lost' so the UI can rank fixes. The lexicographic compareMetrics stays for the optimizer. The score is only for display and reports.

**Sketch:** New src/core/score.js (pure JS). (1) erfc via Abramowitz-Stegun 7.1.26: t=1/(1+0.3275911|x|), y=t(0.254829592+t(-0.284496736+t(1.421413741+t(-1.453152027+t*1.061405429)))), erf=1-y*exp(-x^2), erfc(x)=1-sign(x)*erf. (2) logNormalScore(value,{p10,median}): if value<=0 return 1; standardized = ln(value/median) * 0.9061938024368232 / (-ln(p10/median)); s = erfc(standardized)/2. 0.9061938 is erfc^-1(0.2), so s(p10)=0.9 and s(median)=0.5. Clamp to the band the value is in, as Lighthouse does: value<=p10 gives [0.9,1]; value<=median gives [0.5,0.8999]; otherwise [0,0.4999]. For metrics where higher is better, score x=1-eff instead. (3) Metrics are 'excess' values where 0 is ideal: dSD = area-weighted mean SD/4 - 1; dAng = area-weighted mean corner angle error in degrees; dArea = area-weighted mean |log2(areaUV*gs^2/area3D)|; tdCV = texel-density coefficient of variation across charts; waste = 1 - textureEfficiency (see the efficiency finding); seamN = seamLength3D/sqrt(sum A3); frag = chartCount/sqrt(F). (4) Starting control points as [p10, median]. game_hero: dSD [0.01,0.05], dAng [1,4], dArea [0.05,0.20], tdCV [0.02,0.10], waste [0.35,0.55], seamN [1.5,4], frag [0.1,0.5]. game_prop: dSD [0.02,0.10], dAng [2,6], dArea [0.08,0.30], tdCV [0.05,0.20], waste [0.30,0.50], seamN [1.5,5], frag [0.15,0.8]. lightmap: dSD [0.03,0.15], dAng [3,8], dArea [0.10,0.20] (0.20 = log2(1.15), matching Unity's 15% area-error default), tdCV [0.02,0.10], waste [0.35,0.55], frag [0.1,0.6], no seam term. film_udim: dSD [0.005,0.025], dAng [0.5,2], dArea [0.03,0.12], tdCV [0.01,0.05], waste [0.5,0.75], seamN [1,3]. Weights, in the order SD/angle/area+TD/waste/seams/frag: hero .25/.10/.20/.20/.15/.10; prop .20/.10/.15/.25/.15/.15; lightmap .10/.15/.20/.35/0/.20; film .35/.15/.25/.05/.20/0. (5) Gates: total = min(weighted mean, cap). The cap is 0.49 if there are flips, degenerate-but-used faces, unintended overlaps, or UVs outside [0,1] on a 0-1 preset. It is 0.89 if the mip-bleed check fails at the preset's target mip, or if a sub-texel chart exists on the lightmap preset. Show the gate that capped the score as the first issue. (6) Explanations: for each metric compute gain_i = w_i*(max(s_i,0.9)-s_i)*100 and list the top 3 as 'Reduce stretch: +12 pts'. Link each item to an action (relax, repack with padding X, raise resolution). (7) Calibration, Lighthouse style: run the benchmark corpus through xatlas defaults and Blender Smart UV Project defaults (imported as reference UVs). Set median = median of those default results and p10 = 10th-best percentile across all tools. Store the fitted numbers in score.js with a version string so reported scores are reproducible. Cost is O(#metrics), negligible. Tests: score(p10)=0.9 and score(median)=0.5 to 1e-6; monotone; gates cap.

**Risks:** The starting control points are engineering judgement until they are calibrated on a corpus. Users may treat the number as absolute across presets, so always show the preset name next to it. Seam and fragmentation targets depend heavily on the model (a cube needs about 7 seam edges), so give them low weight or normalise them by a per-model baseline such as the Blender Smart UV result. Lighthouse's exact erfc approximation and clamp code were recalled from memory. The log-normal shape and the control-point semantics were confirmed in the docs.

**Sources:** Google Lighthouse, Performance scoring (log-normal curve, control points, weighted mean, 0-49/50-89/90-100 bands), 2023-2025: https://developer.chrome.com/docs/lighthouse/performance/performance-scoring; Lighthouse source, shared/statistics.js getLogNormalScore (constants recalled from memory, not re-read this session): https://github.com/GoogleChrome/lighthouse; OptCuts (Li, Kaufman, Kazhdan, Jiang, SIGGRAPH Asia 2018) distortion bound 4.1 on symmetric Dirichlet, i.e. 1.025 when divided by 4 (from memory; same bound already in ARCHITECTURE.md qualityScore): https://www.cs.ubc.ca/labs/imager/tr/2018/OptCuts/

### F22. Texture-efficiency accounting: stretch x packing efficiency, 'equivalent resolution', waste waterfall, and the L2-optimal chart scale

*Impact* high · *Effort* S · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/core/metrics.js (Es, L2opt, textureEff, waterfall), src/core/pack.js (equalize: 'area'|'l2'), ui charts (waterfall bar), report export`

**What:** In MCGIM, stretch efficiency is total 3D area divided by total 2D chart area once the map is scaled to unit L2 stretch. That equals 1/L2^2 for the normalised Sander L2. Packing efficiency is summed chart area divided by the texture domain area. Texture efficiency is their product: the fraction of the texture that does useful, uniformly sampled work. Taking the square root of texture efficiency gives an equivalent isometric resolution, which users read easily ('your 2048 atlas samples like a perfect 1680 atlas'). The waste can also be split into additive causes.

**Why:** The plan reports coverage, efficiency, stretchL2 and texel-density CV separately and never combines them. This finding turns them into one physically meaningful number that already includes padding, voids, stretch and density policy, and it shows where texels are lost. It also exposes a real trade-off the plan does not model. Area-equalised density (the plan's pack s = sqrt(A3/A2)) is not the L2-optimal scaling. The optimal per-chart scale lowers L2 by a measurable amount, which matters most for lightmaps and procedural bakes.

**Sketch:** In metrics.js, computed in the existing single pass. Per face T with Sander singular values of the UV-to-3D map (Gamma = 1/sigma2, gamma = 1/sigma1 from math.svd2 of the 3D-to-UV Jacobian): L2_T^2 = (1/sigma1^2 + 1/sigma2^2)/2. Per chart c: Q_c = sum_T L2_T^2*A3_T, A2_c = sum |A2_T|, A3_c. (1) Global normalised L2: L2g^2 = (sum_c Q_c)(sum_c A2_c)/(sum A3)^2. This is invariant to a global scale and is at least 1. (2) Stretch efficiency Es = 1/L2g^2. (3) L2-optimal per-chart scale: scaling chart c by k_c gives L2^2 = (sum Q_c/k_c^2)(sum k_c^2 A2_c)/(sum A3)^2. By Cauchy-Schwarz the minimum is L2opt^2 = (sum_c sqrt(Q_c*A2_c))^2/(sum A3)^2, reached at k_c^2 proportional to sqrt(Q_c/A2_c). Under area-equalised packing (k_c^2 = A3_c/A2_c) the value is L2eq^2 = sum_c(Q_c*A2_c/A3_c)/sum A3. Report densityPolicyEff = L2opt^2/L2eq^2 (at most 1) and add pack option equalize: 'area' | 'l2' that uses k_c = (Q_c/A2_c)^(1/4) before the secant scale search. (4) Packing terms from pack.js outputs, all fractions of R^2: extentEff = extW*extH/R^2; voidEff = coverage*R^2/(extW*extH); paddingEff = chartCoverage/coverage; packingEff = sum exact A2 (texel^2)/R^2, which is about the product of the three (keep the exact value and show the raster mismatch as 'rasterisation'). (5) textureEff = packingEff * Es. equivalentResolution = R*sqrt(textureEff). effectiveTexelsPerUnit = R*sqrt(textureEff/sum A3). (6) Waste waterfall for the UI: start at R^2 texels and subtract, in order, unused extent R^2(1-extentEff), voids, padding, stretch oversampling R^2*packingEff*(1-Es), and density-policy loss. Draw it as a horizontal stacked bar (Chart.js is already vendored). (7) Per-chart table column 'texel share vs. need' = (k_c^2 A2_c/sum)/(A3_c/sum A3) highlights charts that hog space. Tests: isometric unit-square chart packed alone at R with zero padding gives Es=1 and textureEff about 1; a 2x anisotropic chart gives Es = 1/L2^2 with L2 = sqrt((1+1/4)/2)*sqrt(2)... (derive from the plan's fixture); two identical charts scaled 1 and 2 give densityPolicyEff < 1 under 'area' only if their Q differ. O(F).

**Risks:** L2-optimal scaling makes texel density uneven, which artists dislike on albedo (text and brick patterns change scale), so keep 'area' as the default for game and film presets and offer 'l2' for lightmap and bake-only use. Flipped faces make sigma2 <= 0, so exclude them from Q (they are already gated). Box Cutter and Liu 2019 definitions differ slightly (bounding square vs rectangle), so label which one is used.

**Sources:** Sander, Wood, Gortler, Snyder, Hoppe, 'Multi-chart Geometry Images', SGP 2003 (definitions of stretch, packing and texture efficiency); definitions confirmed via the patent text: https://patents.google.com/patent/US7586488B2/en and project page https://hhoppe.com/proj/mcgim/; Sander, Snyder, Gortler, Hoppe, 'Texture Mapping Progressive Meshes', SIGGRAPH 2001 (L2/Linf stretch, chart scaling by stretch): https://hhoppe.com/proj/tmpm/; Limper, Vining, Vining, Alexa, 'Box Cutter: Atlas Refinement for Efficient Packing via Void Elimination', ACM TOG 37(4) 2018, and Liu, Fu, Ye, Chai, Liu, 'Atlas Refinement with Bounded Packing Efficiency', ACM TOG 38(4) 2019 (packing efficiency = chart area / bounding square area; from memory, not re-read)

### F23. Mip-aware bleed and gutter analysis (Unity bilinear-neighbourhood rule applied per mip level) plus a bleed simulator preview

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `new src/core/bake.js (chart raster, pyramid, conflict test, dilation); metrics.js calls it (mipSafe fields); pack.js blockAlign and a 'targetMip' padding helper; ui/uvview red bleed overlay; viewport bleed simulator`

**What:** An exact raster test that reports, per chart and globally, the lowest mip level down to which no texel sampled for a chart contains another chart's data. It uses the same criterion Unity's UV Overlap draw mode uses at level 0 and repeats it on every level of a box-filtered mip pyramid of chart IDs. It also gives a padding recommendation for a target mip and a visual preview of the contamination on the 3D model.

**Why:** The plan's 'padding vs resolution' check only compares a number with a number, and overlapTexels only tests the un-padded charts at level 0. Real bleeding appears at lower mips and depends on grid alignment. This test gives the exact answer for the actual layout (for example 'safe down to 256 px; charts 12 and 40 bleed at 128 px'). It also works on imported UVs, and it ties into the plan's unused blockAlign option: with 2^L alignment the gap needed falls from 3*2^L-1 to 2*2^L texels.

**Sketch:** New src/core/bake.js. (1) Chart-ID raster I at R*R (Int32Array, EMPTY=-1). Reuse pack.js conservative triangle rasterisation so any texel a triangle touches is marked; a texel hit by two charts is MIXED=-2 (a real overlap). (2) Pyramid: B_0 = I. For each level, B_{L+1}[i,j] combines the 4 children, ignoring EMPTY: all non-empty ids equal gives that id, none gives EMPTY, otherwise MIXED. Store a second id plane so MIXED blocks remember two of their contributing ids for attribution. Total memory 4/3 R^2. (3) Neighbourhood rule at level L (Unity: two charts conflict if the bilinear 2x2 neighbourhoods of points inside them share a texel, which means their blocks are within Chebyshev distance <= 2) is equivalent to 'any 3x3 window of B_L holds two distinct ids or a MIXED block'. Implement as separable min/max filters over non-empty ids: a horizontal 3-tap pass writing minH/maxH (Int32Array, empty = +INF/-INF, MIXED forces a flag), then a vertical 3-tap pass. A conflict is min != max or the flag. Record the pair (min,max) in a Map keyed min*2^20+max with the first level seen. O(n_L^2) per level; about 60 ms at 2048^2 for level 0 in a worker. For wrap = 'repeat' (tiling textures) index modulo n_L; for 'clamp' (atlases, lightmaps) do not. Bicubic mode uses a 5x5 window (Unity's 4-texel rule). (4) Outputs: perChartSafeMip[c] = (first conflicting level) - 1 (-1 means it already fails at base, which is overlap or a gutter under 2 texels); globalSafeMip; safeResolution = R >> globalSafeMip; conflictPairs; bleedMask (Uint8 per base texel, set when any ancestor window conflicted, drawn red like Unity). (5) Padding advice for a target level Lt with bilinear sampling: aligned packing (blockAlign snaps placements to multiples of 2^Lt) needs gap >= 2*2^Lt texels, the same as Plarium's (R/Rmin)*2. Unaligned worst case needs a base-texel distance of 3*2^Lt, i.e. an empty gap of 3*2^Lt - 1, because floor differences can lose one block. Preset defaults: hero 4096 with target 512 (Lt=3) gives 16 px aligned / 23 px unaligned; prop 2048 with target 256 (Lt=3) gives 16/23, or accept 512 (Lt=2) for 8/11; lightmap at the lightmap resolution with Lt=0 gives 2 (bilinear) or 4 (bicubic); film UDIM 4096 per tile with Lt=2 gives 8. (6) Bleed simulator (main thread, Canvas2D, no WebGL2 needed): paint each chart a distinct hue into ImageData at R, apply edge padding by propagating labels outward by up to the gutter width (two-pass 8-neighbour chamfer propagation carrying the label, O(R^2)), box-downsample to level L, and use that canvas as the model's map with NearestFilter and no mipmaps. Wrong colours along seams are exactly the bleed. Add a slider 'mip level 0..log2R'. Tests: two squares with a 1 px gap fail at L=0; with a 16 px aligned gap they are safe through L=3 and fail at L=4; repeat mode detects bleeding across the atlas border.

**Risks:** The Unity rule and the Plarium formula were confirmed. The pyramid formulation and the 3*2^L-1 worst-case bound are my own derivation and need unit tests. The test assumes box-filtered power-of-two mips; Kaiser or other mip filters with wider support need 1-2 more blocks, so expose a 'filter taps' option. At R >= 8192 memory is about 350 MB for Int32 planes, so run at min(R, 4096) or use Int16 ids when fewer than 32k charts. Anisotropic filtering samples along a line and can exceed the bilinear footprint, so say so in the UI.

**Sources:** Unity Manual, 'Fixing lightmap UV overlap / UV overlap feedback' (bilinear neighbourhood = 4 nearest texels; charts too close if neighbourhoods of any interior points overlap; at least 2 texels between charts for bilinear, 4 for bicubic): https://docs.unity3d.com/2022.3/Documentation/Manual/ProgressiveLightmapper-UVOverlap.html; Plarium, 'Pixel Padding and Bleeding Detection in 3D Modeling' (padding at base = (BasicResolution / MinimumResolution) x PaddingOnMinTexture; kernel K = P+1; red/green/blue markings): https://company.plarium.com/en/articles/pixel-padding-and-bleeding-detection-in-3d-modeling/; Practitioner guidance, 8-16 px gutter at bake resolution; 1024 with 8 px means 4/2/1 px at 512/256/128: https://www.tripo3d.ai/blog/explore/smart-mesh-uv-padding-rules-to-prevent-mip-bleeding (secondary; polycount wiki Edge_padding was unreachable)

### F24. Bake-readiness gate sets per pipeline (lightmap / game hero / game prop / film UDIM) with the defaults professional tools use

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `new src/core/bake.js (presets and checks); src/core/metrics.js (inputs); src/core/pack.js (margin from minLightmapRes/objectScale); main.js preset dropdown, issue list and fix actions`

**What:** A concrete, data-driven checklist that fills THRESHOLDS for each output target. Each check is PASS, WARN or FAIL with a measured value, the threshold, and a one-click fix mapped to engine ops. It covers target-specific checks the plan does not list: lightmap margin computed from the runtime texel density, sub-texel or unlit charts, hard edges that are not seams (normal-map bakes), mirrored or stacked islands, UDIM tile straddling, and UE-style overlap percentages.

**Why:** The plan lists 'bake-readiness checks (flips, overlap, out-of-range, padding vs resolution, TD CV) shown as toasts' with no numbers and no target-specific rules. This finding supplies thresholds that trace back to Unity and Unreal documentation and adds 6 failure modes that break real bakes but pass the plan's checks.

**Sketch:** src/core/bake.js exports BAKE_PRESETS and checkBakeReadiness(mesh, uv, faceChart, metrics, preset) -> Array<{id, level, value, threshold, message, fix}>. Presets. lightmap: {range:'0-1', unique:true, stackedAllowed:false, gapTexels:2 (bicubic 4), res:'minLightmapRes', targetMip:0, hardAngleDeg:88, angleErrPct:8, areaErrPct:15, minCentresPerChart:4, tdCVMax:0.05}. game_hero: {range:'0-1', unique:false, stackedAllowed:'warn', R:4096, gapTexels:16, targetMip:3, tdTolLog2:0.1375 (+/-10%), sdP95Max:1.05, hardEdgesMustBeSeams:true, subTexelFaceFracMax:0.01}. game_prop: {R:2048, gapTexels:8, targetMip:2, tdTolLog2:0.32 (+/-25%), sdP95Max:1.10}. film_udim: {range:'udim', unique:true, stackedAllowed:false, R:4096 per tile, gapTexels:8, targetMip:2, tdCVMax:0.03 across tiles, sdP95Max:1.03, straddleForbidden:true}. Checks. (1) Unity-style angle and area error: angleErr_corner = |theta_uv - theta_3d|/theta_3d*100, areaErr_face = |a_uv/sum a_uv - a_3d/sum a_3d|/(a_3d/sum a_3d)*100. Report the area-weighted p95 against 8% and 15%. Unity's exact definition is not published, so label them 'Unity-like'. (2) Lightmap margin with Margin Method = Calculate: runtime texels per world unit t = minLightmapRes*minObjectScale; the world length of one UV unit is Lw = sqrt(sum A3/sum A2_uv); the required UV margin is m_uv = gapTexels/(t*Lw). Since sum A2_uv depends on the margin, iterate 2-3 times inside pack's secant scale search. (3) Sub-texel and unlit charts: count texel centres inside each chart using the top-left fill rule at R. FAIL (lightmap) when a chart has fewer than 1 centre (it gets no data and renders black), WARN below 4. Report the fraction of faces with |A2|*R^2 < 0.25. (4) Hard edges not on seams: for each interior edge with dihedral > hardAngle, or with split normals in imported glTF/OBJ (different normal indices on the two sides), require that it is a seam. Report count and 3D length, with fix = add those edges to the cut and re-flatten, because tangent-space normal bakes show shading seams otherwise (common practice, not verified this session). (5) Stacked or mirrored islands: see the overlap finding. They are allowed with a warning for hero/prop albedo and FAIL for lightmap, AO and film. (6) UDIM: tile = 1001 + floor(u) + 10*floor(v). FAIL if a chart's vertices span two tiles (floor over the chart's u,v with eps 1e-6). Run the padding and mip checks per tile, with u,v taken modulo 1. (7) Overlap %, UE style: overlapTexels/coveredTexels*100 at the lightmap resolution, next to the exact overlap area % from the overlap finding. (8) Out of range: FAIL when any u,v is outside [0,1] (plus half a texel for lightmaps) on 0-1 presets. (9) Padding and mip: FAIL when bake.js globalSafeMip < targetMip. (10) Texel density: FAIL when any chart has |log2(td/target)| > tdTol. UX: sort by severity; each item has 'Show' (frame the chart in the UV and 3D views) and 'Fix' buttons (repack with gapTexels, raise R, mark hard edges as seams, split straddling charts, unstack). Include the check list with values in exportReport JSON.

**Risks:** The Unity defaults and the 2/4-texel rule are verified. The hero, prop and film thresholds are industry rules of thumb (secondary sources) and should be editable. Unity's exact angle and area error formulas and UE's overlap-percentage computation are not published, so label ours as approximations. The hard-edge-to-seam rule depends on the baker (MikkTSpace-synced bakers tolerate some cases), so make it WARN, not FAIL, except for the hero preset.

**Sources:** Unity Manual, Lightmap UV settings reference: Hard Angle 88 deg, Angle Error 8%, Area Error 15%, Pack Margin 4 px assuming the mesh fills a 1024 lightmap (range 1-64), Margin Method Manual/Calculate using Min Lightmap Resolution and Min Object Scale: https://docs.unity3d.com/Manual/LightingGiUvs-Reference.html; Unity Manual, UV overlap: at least 2 texels between charts for bilinear, 4 for bicubic: https://docs.unity3d.com/2022.3/Documentation/Manual/ProgressiveLightmapper-UVOverlap.html; Unreal Engine 4.27, Generating Lightmap UVs (Min Lightmap Resolution drives padding; charts normalised to equal density; no overlapping or wrapping islands): https://docs.unrealengine.com/4.27/en-US/WorkingWithContent/Types/StaticMeshes/AutoGeneratedLightmaps/; Unreal forum, 'Lightmap UV are overlapping by 40%' (percentage-style warning): https://forums.unrealengine.com/t/error-lightmap-uv-are-overlapping-by-40/663

### F25. Texel-density toolkit: px/m readouts, target TD with 'Set TD', auto texture-size / UDIM-count suggestion, viewing-distance TD, log2 heatmap

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/core/metrics.js (absolute TD fields), src/core/engine.js (setTexelDensity op), src/core/pack.js (fixed-scale mode, UDIM multi-tile), ui/uvview + viewport heat mode 'td', main.js units, target and calculator panel`

**What:** Texel density TD = sqrt(texel area / world area) in px/m. Artists work to a project-wide target: about 1024 px/m (10.24 px/cm) for first-person or hero assets and about 512 px/m (5.12 px/cm) for third-person or background props. Tools such as Texel Density Checker, RizomUV and Maya read out TD, set it per island, and colour meshes by deviation from the target.

**Why:** The plan only reports relative density (CV, 1.0 when equalised) because OBJ has no units and pack scales to fill the atlas. Professionals think in absolute px/m against a project target and need to know whether it fits the texture. This finding adds world-unit awareness, a 'Set TD' operation, automatic texture-size and UDIM-count suggestions, and a camera-distance calculator. None of these exist in v3 or the plan.

**Sketch:** (1) Units: glTF is in metres; OBJ, STL and PLY get a 'units' dropdown (m, cm, mm, inch) that sets metresPerUnit. (2) Per face: TD_f = sqrt(|A2_f|*W*H/(A3_f*metresPerUnit^2)) px/m. Per chart: TD_c = sqrt(sum|A2|*W*H/(sum A3*mpu^2)), which is the area-weighted form. Show px/m and px/cm (divide by 100). (3) Heatmap mode 'td': x = log2(TD_f/TD_target), diverging colormap clamped to +/-1 (a factor of 2); green band |x| <= log2(1.1) = 0.1375; blue means under-density, red means over. (4) Set TD (engine op 'setTexelDensity', args {target, scope: 'all'|'selectedCharts', W, H}): k = TD_target/TD_c; scale the chart's UVs about the chart's UV centroid by k; then for scope 'all' run pack with equalizeDensity and fixed scale (skip the secant search and use D = TD_target*mpu*... in texels per unit), or report overflow. (5) Fit check and suggestion: needed texture side S = TD_target*mpu*sqrt(sum A3/packingEffEstimate), where packingEffEstimate is the last pack's efficiency or 0.7 by default. Suggest nextPow2(S). For UDIM, tiles = ceil(sum A3*mpu^2*TD_target^2/(W*H*packingEff)) and pack per tile. (6) Viewing-distance calculator (pinhole model): screen pixels per metre at distance d = Hs/(2*d*tan(fovY/2)). Show 'TD needed for 1:1 texel:pixel at d' and the mip level the GPU will pick, log2(TD/needed). For example 1080 px, 60 deg FOV, 3 m gives 1080/(2*3*0.577) = 312 px/m, so a 1024 px/m asset renders about 1.7 mips down. The bleed check should target that mip. (7) Presets store TD_target and a tolerance: hero 1024 px/m +/-10%, prop 512 px/m +/-25%, film per project with +/-5% across UDIM tiles, lightmap in texels per unit. (8) Per-chart table columns: TD, deviation %, UV-space %. Tests: a 1 m x 1 m plane at TD 1024 on a 1K texture covers exactly the full 0-1 square; on 2K it covers 1/4; setTexelDensity reaches the target within 1e-6.

**Risks:** The TD standards come from practitioner and secondary sources, not a formal standard, so keep them editable. OBJ files with unknown units will give wrong absolute numbers unless the user sets units, so show a units badge prominently. Fixed-scale packing can overflow 0-1; handle it by suggesting a larger texture or UDIM rather than silently rescaling.

**Sources:** Beyond Extent, Texel Density deep dive (10.24 px/cm first-person / 5.12 px/cm third-person conventions): https://www.beyondextent.com/deep-dives/deepdive-texeldensity; Polycount discussion, texel density standards for AAA FPS: https://polycount.com/discussion/234887/texel-density-standards-for-aaa-first-person-shooter-games; Texel Density Checker Blender add-on (mrven), which reads out and sets px/cm and px/m per island (recalled from memory): https://github.com/mrven/Blender-Texel-Density-Checker

### F26. Exact, robust overlap validation: adaptive orient2d, winding-count raster cross-check, overlap-area %, and stacked/mirrored island classification

*Impact* medium · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/core/robust.js; src/core/metrics.js (bijectivity, overlapArea, overlapTexelPct, stacked); src/core/bake.js reuses the winding raster; ui/uvview draws intersection points and stacked badges`

**What:** Plain float segment-intersection tests fail in UV layouts because charts touch exactly (collinear shared padding boundaries, snapped texel coordinates, Float32 round-off). Robust predicates make 'intersect or not' exact. A winding-count raster independently catches folds and multi-coverage. Clipping gives overlap area so users see how much overlaps, not just whether anything does. Deliberately stacked or mirrored islands (shared texture space) are classified separately so they are not all reported as errors.

**Why:** The plan's bijectivity test (uniform grid, boundary intersection, point-in-polygon containment) has no exact arithmetic and outputs only booleans and pairs. Imported artist UVs, which usually contain intentional stacks, would all fail. This finding adds exactness on degenerate touching cases, an overlap-% number compatible with UE's warning, a second raster-based validation path for tests, and correct handling of stacked islands and UDIM tiles.

**Sketch:** (1) src/core/robust.js: inline orient2d (fast float filter with errbound 3.33e-16*(|detleft|+|detright|), then the adaptive expansion stages) from robust-predicates, wrapped as a classic IIFE registered in registry.js. It is about 6 KB. Segment test: properIntersect when orient(a,b,c)*orient(a,b,d) < 0 and orient(c,d,a)*orient(c,d,b) < 0. Touching (any orient = 0 with collinear overlap) goes to a separate 'touching' count, which is allowed when gap = 0 is legal. Always run on Float64 copies of the UVs. (2) Candidate pairs: the plan's uniform grid over boundary edges (cell = median edge length) plus a face-level grid for charts flagged by boundary crossing or containment. (3) Overlap area: for each candidate triangle pair from different charts, or non-adjacent triangles in the same chart, clip triangle A against B's 3 half-planes (Sutherland-Hodgman, at most 6 vertices) and add the shoelace area. overlapAreaFraction = sum/sum|A2|. Complexity O(F + k). (4) Winding raster cross-check at R (default 1024): for each triangle, add sign(A2) at every texel centre inside using the top-left fill rule, so shared edges are counted once; also keep an unsigned count (Uint8, saturating). A texel is bad if the unsigned count >= 2. A fold has signed 0 and unsigned 2; a flip-free overlap has signed 2. overlapTexelPct = bad/covered*100 (UE style). Tests assert the raster and exact methods agree up to edge texels. (5) Stacked or mirrored classification: per chart compute the descriptor {A2, perimeter, centroid, second moments Ixx, Iyy, Ixy about the centroid, faces, A3}. Two charts are stacked if their centroids are within 0.5 texel, A2 and the moments are within 1%, face counts are equal, and the symmetric Hausdorff distance between boundary vertex sets (grid lookup) is under 1 texel. Mark them mirrored if their signed-area signs differ. Overlaps entirely explained by stacks go to metrics.bijectivity.stacked: [[a,b,mirrored]] and not to overlappingPairs; the preset decides whether that is legal. Mirrored islands also get a note that tangent w = -1 (a MikkTSpace handedness flip) is needed. (6) UDIM: before testing, bucket charts by tile (floor(u), floor(v)); charts in different tiles cannot overlap. (7) Self-overlap inside one chart with no flips (a boundary that winds around itself) is found by the same boundary test restricted to the chart; report it as selfIntersectingCharts with the exact intersection points for the UV view to draw.

**Risks:** The robust-predicates licence (Unlicense/public domain) and its API were recalled from memory; confirm before vendoring. Clipping every candidate pair can be O(F^2) on pathological inputs (a chart collapsed to a point), so cap at 1e6 pairs and fall back to the raster %. Stacked-island tolerances can misclassify near-identical but distinct islands; let the user override per pair.

**Sources:** Shewchuk, 'Adaptive Precision Floating-Point Arithmetic and Fast Robust Geometric Predicates', DCG 1997: https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~quake/robust.html; robust-predicates, pure-JS port of Shewchuk's predicates (V. Agafonkin, Unlicense): https://github.com/mourner/robust-predicates; Unreal forum, percentage-style lightmap overlap warning: https://forums.unrealengine.com/t/error-lightmap-uv-are-overlapping-by-40/663

### F27. Seam quality beyond length: cross-seam texel mismatch, seam chains, visibility-weighted seam length, texel-axis alignment

*Impact* medium · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/core/metrics.js (seam block), optional src/core/bvh.js for AO, ui seam overlay in viewport and uvview`

**What:** Seam length alone does not tell you how visible a seam will be. Visibility depends on four things. (a) Whether the two sides of a seam have matching UV length and orientation, since mismatch shows as a texel-density jump and bilinear discontinuity. (b) Whether the seam hides in a concave or hard crease. (c) Whether the seam is split into many short fragments, which are hard to paint across. (d) Whether it runs along texel rows or columns, which gives fewer stair-step bleed texels.

**Why:** The plan has seamLength3D, seamNorm and seamEdgeCount only. This finding adds metrics that predict visible painting and bake seams, lets the optimizer or presets prefer hidden seams, and gives paper-comparable UV-normalised seam length (PartUV).

**Sketch:** In metrics.js during the seam pass (seams come from topology, as in the plan). For each seam edge e with 3D length l3, the UV endpoints on side 1 (p0,p1) and side 2 (q0,q1) come from the two faces' corners. (1) Texel mismatch: l1 = |p1-p0|*R, l2 = |q1-q0|*R, mismatch_e = |l1-l2|/max(l1,l2). The rotation between sides is phi_e = angle(p1-p0, q1-q0) reduced mod 90 deg to [0,45]. seamMismatch = sum l3*mismatch/sum l3; also report the fraction of seam length with mismatch < 0.02 and phi < 1 deg ('grid-compatible': texels can be matched exactly, which enables later seam-erasure post-processing). (2) Chains: build the seam graph and count seam-degree per vertex. Chain endpoints and junctions are vertices with degree != 2; walk to extract chains. Report chainCount, median chain length / sqrt(sum A3), and shortFragments (chains shorter than 0.02*sqrt(sum A3)). (3) Visibility weight: signed dihedral theta_e (positive = convex). w_e = 0.25 if |theta_e| >= hardAngle (60 deg default; the seam is hidden and also needed for normal bakes), 0.5 if theta_e <= -20 deg (concave crease), otherwise 1. Optional ambient-occlusion factor: at the edge midpoint cast 16 cosine-weighted rays with the mesh BVH (if mesh.js has one; otherwise a uniform-grid ray caster) and multiply w_e by (1 - 0.75*occludedFraction). visibleSeamNorm = sum w_e*l3/sqrt(sum A3). The score finding uses this in place of seamN. (4) Axis alignment: for each seam side, angle of the UV direction to the nearest of 0/90 deg. alignedFraction = seam UV length within 1 deg divided by total seam UV length (ignoring the chart rotation pack chose, so compute after packing). (5) PartUV-compatible seamLengthUV = sum over boundary edges (each side counted) of |UV length| with packed UVs in [0,1]. (6) UI: seam overlay colour by w_e*mismatch, and a legend listing the 10 worst seams with 'Show'. O(E) plus optional AO O(E*16*log F).

**Risks:** The visibility weights (0.25/0.5/1, 60 deg) are heuristic and uncalibrated. The AO pass is the only expensive part, so keep it optional and off by default. The mismatch metric changes after packing rotation, so compute it on final UVs. For stacked or mirrored islands, cross-seam mismatch is intentional.

**Sources:** PartUV: Part-Based UV Unwrapping of 3D Meshes, arXiv 2511.16659 (2025): seam length = sum of chart-boundary edge lengths with UVs normalised to [0,1], median reported: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16659; Liu, Ferguson, Jacobson, 'Seamless: Seam Erasure and Seam-Aware Decoupling of Shape from Mesh Resolution', SIGGRAPH Asia 2017, on bilinear texture discontinuities across seams (from memory): https://cragl.cs.gmu.edu/seamless/; SeamCrafter (arXiv 2509.20725, 2025), which evaluates artist-style seams (fewer, longer, placed where they are less visible): https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20725

### F28. Blender-parity stretch overlay (verified shader formulas) with a calibrated legend

*Impact* medium · *Effort* S · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/core/metrics.js (blenderStretch, weightToRgb), ui/uvview and viewport heat modes 'blender-area' / 'blender-angle' with a calibrated legend`

**What:** Blender's UV editor stretch display is the reference most artists know. The shader was confirmed. Area mode: x = ratio*total_area_ratio, s = (x > 1) ? 1/x : x, weight = 1 - s (flat per face). Angle mode: per corner, the UV corner angle is rebuilt from the two edge directions (with image-aspect correction) using a numerically safe 2*asin(|v1-v2|/2) form; d = |uvAngle/pi - angle3D/pi|; stretch = 1 - d; weight = 1 - stretch^2. The colormap weight_to_rgb, with blend = w/2 + 0.5, runs blue (0..0.25), cyan to green (0.25..0.5), green to yellow (0.5..0.75), yellow to red (0.75..1), and magenta above 1.

**Why:** The plan names blenderStretch but gives no formulas. This finding gives the exact formulas and adds a legend that turns colours back into physical numbers, which neither Blender nor the plan shows. Values then match what users see in Blender, so A/B screenshots are credible.

**Sketch:** metrics.blenderStretch(mesh, uv, aspect = 1). (1) Area: ratio_f = uvArea_f/area3D_f (0 if either < 1.19e-7, Blender's FLT_EPSILON); total_area_ratio = sum area3D / sum uvArea (CPU-side normalisation, from memory); x = ratio_f*total_area_ratio; s = x > 1 ? 1/x : x; areaW[f] = 1 - s. (2) Angle, per corner i of face f: UV directions a = uv_prev - uv_i and b = uv_next - uv_i, each multiplied component-wise by (aspectX, aspectY) and normalised; q = dot(a,b) >= 0; v = q ? a-b : a+b; ang = 2*asin(min(1,|v|/2)); uvAngle = q ? ang : pi - ang. angle3D = the matching 3D corner angle (math.triAngles3). d = |uvAngle - angle3D|/pi; angleW[3F] = 1 - (1-d)^2. Colours are interpolated per corner as in Blender. (3) weightToRgb(w): blend = w/2+0.5; w <= .25: (0, blend*w*4, blend); <= .5: (0, blend, blend*(1-(w-.25)*4)); <= .75: (blend*(w-.5)*4, blend, 0); <= 1: (blend, blend*(1-(w-.75)*4), 0); otherwise (1,0,1). (4) Calibrated legend. Area: w = 0.25 is a 1.33x density ratio, 0.5 is 2x, 0.75 is 4x. Angle: w = 0.25 is d = 1-sqrt(0.75) = 0.134, i.e. 24.1 deg; w = 0.5 is 52.7 deg; w = 0.75 is 90 deg. So Blender's angle view is insensitive below about 5 deg (w about 0.055); show our own angle heatmap (plan cornerAngleErr) when precision matters. (5) Blender-comparable summary readouts: area-weighted mean areaW and angleW. Tests: isometric map gives all weights 0; a face at 2x relative density gives areaW = 0.5; a 90 deg corner mapped to 45 deg gives angleW = 1-(1-0.25)^2 = 0.4375.

**Risks:** The shader is GPL; re-implementing the math is fine, but do not paste the code into the MIT-style project. The CPU-side definitions of ratio and total_area_ratio were recalled from memory; if they are inverted, the only effect is that x changes to 1/x, which gives the same weight by symmetry, so the risk is low. Blender may change these formulas in later versions.

**Sources:** Blender source, overlay_edit_uv_stretching_vert.glsl (main branch, 2018-2022 Blender Authors, GPL-2.0-or-later; formulas re-implemented, no code copied): https://github.com/blender/blender/blob/main/source/blender/draw/engines/overlay/shaders/overlay_edit_uv_stretching_vert.glsl; Blender source, draw extractors extract_mesh_vbo_edituv_stretch_area / _angle (CPU-side ratio and angle attributes; recalled from memory, not re-read)

### F29. Benchmark corpus, standard reporting protocol, and 'grade existing UVs' mode for cross-tool comparison

*Impact* high · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* medium · verified · *Target* `src/io (UV import, charts from UVs), src/core/metrics.js (PartUV-compatible variants), new src/app/benchmark.js (folder runner, aggregates, profiles), docs/BENCHMARK.md, tests/fixtures`

**What:** A reproducible evaluation harness that runs inside the file:// app. Models come from a user-selected folder. Every result, whether our unwrap or UVs already present in the file from Blender, xatlas or RizomUV, is scored by the same metrics code. Outputs use the metric definitions recent papers use, so the project's claims are comparable with published numbers.

**Why:** The plan's benchmark uses only procedural primitives and has no protocol, so it cannot show that the tool beats Blender or xatlas on real assets. This finding adds a published corpus and baseline numbers, paper-compatible metric variants, success-rate and performance-profile reporting, and a user feature the plan lacks: loading any model with existing UVs and getting a grade and issue list without unwrapping. That feature serves users who already have UVs.

**Sketch:** (1) Grade existing UVs: when the IO layer finds TEXCOORD_0 (glTF) or vt (OBJ), keep them as referenceUV (and TEXCOORD_1 as the lightmap UV). Derive charts with union-find over faces sharing a mesh edge whose two endpoints have identical UV indices, or UVs equal within 1e-7 for welded formats. Seams are edges where the UVs differ. Run computeMetrics, checkBakeReadiness and score with no unwrap, and offer 'A/B: reference vs our unwrap'. (2) Folder runner with no fetch: <input type=file webkitdirectory multiple> works under file://. Read each file with FileReader, parse, send to the worker, and run {reference UVs if present, preset unwrap(s)} for each model. Stream rows into an in-memory CSV/JSON download (Blob plus a normal user-initiated save). (3) Row schema: model, F, V, genus/components, method, preset, seed, version, valid (flips=0 and no unintended overlap), sdMean, sdP95, sdMax, withinOptCutsBound (sdMean <= 1.025), stretchL2, stretchEff, packingEff, textureEff, angleMeanDeg, areaLog2Mean, PartUV angular distortion (1 - mean_f |cos angle(dP/du, dP/dv)|, face-count weighted; dP/du and dP/dv are the columns of the inverse Jacobian lifted to 3D), PartUV area distortion (max_c mean_{f in c} max(s_f,1/s_f) with s_f = (A2_f/A3_f)/(A2_c/A3_c)) and the fraction of charts under tau = 1.25, chartCount, seamNorm, seamLengthUV, visibleSeamNorm, mipSafe, score, timings per stage. (4) Aggregates: success rate; median and p90 of each metric; geometric mean of time ratios against a baseline; Dolan-More performance profiles, where for metric m the ratio r_{p,s} = m_{p,s}/min_s m_{p,s} and rho_s(tau) = |{p : r_{p,s} <= tau}|/|P|, plotted for tau in [1,4] on a log axis with Chart.js; a Pareto scatter of sdMean against seamNorm. (5) Corpus guidance in docs/BENCHMARK.md: user downloads Thingi10K (filter manifold, 1 component, 5k-100k faces, about 110 models), PartObjaverseTiny (200), the ABC first chunk (100), plus the built-in procedural fixtures (sphere, torus knot, bevelled cube, capped cylinder, open hemisphere, high-genus grid) for CI in node --test. Reference baselines are produced once by the user with Blender Smart UV Project (angle 66 deg, margin 0.001) and xatlas defaults, exported as glTF, and graded through the same path. (6) Determinism: fixed seeds, searchMs=0, and settings plus git hash recorded in each row.

**Risks:** Datasets cannot be bundled (size and licences: Objaverse items have mixed CC licences), so the user must download them. The webkitdirectory attribute is non-standard but supported in Chromium, Firefox and Safari. PartUV's metric definitions are paraphrased from the paper's text; check the exact averaging (face-count vs area) against their code before claiming parity. Large models (>500k faces) in a Blob worker may exhaust memory, so cap and skip with a note. The Thingi10K filter numbers come from a search summary of a 2025 paper, not re-read.

**Sources:** PartUV (arXiv 2511.16659, 2025), evaluation protocol: Common Shapes (24), PartObjaverseTiny (200), ABC (100), Trellis (114); metrics: chart count mean/median, seam length (UV normalised to [0,1]), angular distortion = 1 - mean cosine between tangent and bitangent, area distortion = max over charts of mean_f max(s_f, 1/s_f), tau = 1.25; baselines Blender Smart UV 1360.3 charts, xatlas 974.8, PartUV 48.6 on Common Shapes: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16659; Thingi10K (Zhou and Jacobson 2016), used filtered to 110 manifold single-component medium-size shapes in recent neural parameterisation work: https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.04797; FlexPara (arXiv 2504.19210, 2025), recent parameterisation evaluation on Thingi10K subsets: https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19210; Dolan and More, 'Benchmarking optimization software with performance profiles', Math. Programming 2002 (from memory): https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/0102001

## Seam placement and chart segmentation beyond xatlas region growing (feature-, visibility-, concavity- and loop-aware seams; developable, part-based and pelt cut graphs; distortion-bounded cut editing), limited to what plain JS can do with no build step for meshes up to about 100k faces

### F30. Seamster-style visibility field computed in pure JS (multi-view orthographic z-buffer) as a per-edge seam weight

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `new src/core/visibility.js; consumed by segmentation.js, chart.js (cutToDisk weights), metrics.js (visibleSeamLength), engine.js option segmentation.visibility {enabled, views, domain}`

**What:** Seamster renders the mesh orthographically from viewpoints spread evenly over the sphere, with each face drawn in an ID colour. V(f) is the fraction of renders in which face f shows up in the framebuffer. It is near 0 for occluded faces and around 0.5 for prominent ones. Edge visibility V(e) is the average of the two adjacent faces. The cut weight of an interior edge is W(e)=V(e)*|e|, and boundary edges get a small epsilon. You can restrict the view set to part of the sphere, e.g. views from above for four-legged animals, so seams go to the belly and under the chin.

**Why:** The v4 plan has no notion of how visible a seam is. cutToDisk uses plain edge lengths, and the segmentation cost only looks at normals. With visibility weights, cutToDisk paths, pelt trees, boundary refinement and genus-reducing loops all move into hidden areas: armpits, the insides of handles and tunnels, undersides. Seamster reports seams at 1.8-4.3% of total edge length with Sander stretch 1.017-1.095 on 0.7k-28k face models. It also enables a new metric, visibleSeamLength = sum over seam edges of len*V, plus a heat overlay. Neither v3 nor v4 can do this.

**Sketch:** New module src/core/visibility.js: computeVisibility(mesh, {views=64, res=0 (auto), domain:'sphere'|'upperHemisphere'|'custom', up=[0,1,0], dirs}) -> {faceVis: Float32Array(F), vertVis: Float32Array(V), edgeVis: Float32Array(E)}. (1) Directions: Fibonacci sphere with d_k=(sqrt(1-z^2)cos(k*2.39996), sqrt(1-z^2)sin(k*2.39996), z) and z=1-(2k+1)/K. For 'upperHemisphere' drop directions with d.up < -0.2 (Seamster restricts to views from above). (2) For each d build an orthonormal basis (a,b) perpendicular to d. Project every welded vertex to (p.a, p.b, depth=-p.d), map it to pixels over the projected bbox at resolution R, and use R=512 if F>20k, otherwise 256. (3) Rasterise depth only into a Float32Array(R*R) initialised to +Inf, using an edge-function loop over each triangle's pixel bbox; tiny triangles cost almost nothing. (4) Do NOT rely on face-ID coverage, because subpixel triangles vanish at R=256 with 100k faces. Point-test instead: a face centroid (or vertex) counts as visible from d if n.d>0 (front-facing) and depth <= zbuf[px] + bias. Take zbuf as the max over a 3x3 neighbourhood, with bias = 1.5*pixelWorldSize + 1e-4*diag. Increment count[f]. (5) faceVis=count/K_front, where K_front is the number of directions actually used. edgeVis = mean of the two adjacent faces (Seamster). vertVis = area-weighted mean of incident faces. Cost is K*(F triangle setups + (V+F) point tests), about 6.4M setups + 10M lookups for 100k faces/64 views, roughly 1-3 s in a worker. Report progress('visibility'). Seam weight used everywhere: W(e) = |e| * (edgeVis[e] + v0) with floor v0=0.02 so hidden seams aren't free, and W=epsilon*|e| on existing mesh boundary edges. Cache the result per mesh because it depends only on geometry and the view domain. Optional fast path: the UI can render the ID/depth passes with three.js WebGL and post the Float32Array to the worker, but the pure-JS path stays the reference for node tests. Tests: a closed box has all faces visible at about 0.5; the inner faces of a nested inner box have vis 0; the inside of a torus tube hole has lower vis than the outer rim.

**Risks:** Orthographic views from a uniform sphere assume every view direction is equally likely. Characters need the hemisphere or custom domain, which is a UI choice. Point-test bias can mark faces as visible in thin slots; a 3x3 max filter plus the front-face test keeps false positives low. Non-watertight or double-sided scans make interior faces look visible, so treat n.d<=0 as hidden only for closed meshes. Seamster itself used GPU rendering; the CPU timings above are my estimate, not measured.

**Sources:** Sheffer & Hart, 'Seamster: Inconspicuous Low-Distortion Texture Seam Layout', IEEE Visualization 2002 - https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~sheffa/papers/VIS02.pdf (full text read); Nooruddin & Turk, 'Interior/Exterior Classification of Polygonal Models', IEEE Vis 2000 (cited by Seamster as the origin of the method)

### F31. Seamster pelt mode: curvature/cone terminals + visibility-weighted Steiner tree + visibility-ordered genus reduction

*Impact* high · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* medium · verified · *Target* `new src/core/cutgraph.js (terminals, steinerTree, genusReduce); engine.js mode 'pelt'; uses solvers.pcg and visibility.js`

**What:** This is a low-chart-count 'pelt' unwrap for organic meshes, the opposite style to xatlas's many small charts. (1) Estimate where flattening will concentrate distortion: Seamster uses multi-scale angle deficit, BFF iteratively places cones using a conformal scale factor. (2) Select those vertices as terminals until the remaining distortion falls below a user fraction. (3) Connect all terminals and existing boundary loops with an approximate minimum Steiner tree on the edge graph, weighted by visibility*length. (4) On higher-genus surfaces, first cut the least visible genus-reducing loops. The result is one disk per component, with seams that run into ears, fingers and horns through hidden paths.

**Why:** The v4 plan only makes charts by normal-cone growth, then cutToDisk only joins boundary loops. It has no mode that deliberately cuts into high-curvature extremities, so a character ends up with dozens of charts instead of one or a few low-stretch pelts. Seamster reports L2 stretch of 1.017-1.095 with seams of 1.8-4.3% of total edge length, and 1.055 on the hand where Geometry Images got 1.4. Visibility-guided genus reduction also speeds up the Erickson/Har-Peled loop search by avoiding testing every loop. This gives a new 'Pelt (organic)' preset.

**Sketch:** New src/core/cutgraph.js. A) Distortion per vertex, two options. (i) Seamster: K_v=2pi-sum of corner angles. Region distortion D'_i(n)=(2pi-sum_j tau_j)/2pi on the submesh M_i of triangles within vertex distance R=i*maxIncidentEdgeLen(n), where tau_j are the angles at n of the triangles formed by n and the boundary edges of M_i, and D_r(n)=max over 0<=i<=r of D'_i (r=5). Cheap JS approximation: use Gauss-Bonnet, D'_i ≈ sum of K_v over the BFS ball of radius R (capped at 300 vertices), divided by 2pi. Compute it only for vertices with K_v > 0 among the top 10%. Apply Seamster's overlap filter: if n is inside M_i(m) and D_r(n) > D_r(m), set D_r(m)=D_0(m); if they are about equal, keep the larger D_0 and set the other to D_0. Interior boundary vertices with angle sum < 2pi get 0. (ii) CETM cones (BFF): the first cone is the vertex with max K_v (for chi>0). Then repeat: solve L_nn u_n = -K_n with the cotan Laplacian restricted to non-cone vertices (Dirichlet u=0 at cones), using the existing pcg with Jacobi preconditioning and warm start, and add the vertex with max |u| as a cone. Stop when max|u| < 0.5*ln(tauArea), e.g. tauArea=1.25 gives 0.112, or at maxCones=32. B) Terminals: sort by D_r (or D_r/max(V(n),0.02) to favour hidden vertices, per Seamster) and add them until the removed distortion is >= D(M)-D, where D = keepFraction*D(M) (default 0.67, since Seamster cut about a third); with CETM, every cone is a terminal. Add all vertices of existing boundary loops, whose edges weigh epsilon. C) Steiner tree (Mehlhorn-style, equivalent to Seamster's front merging): run one multi-source Dijkstra from all terminals with weights W(e)=|e|*(V(e)+0.02) (or |e| without a visibility field), storing dist, src and predEdge per vertex. For every edge (a,b) with src[a]!=src[b], the bridge cost is dist[a]+W(ab)+dist[b]; keep the cheapest bridge per source pair in a Map. Run Kruskal over the bridges with union-find over terminals. Mark tree edges cut by backtracking preds from a and b. This is O(E log V), about 100 ms at 100k faces. D) Genus reduction before step C, when chi indicates genus g>0: visit vertices in increasing visibility, at most 300 tries. From v, run Dijkstra to build a shortest-path tree. For each non-tree edge (a,b) whose root branches (child of v) differ, form the loop path(a)+ab+path(b) and take the cheapest. Accept it if cutting it keeps the face set connected (flood fill not crossing cuts) and recomputed chi rises by 2 (genus -1). Repeat until g=0. Fall back to tree-cotree (Eppstein 2003) if the budget runs out. E) Then run cutToDisk (the plan's version, now weighted), LSCM → SLIM on the single chart, and optionally the distortion-driven augmentation finding. Engine: segmentation.mode='pelt' with opts {distortionKeep:0.67, method:'seamster'|'cones', useVisibility:true, maxCones:32}.

**Risks:** One large chart per component makes SLIM the bottleneck on 100k faces; progressive or multi-resolution SLIM is required. A saddle vertex that terminates a seam can cause overlaps (Seamster notes this), so exclude negative D_r from terminals. The ball-sum approximation of Seamster's D'_r is my simplification, not the paper's exact construction. Genus reduction by loop testing is quadratic in the worst case, hence the try budget. The BFF default cone count and stopping threshold were not visible in the source summary; the |u| threshold here is my choice.

**Sources:** Sheffer & Hart 2002, Seamster - https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~sheffa/papers/VIS02.pdf (read: region distortion Eq.1-2, terminal selection Alg.1, Steiner tree, genus reduction, Table 1); Boundary First Flattening cone placement source (CETM strategy) - https://github.com/GeometryCollective/boundary-first-flattening/blob/master/src/project/ConePlacement.cpp; Sawhney & Crane, 'Boundary First Flattening', ACM TOG 2017/2018 - https://arxiv.org/abs/1704.06873; Gu, Gortler, Hoppe, 'Geometry Images', SIGGRAPH 2002 - https://hhoppe.com/proj/gim/

### F32. D-Charts developable cone proxies in the Lloyd chart growth (quasi-developable segmentation)

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/core/segmentation.js (proxy abstraction: fitProxy, faceError, growCost), src/core/math.js (sym3 eigen); presets 'Hard surface / developable'`

**What:** This replaces the plane proxy (chart normal N_c plus an angle limit), as used by xatlas and VSA, with a cone proxy <N, theta>. A triangle fits if its normal keeps a constant angle to the axis. Planes (theta=0), cylinders (theta=90 degrees) and cones are all developable and flatten almost isometrically. The fitting error is F(C,t)=(N.n_t - cos theta)^2. The growth cost is Cost=F^alpha * Compact^beta * Straight^gamma with alpha=1, beta=0.7, gamma=0.5. Compact=pi*D(seed,t)^2/A_C and Straight=l_outer/l_inner, and faces are accepted only if F<Fmax=0.2. Lloyd rounds re-fit the proxy and re-seed at the most central of the 10 best-fitting faces. The algorithm stops when fewer than 5% of faces change chart. Holes are filled and adjacent charts merged when their shared band fits a cylinder.

**Why:** The plan's angleDeg=50 normal-cone growth has to split a cylinder or cone (pipes, limbs, bottles, fandisk) into about 360/50 strips. D-Charts keeps them as single charts with near-zero distortion. Paper Table 1: fandisk (9.9k faces) gives 4 charts at L2 stretch 1.000 vs Iso-charts 4 charts at 1.021; bunny (69k) gives 10 charts at L2 1.004 vs Iso-charts 16 at 1.023 and MCGIM 15 at 1.014. The cost is about 10-15% longer boundaries. That means fewer charts and less SLIM work for the same or lower distortion.

**Sketch:** Add segmentation opts.proxy = 'plane' | 'cone' (default 'cone' for the 'atlas-dev' preset). Proxy fit (closed form, replacing the paper's Newton solve): with area-weighted mean normal nbar = sum A_t n_t / sum A_t, the optimum of min sum A_t (N.n_t - c)^2 s.t. |N|=1 with c free is c = N.nbar, and N is the eigenvector of the smallest eigenvalue of M = sum A_t (n_t - nbar)(n_t - nbar)^T, a 3x3 symmetric matrix (cyclic Jacobi, 10 sweeps); theta = acos(clamp(c,-1,1)). Degenerate plane: if lambda_mid(M) < 1e-4*sum A_t, all normals are nearly equal, so set N=nbar/|nbar| and theta=0; otherwise an arbitrary axis would admit unrelated cones. Growth: use the existing global MinHeap with generation stamps. When popping (cost, t, C), recompute the cost if the generation is stale. Accept only if t is unassigned and F(C,t) < Fmax (0.2 organic; about 0.02 mechanical). Compute Compact from D = dual-graph path length from the seed accumulated during growth, dist[t] = dist[parent] + |c_t - c_parent|, and A_C the current chart area. Straight = sum of t's edge lengths not shared with C divided by sum of those shared. My modification: use (F+1e-3) so compactness still acts on perfect developables, since the product would otherwise be 0. Seeds: farthest-point sampling on dual Dijkstra distance for the initial count (use the plan's chart count estimate, or start at 8 and let hole filling add charts). Seed proxy: try the 1-ring fans of the seed's three vertices and keep the proxy with the smallest F for the seed. Re-seed: among the k=10 faces with the smallest F in C, pick the one closest to C's area centroid. Iterate until reassigned < 5% of F, or maxIter=10 to fit the 100k/2 s budget. Holes: take connected components of unassigned faces. If area < 1% of the neighbouring chart area, grow neighbours with Fmax ignored; otherwise create a new chart there, fit, and regrow, recursively. Merge pass: for adjacent charts A,B, band B = faces within 2 rings of the shared boundary. Fit a cylinder with theta=pi/2 (N = smallest eigenvector of sum A_t n_t n_t^T). Merge if (1/A_B) sum A_t (N.n_t)^2 < eta, with eta=1e-2 organic and 1e-5 mechanical. Re-fit after each merge and process pairs in increasing error. Cylinders produce annuli, so the plan's cutToDisk is mandatory afterwards; darts (partial cuts toward high-F regions) come from the distortion-driven augmentation finding. Complexity: O(F log F) per Lloyd round plus O(#charts) 3x3 eigenproblems. Tests: a closed cylinder side yields 1 chart with theta≈90 degrees (plus cap charts); a cone yields 1 chart; a sphere still segments, with beta=0.7 keeping charts round.

**Risks:** Doubly curved regions like the nose tip repel charts, and the paper notes many spawned charts, which is why hole filling beats respawning. Fmax is on a squared cosine difference and is not intuitive, so expose it as an angle tolerance: Fmax=(1-cos(tol))^2 approximately. The paper's runtimes (91-482 s) come from 2005 Graphite with Newton fits and per-chart queues, so our closed form should be much faster, but that is unmeasured. The +1e-3 floor and the hole-size threshold are my choices; the paper does not specify them.

**Sources:** Julius, Kraevoy, Sheffer, 'D-Charts: Quasi-Developable Mesh Segmentation', Eurographics/CGF 2005 - https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~sheffa/papers/EG05.pdf (full text read: Eq.1-3, alpha/beta/gamma, Fmax, seeds k=10, 5% termination, hole filling, straightening, merge eta, Table 1); Cohen-Steiner, Alliez, Desbrun, 'Variational Shape Approximation', SIGGRAPH 2004 - https://doi.org/10.1145/1015706.1015817 (not re-read)

### F33. Unified seam-cost field (concavity minima rule + features + visibility + user paint) and fuzzy-band boundary refinement

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/core/seamcost.js; src/core/segmentation.js (growth cost term + refineBoundaries post-pass); chart.js cutToDisk weights; src/ui/seam-tool.js (paint brush, shortest-path mode); engine.js ops setSeamPaint`

**What:** A single Float32Array seamCost[E] tells every seam-producing stage how bad it is to put a seam on each edge: segmentation growth, boundary refinement, cutToDisk paths, pelt trees, and the manual shortest-path seam tool. It combines length, visibility, concavity (the minima rule: people read part boundaries at concave creases, and concave creases are also occluded), feature flags, and a user-painted preference (AutoCuts-style brushing). After segmentation, the jagged boundary between each pair of charts is replaced by the cheapest path inside a 'fuzzy' band where faces fit both charts, as in D-Charts boundary straightening. Closed-loop boundaries use a min-cut, as in Katz-Tal.

**Why:** The plan's only boundary-shaping terms are xatlas's local roundness and straightness weights plus normalSeam = 1-dot(n_f,n_g), which treats convex and concave creases the same. Refinement removes stair-step boundaries (fewer seam edges, less seam length, cleaner islands to pack) and slides seams into valleys and hidden creases. The painting brush adds an interactive 'seam here / never here' control that neither v3 nor v4 has; AutoCuts showed it is a key UX lever.

**Sketch:** src/core/seamcost.js: buildSeamCost(mesh, {vis, featureFlags, paint, weights}). For edge e with faces f,g, dihedral theta_e=acos(clamp(n_f.n_g)), and concave(e)=1 if (c_g - c_f).n_f > 1e-6*diag (the opposite vertex of g lies above f's plane), else 0. Then cost(e) = |e| * (vis ? (V(e)+0.02)/0.5 : 1) * exp(-kc*concave(e)*theta_e) * (1 + kx*(1-concave(e))*theta_e/pi) * featureFactor(e) * paint(e). Defaults: kc=1.5 per radian, so a 90° concave crease costs about 0.1x; kx=1.0 penalises cutting across convex bulges mildly; featureFactor=0.15 for SHARP/HARD_NORMAL/MATERIAL/UV_SEAM, Infinity for POLY_INTERNAL; paint in [0.05,20] from the brush, default 1. Katz-Tal equivalent for min-cut capacity: angDist = eta*(1-cos theta) with eta=1 concave and 0.2 convex, cap = 1/(1+angDist/mean(angDist)), times |e|. In segmentation growth, replace w.normalSeam*(1-dot(n_f,n_g)) with w.seam*(1 - cost(e)/(|e|*maxFactor)), so crossing a cheap seam edge to annex a face is discouraged. Refinement, refineBoundaries(mesh, faceChart, proxies, seamCost, {rings:3}): (1) Collect boundary segments between chart pairs (A,B), i.e. maximal chains of shared edges whose endpoints are vertices with ≥3 incident charts or on the mesh boundary. (2) Band = faces of A∪B within 3 dual rings of the segment that are acceptable to both proxies (angle/F threshold of the other chart), as in D-Charts virtual mutual growth. (3) Open segment with endpoints p,q: Dijkstra from p to q over vertex-edge graph edges whose incident faces are both in the band, or which lie on the original segment, weighted by cost(e). The new path splits the band; flood-fill band faces from the A-side rim without crossing the path, label them A, the rest B. (4) Closed loop (B enclosed by A): s-t min-cut on the band dual graph with capacity cost(e). The source is A's faces adjacent to the band outside, the sink is B's; Edmonds-Karp/Dinic is fine for bands under 5k faces. (5) Reject the change if either chart becomes disconnected or empty, or chart error rises above threshold; otherwise commit. Run 2 passes. Complexity is O(sum of band*log band), much less than F log F. UI: a paint brush in the 3D view writes per-vertex paint, and edge paint = geometric mean of its endpoints. The worker op setSeamPaint(Float32Array V) is stored like cut flags and saved in snapshots and presets.

**Risks:** The Katz-Tal eta values (1 concave, 0.1-0.2 convex) and the AutoCuts brush mechanism are from memory, not re-read this session. Only the D-Charts straightening procedure was read. Refinement can undo the xatlas roundness objective, so cap the band width. Min-cut on noisy scans can produce tiny slivers; post-merge charts below minChartFaces. All constants except D-Charts' are proposals that need benchmark tuning.

**Sources:** Julius, Kraevoy, Sheffer 2005, D-Charts, Sec. 3.3 'Straightening boundaries' - https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~sheffa/papers/EG05.pdf (read); Katz & Tal, 'Hierarchical Mesh Decomposition using Fuzzy Clustering and Cuts', SIGGRAPH 2003 - https://doi.org/10.1145/882262.882369 (from memory, not re-read); Poranne, Tarini, Huber, Panozzo, Sorkine-Hornung, 'Autocuts: Simultaneous Distortion and Cut Optimization for UV Mapping', SIGGRAPH Asia 2017 - https://doi.org/10.1145/3130800.3130845 (brush-guided cut preference; from memory)

### F34. Seed seams from import attributes and hysteresis feature chains (hard normals, materials, UV seams, smoothing groups, n-gon diagonals)

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/io/obj.js, src/io/gltf.js (attribute retention); src/core/mesh.js (computeEdgeFlags, featureChains); segmentation.js (polygon super-faces, feature-distance seeding); engine.js opts.seams.respect`

**What:** The current buildMesh welds by position only, so it throws away the discontinuity information artists put in files. That information includes split vertex normals (hard edges), usemtl or glTF primitive material boundaries, existing vt/UV discontinuities (artist seams), OBJ smoothing groups, and polygon structure (the diagonals of triangulated quads and n-gons should never be seams). As in xatlas, where normalSeam and textureSeam are attribute-based, these become per-edge flags. A Canny-style hysteresis on dihedral angle then turns noisy sharp edges into continuous feature curves.

**Why:** The plan's normalSeam=1-dot(n_f,n_g) is a dihedral proxy and cannot tell an intentionally hard edge from a smooth-shaded 50° bevel. Its 'seamsFromAngle(deg)' is a single threshold that gives broken, noisy seam fragments. Hard-normal edges must also be UV splits for correct normal-map baking, and a seam on an existing shading or material discontinuity is invisible. Keeping polygon diagonals uncuttable removes zigzag seams through quads, a common complaint about automatic unwrappers on game assets.

**Sketch:** IO (parsers in src/io/*): keep per-corner attribute indices before welding: cornerNormalId, cornerUvId, faceMaterial (Int32), faceSmoothGroup (Int32, 0=off), facePolyId (Int32; n-gons fan-triangulated share one id). glTF: take normals and UVs from accessors, where vertices are already split. mesh.js computeEdgeFlags(mesh, attrs) -> Uint8Array(E) with bits 1 SHARP, 2 HARD_NORMAL, 4 MATERIAL, 8 UV_SEAM, 16 POLY_INTERNAL, 32 NONMANIFOLD, 64 BOUNDARY. For edge (a,b) with faces f,g, find the corners of f and g at welded a and b. HARD_NORMAL if at either endpoint angle(normal_f, normal_g) > 1°. UV_SEAM if |uv_f - uv_g| > 1e-6 at either endpoint. MATERIAL if the materials differ. Smoothing: HARD if sg_f==0 or sg_f!=sg_g. POLY_INTERNAL if polyId_f==polyId_g. Feature chains (the SHARP bit): theta_e = dihedral; strong if theta_e >= hi (default 60°, 'mechanical' preset 30°), weak if >= lo = 0.5*hi. Start from strong edges and extend each chain end through vertex v, picking among weak incident edges the one maximising theta_e*cos(turn) with turn = angle between chain tangent and candidate direction; stop if turn > 35° or no weak edge. Drop chains whose total length is < 1.5% of bbox diag and that touch no other chain (noise). Optionally close gaps of ≤ 2 edges between chain endpoints with a shortest path. This follows the spirit of LSCM 2002's feature growing with look-ahead and minimum feature length; its exact SOD criterion and parameters were not verified. Use: (a) engine opts.seams.respect = {uvSeams:false, hardNormals:true, materials:true} copies the chosen bits into the hard cut array (the plan's single cut mechanism). (b) Soft use: seamcost featureFactor=0.15 on flagged edges, as in xatlas normalSeamWeight=4 / textureSeamWeight=0.5 in the growth cost. (c) POLY_INTERNAL edges: growth treats a polygon as one super-face (always add all its triangles together), and all path searches treat those edges as weight Infinity. (d) Chart seeding: xatlas and plane-proxy seeds are placed at local maxima of dual geodesic distance to feature edges (multi-source Dijkstra from feature edges), so charts grow outward and meet at features, as in LSCM 2002. Complexity O(E). Tests: a cube OBJ with per-face normals gives 12 HARD edges, and seams = those edges yields 6 charts; a quad plane OBJ gives no seam on diagonals; a two-material sphere gives charts that never straddle the material boundary.

**Risks:** Welding currently discards corner attributes, so the IO/mesh contract (ARCHITECTURE sec. 4.3) must change: add optional attrs to buildMesh. Some exports (STL, many scans) have no attributes, so the dihedral hysteresis is the only signal there. Respecting artist UV seams by default may lock in bad UVs, so default it off. xatlas weight values and LSCM 2002 feature-detection details are from memory.

**Sources:** xatlas ChartOptions (normalSeamWeight, textureSeamWeight, normalDeviationWeight, roundnessWeight, straightnessWeight, maxCost) - https://github.com/jpcy/xatlas (xatlas.h; from memory); Lévy, Petitjean, Ray, Maillot, 'Least Squares Conformal Maps for Automatic Texture Atlas Generation', SIGGRAPH 2002 (feature detection + chart growth away from features) - https://doi.org/10.1145/566654.566590 (from memory); Blender manual, UV unwrapping tools (Mark Seams from sharp / Seams from Islands) - https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/meshes/uv/unwrapping/seams.html

### F35. Edge-loop-following seam routing (quad recovery, loop graph, turn-penalised shortest paths)

*Impact* medium · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/core/loops.js; chart.js (cutToDisk path search), cutgraph.js, segmentation.js (refineBoundaries & straightness), src/ui/seam-tool.js (loop select, shortest path)`

**What:** Artists cut along edge loops: chains of edges that pass straight through regular valence-4 vertices of a quad-dominant mesh. On triangulated input the quads are recovered first, either from polygon ids or by greedily pairing triangles. Loop continuation is then defined at each regular vertex, and every seam path search runs on a (vertex, incoming edge) state graph. Continuing along the loop is free and turning costs extra. Seams therefore follow loops instead of zigzagging across the diagonal lattice.

**Why:** The plan's cutToDisk and manual seam tool use plain Dijkstra on edge lengths, which on quad-based assets produces staircase seams through diagonals. Those look bad, are hard to edit, and waste texels along jagged island borders. Loop routing gives straighter island borders (better packing efficiency) and seams that match what an artist would draw. It also enables Alt-click whole-loop seam selection in the seam tool, which the plan lacks.

**Sketch:** src/core/loops.js. (1) Quad recovery. If facePolyId exists, a 4-triangle... more precisely a 2-triangle polygon is a quad. Otherwise, for each interior edge with faces f,g compute the candidate quad's corner angle error q = max over its 4 corners of |angle-90°| and dihedral(f,g). Accept pairs sorted by q if q < 40° and dihedral < 40° (Blender Tris-to-Quads defaults of face/shape thresholds of 40°, from memory), each triangle used once. Mark the paired edge DIAGONAL (POLY_INTERNAL). (2) Quad edge graph: drop DIAGONAL edges; for each vertex, sort its remaining incident edges cyclically using the face fan. At a vertex of quad-valence 4 with no boundary, opposite(e) = the edge two steps along the cycle. Loop ids come from union-find over (e, opposite(e) at each regular endpoint). Loops end at irregular vertices, triangles and boundaries. (3) Routing: Dijkstra over directed states s=(v, e_in), about 2E states (300k for 100k faces). Transition to e_out incident to v, e_out != e_in, not DIAGONAL: cost = seamCost(e_out) + lambdaTurn*meanEdgeLen*(e_out==opposite_v(e_in) ? 0 : (irregular(v) ? 0.25 : 1)). lambdaTurn defaults to 0.6. Source states are (v0, none) with 0 turn cost, the target is any state at v1 or any vertex of the target set. Use typed-array binary heap keys. (4) Integration: cutToDisk loop-to-loop paths, the pelt Steiner tree (a multi-source version of the same state graph), refineBoundaries open-segment paths, and seam tool Ctrl+click (path from last picked vertex) plus Alt+click (whole loop by walking opposite() both ways until irregular or boundary). (5) Segmentation straightness bonus: in xatlas-style growth, when a boundary edge's loop continuation is also on the chart boundary, discount the straightness term by 50%, so boundaries snap to loops. Tests: a 20x20 quad grid triangulated with alternating diagonals; the path between opposite corners of a row must be the straight loop (turns=0), and all-diagonal paths are rejected.

**Risks:** Pure triangle scans have no loops; the turn penalty then does no harm but brings no gain, so detect a quad-pairing ratio below 60% and skip. Greedy tri-pairing can pick the wrong diagonal on rhombic grids, so weight by coplanarity first. The state-graph Dijkstra doubles memory versus vertex Dijkstra. Blender threshold defaults are from memory.

**Sources:** Blender manual, Select Edge Loops / Select Shortest Path / Triangles to Quads - https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/meshes/selecting/loops.html (from memory); Sheffer & Hart 2002, Seamster (shortest-path seam trees that these routes plug into) - https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~sheffa/papers/VIS02.pdf

### F36. PartUV-style top-down recursive chart search with distortion-validated merging (geometric hierarchy instead of a learned PartField)

*Impact* high · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* medium · verified · *Target* `new src/core/hierarchy.js (HFC tree, normalHeuristic, mergeHeuristic, recursive search); segmentation.js mode 'parts'; engine.js (evaluation budget, optional worker pool); metrics.js (PartUV area-stretch metric for comparison)`

**What:** PartUV takes a binary part tree (from the PartField network) and searches it top-down with a user distortion threshold tau and a chart budget B. At each node, a fast normal heuristic (agglomerative clustering of face normals giving 1-10 candidate chart counts) proposes decompositions, and each candidate is flattened and tested. If none passes, it recurses into the children. If one passes, it runs a merge heuristic: 6 labels from the part's OBB faces, connected components, then iteratively merging the smallest component into the neighbour with the longest shared edge when distortion stays <= tau with no overlap. It then tightens the budget to B'=min(B, best-1) and checks whether the children can do better. Distortion is the max over charts of the per-chart mean of max(s_f, 1/s_f), where s_f is the normalised 2D/3D area ratio; tau defaults to 1.25.

**Why:** xatlas-style growth optimises a local cost and never checks whether a chart actually flattens well. It over-segments noisy or AI-generated meshes badly. PartUV Table 1 averages: Common Shapes 48.6 charts vs xatlas 974.8 vs Blender 1360.3. Trellis: 568.7 vs 1541.6 (xatlas) vs 3352.9 (Blender), seam length 58.8 vs 94.5 (Blender), area distortion 1.373 vs 2.357 (xatlas), with similar angular distortion. Distortion-validated merging gives a hard quality guarantee per chart, which the plan's heuristic weights cannot.

**Sketch:** src/core/hierarchy.js + segmentation mode 'parts'. (1) Tree without learning: hierarchical face clustering on the dual graph. Start with each face (or polygon super-face) as a cluster and put every dual edge (A,B) in a MinHeap with cost = fitErr(A∪B) + wShape*(perim^2/(4pi*area) - 1) + wConc*concaveShare(A,B). fitErr is the D-Charts cone error, area-normalised sum A_t(N.n_t - cos theta)^2, using the 3x3 closed-form fit on accumulated moments sum A, sum A n, sum A n n^T so merges are O(1). concaveShare is the length fraction of the shared boundary with concave dihedral > 20° times the mean angle, which makes concave part boundaries contract last. Pop, union-find merge, push updated neighbour costs with lazy generation checks. This records a binary tree in O(F log F); wShape=0.05, wConc=0.5 as starting values. (2) search(node, tau, B), per Algorithm 1: cands = normalHeuristic(node) runs average-linkage agglomerative clustering on the normals of the node's faces to k=1..10 clusters, then splits into connected components. For each cand, flatten every chart (LSCM, plus 5 SLIM iterations only if LSCM flips) and compute dist = max over charts of mean over faces of max(s,1/s), with s_f = (A2D_f/sum A2D)/(A3D_f/sum A3D). Valid if dist <= tau, no flips, and the exact boundary self-intersection test passes. If none are valid: return search(left) ∪ search(right). Else best = the valid candidate with fewest charts, improved by mergeHeuristic: OBB via PCA of the face centroids, label = argmax over ±axes of n_t.axis, components, then repeatedly take the smallest component and try merging it with its neighbours in decreasing shared-length order, accepting the first with dist <= tau and no overlap. Then B' = min(B, best.count-1); if B' >= 2, childRes = search(left, tau, B'-1) ∪ search(right, tau, ...), and take it if its count < best.count. Return best. (3) Speed: PartUV uses ABF++ (5 iterations) on GPU-simplified surrogate meshes. In JS, for candidate testing, run LSCM with PCG tolerance 1e-4 and maxIter 200, warm-started from the parent's UV, on charts decimated to at most 2k faces by edge collapse (optional) or accept full resolution under 5k faces. Re-flatten only the final charts fully. Cache chart→dist by a sorted face-id hash. Optional: a pool of navigator.hardwareConcurrency Blob workers for candidate evaluation. (4) Engine opts: {tau:1.25, budget:Infinity, maxEvaluations:400}; report progress per node.

**Risks:** The semantic quality comes from PartField, a neural network that cannot run here without weights and a heavy runtime, so the geometric HFC tree will give less 'part-aligned' charts on semantic parts with no concavity cues. PartUV ran 10-40 s per shape on a 96-core CPU with an H100, so a single JS worker needs strict evaluation budgets. Candidate flattening dominates. HFC cost weights are untuned proposals, and the Garland et al. details are from memory.

**Sources:** Wang et al., 'PartUV: Part-Based UV Unwrapping of 3D Meshes', SIGGRAPH Asia 2025 - https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16659 (HTML read: Algorithm 1, heuristics, tau=1.25, ABF++ 5 iters, Table 1); PartUV code - https://github.com/EricWang12/PartUV; Garland, Willmott, Heckbert, 'Hierarchical Face Clustering on Polygonal Surfaces', I3D 2001 (dual-edge contraction hierarchy; from memory)

### F37. Distortion-bounded cut augmentation and seam zipping (Geometry-Images/D-Charts darts + OptCuts-lite topology merge)

*Impact* medium · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* medium · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/core/topology-opt.js; chart.js (splitAlongPath / mergeEdge on Local with UV duplication); optimize.js (warm-started SLIM, local stencil solve); engine.js`

**What:** This is a per-chart topology loop that turns a distortion target into a guarantee with minimal seams. Augment step: while the chart's symmetric Dirichlet (SD) is above the bound, cut a cheapest path from the most distorted interior vertex to the current boundary (a 'dart', as in Geometry Images and D-Charts), then continue optimising from the current UVs. Zip step: an OptCuts-style merge re-glues seam edges, starting with the most visible and longest, when the chart still meets the bound. OptCuts minimises seam length subject to E_SD <= b_d by alternating geometry and topology steps with an adaptively weighted objective. Its code takes bounds in (4, inf) (4 = isometry in its SD normalisation), e.g. 4.1, and lambda starts at 0.999 (fixed lambda 0.025 'works generally well').

**Why:** The plan only optimises geometry (SLIM/ARAP) on a fixed topology. Its 'SD < 1.2' is a test expectation, not a guarantee, and any chart that cannot reach it stays distorted. Augmentation fixes the worst charts: bulbous extremities and chart centres on doubly curved areas. Zipping removes seams that segmentation made unnecessarily, which lowers seam length and chart count. Full OptCuts only succeeded on 9 of 24 Common Shapes in PartUV's benchmark and is slow; this per-chart 'lite' version keeps the benefit with bounded cost.

**Sketch:** src/core/topology-opt.js: refineChartCuts(mesh, chart, {sdBound:1.025, maxCuts:16, maxZips:64, seamCost, timeBudgetMs:1500}). SD normalisation: per face sd_f = (s1^2 + s2^2 + s1^-2 + s2^-2)/4 >= 1 (OptCuts' 4.1 bound → 1.025). Chart SD = area-weighted mean at rest scale (plan's normalizeChartScale). A) Augment: flatten (existing LSCM→SLIM). Vertex distortion d_v = area-weighted mean sd over incident faces, or area stretch |ln(A2D/A3D)| for conformal inits. Loop: if SD <= sdBound or cuts == maxCuts, stop. v* = argmax d_v over interior vertices with d_v > 1.5*median, excluding the 2-ring of existing cut tips. Path = multi-source Dijkstra from all boundary vertices to v*, weighted by seamCost (loop-aware if available). Apply the cut: split vertices along the path and duplicate UVs (both copies start at the same UV, so the configuration stays valid and energy does not increase). Run 20 SLIM iterations warm-started. If SD improved by < 2% relative, revert the cut and mark v* tabu. B) Zip: candidates are cut edges e=(a,b) whose endpoint b is a cut-tree leaf (dart tip) or both of whose sides lie on this chart. Order by seamCost(e)*V(e) descending, most visible first. Trial: merge the two UV copies of the non-tip vertex at their midpoint, then run 10 local SD gradient/Newton iterations on its 2-ring with everything else fixed. Accept the zip if local SD increase * local area / chart area + current SD <= sdBound, then confirm with 10 global SLIM iterations. Revert if the confirmed SD > sdBound, any flip appears, or the boundary self-intersects (plan's exact test). The OptCuts objective for reporting is E_w = (1-lambda)*E_SD + lambda*E_seam with E_seam = sum of cut lengths / sqrt(A_total). Cost: each accepted operation is about 20 SLIM iterations on one chart, which is fine for charts under 10k faces; skip larger charts or run under the time budget.
