**Sources:** BFF paper section on cone singularities: https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/1704.06873 (read); BFF ConePlacement.cpp: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GeometryCollective/boundary-first-flattening/master/src/project/ConePlacement.cpp (read: boundary vertices treated as cones; Dirichlet solves at cones; next cone = argmax |u|; curvature redistributed and normalised to 2*pi*chi); Ben-Chen, Gotsman, Bunin, Conformal Flattening by Curvature Prescription and Metric Scaling, EG 2008 (cited by BFF; not fetched)

### F54. Projected Newton for symmetric Dirichlet with a closed-form 2D eigensystem (SLIM warm-up, then Newton finish)

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/core/optimize.js (optimizeChart optimizer:'newton'|'slim+newton', sdEigenHessian helper in math.js); engine/presets defaults; benchmark page A/B`

**What:** A true Newton method on the symmetric Dirichlet energy. Each triangle's 4x4 Hessian with respect to vec(J) is made positive semidefinite analytically, with no eigen-solver, from its signed singular values. It is assembled into the same fixed 2n x 2n CSR pattern as SLIM and solved (direct LDLT or inexact PCG), with the existing flip-free maximum step and Armijo backtracking. Composite Majorization and the complex-view Hessians of Golla 2018 give essentially the same PSD proxy.

**Why:** SLIM, even with Anderson acceleration, converges linearly. It removes gross distortion in about 5-10 iterations and then stalls. Projected Newton converges quadratically once near the optimum, so the final SD and max-stretch tails drop further at the same per-iteration cost: one assembly and one solve on the same pattern. The published comparisons (Composite Majorization; Smith 2019) show large wall-clock wins at high accuracy. These are not re-measured here, so add a benchmark A/B comparison. The plan's optimizer: 'slim' | 'arap' becomes 'slim' | 'arap' | 'newton' | 'slim+newton' (default). It also removes the need to hand-tune Anderson safeguards in the late phase.

**Sketch:** Per triangle t with rest operator (gx, gy, A_t) from buildRestFrames: J = [[a,b],[c,d]], with a = sum gx_j u_j, b = sum gy_j u_j, c = sum gx_j v_j, d = sum gy_j v_j. Take the signed 2x2 SVD J = U diag(s1,s2) V^T (flip-free, so s1 >= s2 > 0). Use Psi = s1^2 + s1^-2 + s2^2 + s2^-2 (divide by 4 to match the plan's normalisation), with dPsi/ds_i = 2 s_i - 2 s_i^-3. Gradient: dPsi/dJ = U diag(dPsi/ds1, dPsi/ds2) V^T, pushed to vertices as g_u[j] += A_t (G11 gx_j + G12 gy_j) and g_v[j] += A_t (G21 gx_j + G22 gy_j). Eigen-decomposition of the 4x4 Hessian (my own derivation, self-checked at identity), each mode as (lambda, Q) with q = vec(Q) row-major [J11,J12,J21,J22]: scale-1: lambda = 2 + 6 s1^-4, Q = U diag(1,0) V^T. Scale-2: lambda = 2 + 6 s2^-4, Q = U diag(0,1) V^T (SD's sigma-Hessian is diagonal). Flip: lambda = 2 + 2(s1^2 + s1 s2 + s2^2)/(s1^3 s2^3), Q = U [[0,1],[1,0]] V^T / sqrt2. Twist: lambda = 2 - 2(s1^2 - s1 s2 + s2^2)/(s1^3 s2^3), Q = U [[0,-1],[1,0]] V^T / sqrt2. Check at s1 = s2 = 1: scale 8, flip 8, twist 0 (rotation). Only twist can go negative, so clamp lambda+ = max(lambda, 0). Let B be the 4x6 map from (u0,u1,u2,v0,v1,v2) to vec(J), with rows [gx,0], [gy,0], [0,gx], [0,gy]. For each mode, w = B^T q (a 6-vector) and K_t += A_t lambda+ w w^T. Scatter into the interleaved CSR through the precomputed slot map, which is identical to SLIM's pattern. Solve (K + delta I) p = -g with delta = 1e-8 * meanDiag. Use LDLT refactoring with a reused symbolic phase when affordable; otherwise PCG with tol = min(0.5, sqrt(|g|/|g0|)) relative (inexact Newton) and the prefactored-Laplacian preconditioner. Line search: alpha_max from the existing per-triangle quadratic, alpha = min(1, 0.9 alpha_max), then Armijo E(x + alpha p) <= E(x) + 1e-4 alpha g.p with halving up to 20 times. If the direction fails to descend (g.p >= 0), increase delta by 10x and retry. Schedule 'slim+newton': run SLIM while (E_prev - E)/E > 1e-2 (max 8 iterations), then Newton until |g|_inf * sqrt(totalArea) < 1e-7 or relative decrease < 1e-9 (max 30). The same eigen-formula generalises to any isotropic Psi(s1,s2): twist = (Psi1 + Psi2)/(s1 + s2), flip = (Psi1 - Psi2)/(s1 - s2) (use the closed form when s1 is about s2), scale = eigenpairs of the 2x2 sigma-Hessian.

**Risks:** Far from the optimum (for example a Tutte start) the PSD-projected Newton step is poor and many line-search halvings occur; this is exactly why SLIM warm-up (or Progressive/BFF init) is needed. Refactoring 2n x 2n systems per iteration is costly for charts above about 20k vertices, where inexact PCG is required. Degenerate or near-zero-area rest triangles give huge lambda, so clamp rest area to at least 1e-12 of the chart area. The eigen-formulas are derived rather than copied from the paper and must be finite-difference tested against the energy.

**Sources:** Smith, de Goes, Kim, Analytic Eigensystems for Isotropic Distortion Energies, ACM TOG 2019 (knowledge, not fetched): https://graphics.pixar.com/library/AnalyticEigensystems/; Shtengel, Poranne, Sorkine-Hornung, Kovalsky, Lipman, Geometric Optimization via Composite Majorization, ACM TOG 2017 (knowledge); Teran, Sifakis, Irving, Fedkiw 2005 (PSD projection); Golla, Seidel, Chen 2018 complex-view Hessians (knowledge); Rabinovich et al. SLIM 2017: https://igl.ethz.ch/projects/slim/ ; Blender SLIM energy s1^2+s1^-2+s2^2+s2^-2 with LDLT: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blender/blender/main/intern/slim/intern/slim.cpp (read); Claici, Bessmeltsev, Schaefer, Solomon, Isometry-Aware Preconditioning (AKVF), CGF 2017: https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/abs/10.1111/cgf.13243

### F55. Foldover-free untangling (Garanzha et al. 2021, 'Foldover-free maps in 50 lines of code') as a flip-repair stage and an 'Repair imported UVs' feature

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/core/optimize.js (untangleChart, lbfgs helper) or new src/core/untangle.js; parameterize.js initChart fallback chain; UI 'Repair UVs' in the IO/import panel and live UV editing`

**What:** A regularised, barrier-free energy that remains finite and smooth for inverted triangles. Each triangle has chi(D, eps) = (D + sqrt(eps^2 + D^2))/2, a smooth max(D,0), with D = det J. The shape term is f_eps = tr(J^T J) / chi(D,eps)^(2/d) (d = 2, so exponent 1) and the area term is g_eps = (D^2 + 1)/chi(D,eps). The energy is F = sum_t vol_t (f_eps + lambda g_eps). Minimising F for a sequence of decreasing eps drives every det J positive from arbitrarily tangled starts, with free or pinned boundaries.

**Why:** In the plan, any flip after LSCM makes initChart fall back to Tutte, which has high distortion and requires Progressive Parameterisation. The untangler instead repairs the few flipped triangles of a good conformal map (LSCM/BFF/ABF/prolongated multilevel map) while keeping its low distortion, and then hands a flip-free map to SLIM/PN, which needs flip-free input. It also enables features neither v3 nor v4 has: (a) repair foldovers in UVs imported from glTF/OBJ, with the boundary pinned to preserve the existing layout and packing or free followed by a repack; (b) keep maps valid while the user drags pins or edits seams in the UV view (live mode, a few iterations per frame).

**Sketch:** Use rest frames from buildRestFrames (3D isometric rest, so the energy measures real distortion; the paper uses an equilateral target for its benchmark), after scaling the UV so sum |signed UV area| = sum rest area. Per triangle J = [[a,b],[c,d]], D = ad - bc and cof = [[d,-c],[-b,a]] (dD/dJ). With r = sqrt(eps^2 + D^2): chi = (D + r)/2 and chi' = (1 + D/r)/2. f = (a^2+b^2+c^2+d^2)/chi and df/dJ = 2J/chi - (f chi'/chi) cof. g = (D^2+1)/chi and dg/dJ = (2D/chi - g chi'/chi) cof. Chain to vertices with gx/gy weighted by A_t, and zero gradients of pinned vertices. Outer loop (verified): eps^0 from the paper's practical rule eps = sqrt(1e-12 + 0.04 * min(0, min_t D_t)^2); if min D >= 0 initially, use eps = 1e-3 * mean|D|. Each stage minimises F(., eps) with L-BFGS (memory m = 8, two-loop recursion, Armijo backtracking c1 = 1e-4, first step scaled by 1/|g|, at most 200 iterations or until |g| < 1e-6 * |g0|). No flip-limited line search is needed because the energy is finite through inversion. Stage update (verified): sigma = max(0.1, 1 - F(U_new, eps)/F(U_old, eps)). If D_minus < 0: eps <- (1 - sigma * sqrt(D_minus^2 + eps^2)/(|D_minus| + sqrt(D_minus^2 + eps^2))) * eps; otherwise eps <- (1 - sigma) * eps. Stop when min_t D_t > 0 and F_k > (1 - 1e-3) F_{k-1}. Then run SLIM or projected Newton with the flip-free line search. lambda trades shape against area; start with lambda = 1 and expose it as 'area weight' (default not verified). Engine integration: initChart 'lscm'/'bff' -> countFlips > 0 -> untangle(local, {pinBoundary:false}) -> if still flipped then Tutte fallback (record it in fallbacks). Importer: per chart, flag det <= 0 triangles and offer 'Repair UVs (keep layout)', which calls untangle with pinBoundary:true.

**Risks:** Convergence to positive determinants is not guaranteed for every input; the paper reports rare failures on a large benchmark, so keep the Tutte fallback. The recommended lambda default and inner iteration counts were not found in the abstract/HTML summary. Pinned-boundary repair can be impossible when the pinned boundary itself self-overlaps. L-BFGS on 50k-vertex charts may need many iterations, so a PSD-projected Newton variant can be added later.

**Sources:** Garanzha, Kaporin, Kudryavtseva, Protais, Ray, Sokolov, Foldover-free maps in 50 lines of code, ACM TOG (SIGGRAPH) 2021: https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.03069 and https://arxiv.org/html/2102.03069v1 (read)

### F56. SCAF scaffold for global (self-overlap-free) bijectivity of each chart during SLIM or Newton

*Impact* medium · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* medium · verified · *Target* `new src/core/scaffold.js (cdt2d, buildScaffold, scafOptimize) plus hooks in optimize.js; metrics.js bijectivity used to decide when to enable`

**What:** The empty space between the chart boundary and a bounding frame is triangulated (the scaffold). Scaffold triangles get a tiny symmetric-Dirichlet energy whose rest shape is their own current shape, and they are included in the flip-free line search. The chart boundary cannot pass through itself without inverting a scaffold triangle, so local injectivity becomes global bijectivity. The scaffold is re-triangulated every iteration so it never blocks large motions.

**Why:** In the v4 plan, SLIM with a flip-free line search guarantees only local injectivity. Long arms, spirals and C-shaped charts can still overlap themselves after optimisation, and the plan only detects this (metrics.bijectivity.selfIntersectingCharts). With SCAF, zero self-overlap holds by construction. That removes a class of 'valid: false' results and the extra splits or projection fallbacks they would trigger. The same machinery enables a globally bijective live 'Relax' in the UV view.

**Sketch:** (1) Frame: centre m = (min + max)/2 of the chart UVs; frame corners m +/- 3 (max - m) (libigl scaf_range 3); 5 points per side. (2) Scaffold mesh: constrained Delaunay triangulation of {frame polygon, chart boundary loop(s)} with no Steiner points. Implement in JS: Bowyer-Watson or Delaunator-style incremental Delaunay on frame + boundary vertices, then constraint-edge recovery by edge flips (Sloan 1993), then flood-fill removal of triangles inside the chart. Around 400 lines; without the 'q' quality refinement, skinny scaffold triangles are acceptable. (3) Unknowns: chart vertices plus frame vertices, with frame vertices hard-pinned or soft-pinned (1e3). Boundary vertices are shared between chart and scaffold. (4) Per iteration: scaffold rest frames from current positions (J_s = I at x_k, so zero gradient but a regularising proxy). Weight w_s = chartRestArea * E_current / (numScaffoldTris * 100), with E normalised per unit area as in libigl. Local step for scaffold triangles uses the SLIM weights from their J; global matrix = L_chart + w_s L_scaffold on the same interleaved system (scaffold assembly changes pattern each remesh, so run symbolic LDLT again or use PCG); line search alpha_max = min over chart AND scaffold triangles of the flip-free root; energy for backtracking = E_chart + w_s E_scaffold. (5) Remeshing policy for JS speed: remesh after every accepted step (as the paper does), or lazily when the scaffold limits alpha_max < 0.1 or a scaffold min angle < 1 degree. (6) Engine option bijective: 'scaf' | 'check' (default 'check'; preset 'Game-ready' turns scaf on for charts whose bijectivity check fails, then re-optimises only those). Per-iteration cost adds O(b log b) for b boundary vertices.

**Risks:** A robust CDT in floating point is the hard part and needs an orient2d/incircle epsilon strategy (for example Shewchuk-style adaptive predicates or snapping). Remeshing every iteration changes the sparsity pattern, so factor reuse is lost for the combined system. A lighter alternative is the boundary edge-vertex barrier with collision-limited step of Smith & Schaefer 2015, but its formulas were not verified in this research. Inter-chart overlaps remain the packer's job.

**Sources:** Jiang, Schaefer, Panozzo, Simplicial Complex Augmentation Framework for Bijective Maps, ACM TOG (SIGGRAPH Asia) 2017 (knowledge); libigl implementation: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/libigl/libigl/main/include/igl/triangle/scaf.cpp (read: frame = UV bbox scaled 3x about centre, 5 frame points per side, remesh each iteration with Triangle 'qYYQ', scaffold weight = mesh_measure * energy / (sf_num * 100), L = L_mesh + L_scaffold, flip_avoiding_line_search over mesh + scaffold); Smith & Schaefer, Bijective Parameterization with Free Boundaries, SIGGRAPH 2015 (not read; the fetch was redirected): https://people.engr.tamu.edu/schaefer/research/bijective.pdf

### F57. LinABF / ABF++ angle-based flattening with angle-driven LSCM or harmonic reconstruction (Blender's 'Angle Based' method)

*Impact* medium · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/core/parameterize.js (solveLinABF, lscmFromAngles); preset 'Conformal (ABF)'; A/B compare`

**What:** The method first solves for planar triangle angles close to the 3D angles under the planarity constraints: triangle sum pi, interior-vertex sum 2pi, and the wheel (sine) consistency around each interior vertex. It then builds the UVs from those angles. LinABF linearises the sine constraint in log space, which turns ABF into one weighted least-norm linear solve (optionally 2-3 re-linearisations). ABF++ does Newton iterations with the triangle constraints eliminated analytically.

**Why:** LSCM uses 3D corner angles that are inconsistent for curved or irregular (obtuse, anisotropic) meshes, so its angle error concentrates badly on coarse game meshes. Angles from ABF are exactly planar-consistent, so reconstruction leaves little conformal error, usually noticeably lower angular distortion than LSCM. That matters on low-poly assets where SLIM's isometric optimum is not wanted (for example normal-map baking favours conformality). It also gives the A/B compare view a third classic reference method matching Blender's default.

**Sketch:** Unknowns: corrections e_c for every corner c (3T), with alpha = beta* + e. Targets: beta*_c = theta_c * 2pi/sum theta at interior vertices and theta_c at boundary vertices (3D angles). Weights w_c = 1/beta*_c^2. Constraints C e = r: (i) per triangle, e_1 + e_2 + e_3 = pi - sum beta*; (ii) per interior vertex, sum over its corners of e = 2pi - sum beta* (0 by construction on the first pass); (iii) per interior vertex (wheel), with c1 the next corner and c2 the previous corner in each incident triangle, sum_t [cot(beta*_c1) e_c1 - cot(beta*_c2) e_c2] = sum_t [log sin beta*_c2 - log sin beta*_c1]. Solution: lambda from (C W^-1 C^T) lambda = r, then e = W^-1 C^T lambda, with W^-1 = diag(beta*^2). Reduction: C_tri W^-1 C_tri^T is diagonal (each corner lies in one triangle, entry = sum of that triangle's beta*^2), so eliminate the T triangle multipliers analytically and solve the SPD Schur complement of size 2 V_int with LDLT. Re-linearise at alpha 2-3 times (Gauss-Newton), clamping alpha to [1e-4, pi - 1e-4]. Reconstruction option A (Blender style): per triangle, set local 2D edge lengths from the law of sines (the edge opposite corner k is proportional to sin alpha_k, scaled so the perimeter matches 3D) and feed these local triangles to the existing solveLSCM row assembly instead of the 3D-projected triangles. Option B (pin-free): a harmonic map with cot(alpha) weights and BFF's boundary pipeline, where interior Omega = 0 by construction, so only boundary curvature k = pi - sum alpha applies. Register as initChart method 'abf'.

**Risks:** Near-degenerate angles make the log-sin terms ill-conditioned, so clamping is required. Without iteration the linearised solution is only approximate. The benefit over BFF (which is also conformal and pin-free) is mostly on coarse meshes, so it may be lower priority than BFF. Formulas are from memory of the papers and were not re-fetched.

**Sources:** Sheffer, Levy, Mogilnitsky, Bogomyakov, ABF++: Fast and Robust Angle Based Flattening, ACM TOG 24(2) 2005 (knowledge); Zayer, Levy, Seidel, Linear Angle Based Parameterization, SGP 2007 (knowledge); Blender manual 4.3 UV operators (Angle Based / Conformal / Minimum Stretch): https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/4.3/modeling/meshes/editing/uv.html

### F58. Spectral Conformal Parameterization (Mullen et al. 2008): pin-free LSCM via a generalized eigenvector

*Impact* medium · *Effort* S · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/core/parameterize.js (solveSCP); presets / A/B compare`

**What:** LSCM's conformal energy E_C = E_Dirichlet - Area is a PSD quadratic form x^T L_C x with a 2D null space of translations. Instead of pinning two vertices, SCP minimises x^T L_C x subject to x^T B x = 1 (B a boundary mass matrix) and B-orthogonality to the constants, i.e. the Fiedler vector of L_C x = lambda B x. It is computed by inverse iteration with one factorisation of L_C.

**Why:** It removes LSCM's dependence on pin choice and the stretching or shrinking around the two pins that shows up on elongated or curved charts, at almost no extra code: the same LSCM assembly plus 10-40 back-solves with the LDLT finding. It is a cheap robust default for 'conformal' presets if BFF is postponed, and a third conformal reference in A/B compare. Unlike BFF it offers no boundary control or cones.

**Sketch:** Build L_D (2n x 2n interleaved) = cotan stiffness (A_ij = -1/2(cot + cot)) applied to u and v separately. Area term: for each directed boundary edge i->j of the CCW loop, signed area = 1/2 sum (u_i v_j - u_j v_i), as a symmetric matrix M with M[u_i, v_j] and M[v_j, u_i] += 1/4 and M[u_j, v_i] and M[v_i, u_j] -= 1/4. Set L_C = L_D - M (scale so x^T L_C x = LSCM energy). Unit test: L_C x = 0 for the identity map of a planar patch and for any similarity of it, which fixes the signs. B: diagonal with the lumped boundary length b_i = 1/2(l_prev + l_next) on u_i and v_i of boundary vertices, 0 elsewhere. Factor F = LDLT(L_C + eps I) with eps = 1e-9 * meanDiag. Inverse iteration: start x = the planar projection; repeat y = solve(F, B x); remove the constant components (y_u -= (e^T B y_u)/(e^T B e), same for v); x = y / sqrt(y^T B y); stop when the Rayleigh quotient changes by less than 1e-10 relatively (typically 10-40 iterations). Eigenvalues come in rotation pairs ((u,v) and (-v,u) have equal energy), so convergence to that 2D eigenspace is enough: any combination is a rotated or scaled copy of the same conformal map. Then check orientation (negate v if the signed area < 0) and normalizeChartScale. Register as initChart method 'scp'.

**Risks:** It needs the direct solver; with PCG inside the inverse iteration it would be slow. The exact normalisation of B in the paper (plain boundary indicator vs lumped lengths, centring term) was not re-verified. Like LSCM it can flip on bad meshes, so keep the flip check and untangle fallback. BFF (u_B = 0) supersedes it when implemented.

**Sources:** Mullen, Tong, Alliez, Desbrun, Spectral Conformal Parameterization, Computer Graphics Forum (SGP) 2008 (knowledge; not fetched within budget)

### F59. Multilevel coarse-to-fine flattening for very large charts (2026 Beltrami-coefficient prolongation, plus a simpler progressive-mesh prolongation)

*Impact* medium · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* medium · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/core/multilevel.js (simplifyChart, prolongate) used by engine.js when chart vertex count > threshold (for example 20k)`

**What:** Instead of running a full-space optimiser (SLIM/Progressive) on a 50k-500k-face chart, simplify the chart, parameterise the coarse mesh with low isometric distortion, and transfer the result back. Fargion & Weber prolong the coarse map's Beltrami coefficients (which encode the conformal distortion, i.e. the deformed triangle angles) to the fine mesh. That yields a customised Laplacian whose harmonic map reproduces the low-distortion map, and they report injective free-boundary maps up to 50x faster than state-of-the-art methods.

**Why:** The v4 plan's cost per SLIM iteration scales with chart size, and Progressive Parameterisation from a Tutte start needs many iterations on huge charts. Solving about 5k coarse vertices plus one fine linear solve and 2-4 fine polishing iterations should cut large-chart optimise time several-fold. That keeps 'whole-object' and low-seam presets interactive in the browser worker, where v3 and v4 would take tens of seconds.

**Sketch:** (1) Simplify: edge-collapse priority queue (typed-array min-heap) keyed by 3D edge length or QEM, down to min(5000, n/8) vertices. Preserve disk topology with the link condition, collapse boundary vertices only along boundary edges, and reject collapses that rotate any face normal by more than 60 degrees or create triangles with an aspect ratio above 20. Record the collapse sequence (the progressive mesh). (2) Coarse solve: BFF, SLIM+Newton and untangle as needed until converged. (3a) Simple prolongation (recommended first): reinsert vertices in reverse collapse order. Place each new vertex at the MVC-weighted average of its current 1-ring UVs (3D mean-value weights, Floater) and run 1-3 local Gauss-Seidel SD steps on that ring if any ring triangle flips. At the fine level run untangle if flips remain, then 2-4 SLIM/PN iterations. (3b) Beltrami prolongation (paper idea; details unverified): for each coarse triangle compute from J = [[a,b],[c,d]] the values f_z = 1/2((a+d) + i(c-b)), f_zbar = 1/2((a-d) + i(c+b)) and mu = f_zbar/f_z (|mu| = (s1-s2)/(s1+s2)). Assign mu to the fine triangles whose centroids map into that coarse triangle, optionally averaged over neighbours. Build the Beltrami Laplacian per fine triangle as K_t = A_t G^T P G with G the 2x3 gradient operator (gx, gy) and P = (1/(1-|mu|^2)) [[(rho-1)^2 + tau^2, -2 tau], [-2 tau, (rho+1)^2 + tau^2]] for mu = rho + i tau; equivalently, cotan weights of the rest triangle deformed by the affine map with that mu. Solve the u and v harmonic problems with Dirichlet boundary = prolonged coarse boundary positions (one LDLT factorisation, two solves), then check flips and polish.

**Risks:** Only the abstract-level description of the 2026 paper was verified. How it handles free boundaries and guarantees injectivity is unknown, and the Beltrami Laplacian form above comes from the Linear Beltrami Solver literature, not from that paper. Simplification that preserves disk topology and avoids degenerate triangles is fiddly. Prolongated maps can flip, so the untangler is required. Benefits appear only for large charts, and small charts should skip the path.

**Sources:** Fargion & Weber, Fast Injective Mesh Parameterization via Beltrami Coefficient Prolongation, Computer Graphics Forum 2026: https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/cgf.70341 (abstract via search only; full text returned 403); Lui, Lam, Yau, Gu, Teichmuller mapping / Linear Beltrami Solver (2013-2014) for the discrete Beltrami-weighted Laplacian (knowledge); Liu et al., Progressive Parameterizations, TOG 2018: https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3197517.3201331

### F60. Importance-weighted and energy-selectable optimisation: painted texel-density or priority weights (Blender 4.3 'Importance Weights') and exponential-SD max-stretch control

*Impact* medium · *Effort* S · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/core/optimize.js (buildRestFrames scale/priority inputs, energy registry), metrics.js (density-aware stretch), engine.js options, UI paint mode`

**What:** Blender 4.3's 'Minimum Stretch' unwrap is SLIM with symmetric Dirichlet. It lets a vertex group ('Importance Weights') bias which regions get priority, and it supports exponential energies with exp_factor. The generic SLIM reweighting for any isotropic energy Psi(s1,s2) is W = U diag(w1,w2) U^T with w_i = sqrt(dPsi/ds_i / (2(s_i - 1))), which makes energy choice a small, local code change.

**Why:** The plan fixes the energy to 'sd' | 'arap' and treats every triangle equally. Painted weights give artists texel-density control (faces, hands and logos get more UV area) and priority control (keep stretch low where it matters) without new seams, matching Blender 4.3 and going beyond v3/v4. Exponential SD suppresses the worst-stretched triangles, which the plan's area-averaged SD tolerates, so it directly improves the max-stretch metric and histogram tail shown in the UI.

**Sketch:** Data: per-vertex weight Float32Array(W) in [0,1], painted in the 3D view (brush with radius and falloff) or imported from glTF vertex colour or a custom attribute; per-face mean wbar_t. Two independent controls. (a) Density: target scale k_t = 2^(gamma (wbar_t - 0.5) * 2), with gamma = 'density range' in octaves (default 1, so +/- 1 octave in edge length). Apply it in buildRestFrames by scaling the rest 2D coordinates by k_t, i.e. gx, gy /= k_t and A_t *= k_t^2 (so the triangle's rest area grows k_t^2), followed by normalizeChartScale. (b) Priority: omega_t = 1 + p * wbar_t (p = 'priority strength', default 4) multiplies A_t only in the energy and assembly, not in the rest geometry. Energy dropdown: 'sd' (w_i = sqrt((s - s^-3)/(s - 1)), as in the plan); 'arap' (w = 1); 'exp-sd' with Psi = exp(s_exp (SD/4 - 1)), dPsi/ds_i = exp(...) * (s_exp/4) * (2 s_i - 2 s_i^-3), so w_i = w_i^SD * sqrt((s_exp/4) exp(s_exp (SD_t/4 - 1))). Use s_exp = 1 (Blender's exp_factor default) and clamp the exponent to <= 50. For projected Newton reuse the generic analytic eigen-formulas with Psi' and Psi''. Metrics: report texel density error relative to k_t (the expected scale) so painted regions are not flagged as stretch. UI: 'Paint importance' mode, sliders for density range and priority, and the energy selector in presets.

**Risks:** Blender's exact weight-to-scale formula was not extracted; only that per-face weights multiply the Jacobian columns was confirmed, and the k_t/omega_t mapping above is a design proposal. Exponential energies can overflow or become stiff and need exponent clamps and line-search safeguards. Density painting across chart seams can create visible texel-density jumps, so consider smoothing wbar across seams.

**Sources:** Blender 4.3 Minimum Stretch (SLIM) PR: https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender/pulls/114545 ; release notes: https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.3/modeling/ ; manual: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/4.3/modeling/meshes/editing/uv.html; Blender slim.cpp (read): per-face weights multiply Jacobian columns (weights.cwiseProduct(Ji.col(k))), energies SYMMETRIC_DIRICHLET / ARAP / LOG_ARAP / CONFORMAL / EXP variants, exp_factor = 1.0, proximal_p = 1e-4, soft pin constraints: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blender/blender/main/intern/slim/intern/slim.cpp; Rabinovich, Poranne, Panozzo, Sorkine-Hornung, SLIM, TOG 2017 (generic reweighting formula): https://igl.ethz.ch/projects/slim/

## Feature & UX benchmark of professional UV tools (Blender 4.x/5.x, RizomUV, Maya UV Toolkit, Houdini/SideFX Labs, UVPackmaster, ZBrush UV Master, Unity/Unreal lightmap UVs, Ministry of Flat): user-facing features worth adding to the pure-JS v4 kernel, each with an implementable algorithm and UI sketch

### F61. User pins + Live Unwrap (constrained LSCM/SLIM with warm-started worker re-solves)

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/core/parameterize.js (solveLSCM pins option), src/core/optimize.js (DOF elimination), src/core/engine.js (pinCorner, liveUnwrap, unwrapSelected), src/core/worker-main.js (new ops), src/ui/uv-view.js (vertex select/drag), src/client/engine-client.js (request coalescing)`

**What:** Blender lets artists pin UV vertices (P / Alt-P) so Unwrap keeps them fixed, and Live Unwrap keeps re-solving the selected islands while pinned vertices are dragged. Pack Islands also has 'Lock Pinned Islands'. Maya Unfold and RizomUV have the same pin/lock idea. The v4 plan only uses LSCM's two automatic farthest-apart pins and has no user constraints.

**Why:** Right now neither v3 nor the v4 plan lets the user control a chart's shape: every solve is automatic. Pins turn the same LSCM/SLIM code into a direct-manipulation tool, and they are also what Rectify, Straighten, Stitch and Lock-in-pack need. A warm-started PCG from the previous solution usually converges in about 5-30 iterations instead of hundreds from a cold start. My estimate (not benchmarked) is interactive drag rates in the worker for charts up to about 5-10k faces.

**Sketch:** (1) State: the engine keeps pinCorner: Uint8Array(3F). Pins live on corners (UV vertices), so pinning one side of a seam does not pin the other side. A chart-local vertex is pinned if any corner merged into it is pinned. Its rest-scale target is local = R(-theta)*(packed - t)/s, using the chart's pack transform. New API: engine.setPins(cornerIds, value); engine.unwrapSelected(chartIds, opts), which keeps pins; engine.liveUnwrap(chartId, [{localId,u,v}], {solver:'lscm'|'slim', maxIter}), which returns only that chart's packedUV. (2) LSCM with k pins: split the conformal matrix M (2T rows, interleaved u,v unknowns) into free columns F and pinned columns P. Solve (M_F^T M_F) x_F = -M_F^T M_P x_P with pcg (block2), warm-started from the current UV. k=0: keep the current farthest-pair rule. k=1: solve with the auto pair, then translate so the pin matches exactly. k>=2: the solution is unique. (3) SLIM/ARAP with pins: map each dof to a row index (-1 if pinned), assemble K_FF, and subtract K_FP x_P from the rhs. Apply the proximal term only to free DOFs. The line-search direction has d_P = 0, so the existing per-triangle alpha_max quadratic works unchanged. If the pinned start already has flips (the user dragged a pin through the island), run 3 ARAP iterations with pins (no barrier), then switch to SLIM. If flips remain, report 'pins force a fold-over' instead of looping. (4) Live Unwrap: dragging a pinned vertex in the UV view posts at most one request at a time; newer drags overwrite a single pending slot (latest wins). The worker caches each chart's CSR pattern and rest frames between calls, so each tick only refills values. LSCM tick: pcg maxIter 30, tol 1e-4, warm start. On pointerup, run full SLIM (12 iterations, Anderson 5) and refresh metrics. (5) Packing: 'Lock pinned islands' pre-blits pinned charts at their current position (see the packing finding). UI: UV-view vertex mode picks the nearest UV vertex within 6 CSS px through a uniform grid. P / Alt+P pin and unpin; pinned vertices are drawn as red squares; a Live toggle; 'Unwrap selected (U)' honours pins. Tests: pins held to 1e-9; LSCM pinned at its own unpinned solution reproduces that solution; SLIM energy never increases and no flips are accepted with pins.

**Risks:** Pins that contradict each other (very close pins with very different UVs) cause flips that SLIM's barrier cannot fix; this needs the ARAP pre-pass and a clear message. Corner-based pins complicate the snapshot format. Live-drag speed on very large charts depends on PCG iteration counts that have not been benchmarked.

**Sources:** Blender Manual (current, UV editing: Pin/Unpin P/Alt-P, Live Unwrap, Lock Pinned Islands) RST source: https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/uv/editing.rst; Blender Manual rendered page: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/meshes/uv/editing.html; Levy et al. 2002, LSCM (pinned-vertex elimination), SIGGRAPH: https://doi.org/10.1145/566654.566590; Rabinovich et al. 2017, SLIM, ACM TOG: https://doi.org/10.1145/2983621

### F62. Quad-grid Rectify: Follow Active Quads, rectangle-boundary unwrap, and Straighten edge chains

*Impact* high · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `new src/core/rectify.js (quad recovery, follow-quads, rectangle boundary, straighten), src/core/optimize.js (per-coordinate constraints), src/core/engine.js (rectifyCharts op), src/ui/uv-view.js + seam-tool.js (active quad pick)`

**What:** Tools that turn quad strips and grids (belts, planks, trims, pipes, hard-surface panels) into perfectly axis-aligned rectangular UV grids. Blender has Follow Active Quads and Align Straighten, Maya has Straighten UVs, RizomUV has Align/Distribute, and TexTools has Rectify. These are among the most-used hard-surface and trim-sheet tools. Neither v3 nor the plan can produce straight UV borders.

**Why:** SLIM and LSCM give curved, wobbly boundaries even on clean quad strips, which wastes packing space and makes trim sheets and tiling textures impossible. A rectified strip packs into its bounding box (100% bbox fill against roughly 70-85% for a relaxed organic boundary), aligns to texel rows, and is what hard-surface artists expect.

**Sketch:** A. Quad recovery from the triangle soup. For each uncut interior edge e=(f,g) that is the longest edge of both triangles, form a quad from the 4 welded vertices. Score = (1 - n_f.n_g) + sum over corners of |angle - 90deg|/360. Accept only if the dihedral is under 15deg and every quad corner angle is in [45,135]deg. Greedy matching in ascending score, each triangle in at most one quad. Store quads: Int32Array(4Q) in cyclic order, plus quadOfFace. B. Follow Active Quads. The user clicks an active quad in 3D (or auto-pick: the most rectangular quad near the chart centroid). Set its UVs to a W x H rectangle, where W and H are the mean 3D lengths of its opposite edge pairs. BFS over quad adjacency through uncut edges. When crossing shared edge (b,c) from quad (a,b,c,d) into a neighbour whose other vertices are x (adjacent to b) and y (adjacent to c): uv_x = uv_b + (uv_b - uv_a)*k and uv_y = uv_c + (uv_c - uv_d)*k. Here k = 1 for Even and k = |x-b|3D / |b-a|3D for Length. Length Average: union-find quad edges into edge rings (opposite edges of a quad share a ring) and give each ring its mean 3D length l_r. Assign integer grid indices (i,j) to vertices during the BFS and set u = sum over i' < i of l(column ring i'), and v likewise. If any vertex gets two different (i,j) (irregular valence or monodromy), abort Length Average and fall back to Length. Leftover triangles in the chart are solved by SLIM with all grid vertices pinned (reusing the pin machinery). C. Rectangle-boundary unwrap, for strips that are not clean grids. For each boundary vertex, compute the 3D interior angle sum theta_v over its chart triangles. Take the 4 boundary vertices with the smallest theta_v among those under 150deg, at least 5% of the perimeter apart. Side arc lengths L0..L3 give W = (L0+L2)/2 and H = (L1+L3)/2. Place boundary vertices on the rectangle by arc-length fraction. Fill the interior with mean-value Tutte on the fixed boundary, which is bijective because a rectangle is convex. Then run SLIM with sliding constraints: top/bottom vertices fix only v, left/right vertices fix only u, and corners fix both. Per-coordinate DOF elimination is trivial with interleaved unknowns. This keeps the rectangle while relaxing arc-length spacing. D. Straighten, for a selected UV edge chain or automatically for every boundary chain between corners whose chord is within tol = 10deg of U or V. Project chain vertices onto the chord (or set v = mean v), space them by 3D arc length, pin them, and re-solve the interior with SLIM. Add a metric 'gridness' = area fraction of chart quads whose UV edges are within 1deg of an axis. UI: Rectify button with a mode dropdown (Grid / Rectangle / Straighten boundary), click-active-quad in 3D, and a tolerance slider.

**Risks:** The triangle-pairing heuristic can pick the wrong diagonal on skewed quads; offer 'import quads from OBJ f-lines' later (the OBJ loader would need to keep polygon ids). The corner heuristic fails on charts without 4 clear corners, so fall back to Straighten-only. Grid-index monodromy on cylinders needs a cut first (cutToDisk already provides one). The Blender follow-quads propagation formula is from memory of the script, not re-read.

**Sources:** Blender Manual, Follow Active Quads edge length modes Even / Length / Length Average (RST): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/editing/uv.rst; Blender Manual, Align > Straighten / Straighten X / Straighten Y (RST): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/uv/editing.rst; Blender source uvcalc_follow_active.py (from memory, not re-fetched): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender/src/branch/main/scripts/startup/bl_operators/uvcalc_follow_active.py; Maya Straighten UVs (angle tolerance) via Autodesk UV mapping tips: https://knowledge.autodesk.com/support/maya/learn-explore/caas/CloudHelp/cloudhelp/2020/ENU/Maya-Modeling/files/GUID-1CEF8A5B-1051-40F1-ACFE-F29AD066F56F-htm.html; Floater 1997, convex-boundary Tutte embeddings are bijective: https://doi.org/10.1016/S0167-8396(96)00031-3

### F63. Island transform and alignment toolkit (Align Rotation Auto/Edge/Geometry, align/distribute/arrange, texel snap)

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `new src/core/islands.js; src/core/engine.js (transformCharts, undo); src/ui/uv-view.js (gizmo, pivot, snapping); src/ui/main.js (toolbar/shortcuts)`

**What:** Everyday island manipulation that all professional tools ship: move, rotate and scale islands with a modal gizmo; auto-orient islands to axes; orient islands so a world direction (for example up) points +V; align, distribute and arrange islands; mirror; and snap to the texel grid. The plan's uv-view only supports 'islandmove' dragging, and orientToAxis happens only inside packing.

**Why:** The plan can only move islands. These tools make the generated layout finishable by hand. The closed-form auto-orientation is O(E) and exact, and geometry-based orientation fixes upside-down wood grain, text and decal direction, which the packer's min-area rectangle ignores.

**Sketch:** Add a pure module src/core/islands.js that edits per-chart transforms {scale, rotation, tx, ty} (already in pack.js output), so edits compose and undo cleanly. (1) Align Rotation Auto: minimise sum over UV edges of l_e*sin^2(2(phi_e - theta)), where phi_e is the edge angle, l_e its UV length, and boundary edges are weighted x2. Because sin^2(2x) = (1 - cos 4x)/2, the optimum is theta* = 0.25*atan2(sum l_e sin 4phi_e, sum l_e cos 4phi_e). Rotate by -theta*, then optionally stand the island up (h >= w). (2) Align Rotation Edge: the same formula restricted to selected edges, then pick the 90deg multiple that points their mean direction +V. (3) Align Rotation Geometry, with world axis a in {X,Y,Z} (like Maya Orient Shells 3D, or Houdini's V Direction attribute). For each triangle, express a in the isometric rest frame: r_t = (a.e1, a.e2); skip the triangle if |r_t| < 0.2. Its UV direction is d_t = J_t r_t, using buildRestFrames gradients. Sum d = sum A_t d_t and rotate the island by (pi/2 - atan2(d_y, d_x)) so world-up maps to +V. (4) Align positions (Blender Align with Position Mode Mean/Min/Max): set every selected island's bbox min, centre or max on one axis to the min, mean or max of the selection. (5) Distribute (RizomUV): sort by bbox centre along an axis; gap = (span - sum of widths)/(n - 1). (6) Arrange (Blender 5.0): shelf layout sorted by height descending, row width = 1.1*sqrt(sum of bbox areas), margin in texels. (7) Transform: rotate/scale about a pivot (bbox centre, UV-area centroid, or 2D cursor); mirror U/V (warn that mirroring reverses winding, so metrics will report flips); move by one UDIM tile; texel snap tx = round(tx*R)/R and ty likewise, so bbox corners land on texel boundaries. Engine API: transformCharts(ids, op) -> UnwrapResult, recomputing bijectivity only for touched charts. UI: Blender-style modal gizmo in uv-view.js (G/R/S, X/Y axis lock, typed numeric input, Ctrl for 15deg or 1-texel steps), toolbar buttons for Align Auto/Edge/Geometry, and an 'Orient islands: none / auto / world-up' option in unwrap presets. Tests: a rectangle rotated by 23deg returns to axis-aligned within 1e-9; geometry mode maps a cylinder's +Y to +V.

**Risks:** Manual edits invalidate cached packing and bijectivity, so dirty flags are needed. Geometry mode is ill-defined for charts nearly perpendicular to the axis (skip them and keep Auto). Blender's exact internal Auto algorithm was not read; the closed-form 4-phi mean is my own formulation.

**Sources:** Blender Manual: Align Rotation (Auto / Edge / Geometry), Align (Straighten, Align Auto/Vertically/Horizontally; Position Mode Mean/Min/Max), Mirror, Randomize (RST): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/uv/editing.rst; Blender 5.0 release notes, Modeling & UV ('Arrange and align selected UV islands', 'Move Selected UVs on Axis', Pack to custom region): https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/5.0/modeling/; RizomUV changelog (Distribute islands/edges/vertices, Photoshop-like): https://www.rizomuv.com/changelog/; Maya Orient Shells (3D/world direction or 2D horizontal/vertical), Autodesk UV tips: https://knowledge.autodesk.com/support/maya/learn-explore/caas/CloudHelp/cloudhelp/2020/ENU/Maya-Modeling/files/GUID-1CEF8A5B-1051-40F1-ACFE-F29AD066F56F-htm.html

### F64. Stack Similar islands + symmetry-aware seams and Fold/Copy Mirrored UVs

*Impact* medium · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `new src/core/similarity.js (descriptors, Procrustes matching, symmetry detection); src/core/pack.js (leader-only rasterisation); src/core/metrics.js (stack-aware bijectivity, faceFlag 3); src/core/segmentation.js (symmetric growth option); src/ui/uv-view.js`

**What:** Detects islands with the same shape (repeated bolts, planks, instanced or mirrored parts) and stacks them onto one texture region to save space. Symmetry tools detect a mirror plane, mirror seams, and copy or fold UVs across it. This is standard in RizomUV, UVPackmaster, Maya (Stack Similar) and Blender (Copy Mirrored UV).

**Why:** The plan treats every island as unique and its bijectivity check treats any overlap as an error, so kit-bashed or symmetric assets waste 2x or more texture space. Stacking N identical islands frees (N-1)/N of their area, which directly raises achievable texel density after the scale search. Symmetric seams also give left/right-consistent layouts that artists expect.

**Sketch:** (1) Bucketing: per chart d = (faces, local verts, boundary loop count, area3D, perimeter3D, areaUV). Sort by area3D and sweep pairs whose area and perimeter differ by less than 3% (Border mode ignores counts). (2) Border-shape match: resample the outer UV loop to N = 64 points by arc length, centre at the UV area centroid, and normalise by sqrt(areaUV) if Adjust Scale is on. For each cyclic shift k (and the reversed, x-mirrored sequence if flipping is allowed), solve 2D Procrustes in closed form: theta = atan2(sum(p_x q_y - p_y q_x), sum(p_x q_x + p_y q_y)), s = sum(p . Rq)/sum|q|^2. Residual rho = sqrt(mean|p - sRq|^2)/sqrt(areaUV); accept if rho < threshold (default 0.01). Speed-up: coarse N=16 over all shifts, then refine the best 3 shifts at N=64, about 2k flops per pair. (3) Vertex/Topology mode: if vertex counts are equal, after Procrustes match vertices by nearest neighbour in a uniform grid (radius = vertexThreshold*sqrt(areaUV), default 0.002). Require a bijection, and identical triangle index sets for Topology mode. 'Correct Vertices' then copies the leader's UVs exactly so stacked texels are identical. (4) Stack: the leader is the largest island in each group. Members get T_member = T_leader composed with A_member->leader. The packer rasterises only leaders. (5) Symmetry: test mirror planes from {bbox-centre X/Y/Z planes, PCA planes}. Reflect each welded vertex and look it up in a hash grid with cell = 1e-4 x bbox diagonal; accept if at least 98% match; store mirrorVert: Int32Array. Offer three options: (a) Symmetric seams: mirror every manual cut edge, and grow segmentation on one half and map faces to the other; (b) Copy Mirrored UV: copy each corner's UV to its mirror corner; (c) Fold mirrored: stack each chart with its mirror chart. Contract changes: result.stacks: Int32Array[]. bijectivity.valid ignores overlap pairs inside the same stack. Faces of mirrored stack members get a new faceFlag 3 'mirrored-stack' instead of 'flipped', because mirrored twins map with reversed UV orientation. UI: Select Similar, Stack Similar and Unstack buttons with a threshold slider; stacks drawn with a shared hatch and a member-count badge; the exporter warns that stacked charts are not valid for lightmaps or unique baking.

**Risks:** Border-only matching can stack islands that match in UV but differ in 3D (for example relief detail), so default to Vertex mode when counts match. Changing metric semantics (intentional overlap) must be explicit in the report or users will misread validity. UVPackmaster's internal similarity algorithm is not documented; the Procrustes approach is my own.

**Sources:** UVPackmaster 4 docs, Aligning Functionalities (Select Similar, Align Similar, Group By Similarity, Split Overlapping; modes Border Shape / Vertex Position / Topology; Precision default 500, Similarity Threshold, Flipping Enable, Adjust Scale, Match 3D Axis, Correct Vertices, Vertex Threshold): https://uvpackmaster.com/doc4/blender/latest/35-aligning-functionalities/; UVPackmaster 3 Stack Groups: https://uvpackmaster.com/doc3/blender/latest/20-packing-functionalities/55-stack-groups/; Blender Manual, Copy Mirrored UV Coordinates (Mesh Axis, UV Axis, Precision) (RST): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/uv/editing.rst; RizomUV (Stack Similar, Fold Mirrored), changelog: https://www.rizomuv.com/changelog/

### F65. Texel-density toolkit: Get/Set/Match/Normalize in px/unit, per-island multipliers, painted importance weights

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/core/islands.js (TD ops), src/core/optimize.js (per-face target scale in buildRestFrames), src/core/pack.js (multipliers, fixed-scale mode), src/core/metrics.js (absolute TD, td-target heat), src/ui/main.js + src/ui/app.js (TD panel, unit setting, density brush)`

**What:** Texel density (TD) in real units: read it from islands, set it to a target value, match it to the active island, or normalise a selection to its mean, min or max. Per-island multipliers give more resolution to faces or less to soles, and painted density weights change resolution inside a chart. Maya, RizomUV, ZBrush (Density painting) and Blender (Importance Weights) offer versions of this.

**Why:** The plan has only a binary equalizeDensity and a relative TD coefficient of variation. Production artists work to an absolute target (for example px/m at a map size) shared across many assets, and deliberately vary density (faces, hands). The painted weight also enters SLIM itself, so density changes smoothly inside a chart instead of only per island.

**Sketch:** Show this definition everywhere: TD_i = R*sqrt(A_uv,i / A_3D,i) px per scene unit (A_uv in 0-1 UV units, R = map size). Also show px/m via a unit setting (glTF = metres; OBJ/STL/PLY user-selected cm/m/in). Operations in islands.js: Get reads the active island's TD, or the selection's area-weighted TD R*sqrt(sum A_uv / sum A_3D). Set scales each selected island about its pivot by k = TD_target/TD_i. Match sets all to the active island's TD. Normalize (RizomUV) targets the mean, min or max of the selection. All of these change transforms only and are followed by an optional fixed-scale repack (equalizeDensity=false, no secant search). Per-island multiplier m_i (default 1): the packer uses s_i = sqrt(A_3D,i*m_i^2/A_uv,i)*D, and metrics report density relative to m_i. Painted importance (ZBrush Density, Blender Importance Weights): a per-face weight w_f in [0.25, 4]. Inside a chart, it scales each rest triangle by w_f in SLIM (gx and gy divided by w_f, A_t multiplied by w_f^2, normalizeChartScale uses sum A_t w_f^2), so the optimal UV triangle is w_f times larger. Between charts, m_i = sqrt(sum A_f w_f^2 / sum A_f). Metric additions: perChart.tdPxPerUnit, a TD histogram, CV relative to targets, and heat mode 'td-target' colouring log2(TD/target) on a diverging ramp (blue low, green within 10% of target, red high). UI: a TD panel with Get / Set / Match / Normalize buttons, a numeric field with a map-size dropdown (512-8192), a multiplier field per selected island, and a 3D Density brush that shares infrastructure with seam painting.

**Risks:** Scene units are unknown for OBJ/STL/PLY, so users must pick a unit or the absolute px/m value is meaningless. Commonly quoted game targets (for example 10.24 px/cm at 4K for FPS hero assets) are conventions I could not verify and should stay editable presets. Fixed-TD packing may overflow the atlas; combine with multi-tile UDIM packing or scale down with a warning.

**Sources:** Maya texel density Get/Set with Map Size (Autodesk UV tips): https://knowledge.autodesk.com/support/maya/learn-explore/caas/CloudHelp/cloudhelp/2020/ENU/Maya-Modeling/files/GUID-1CEF8A5B-1051-40F1-ACFE-F29AD066F56F-htm.html; RizomUV texel density workflow overhaul (read selection density, normalise to average/min/...): https://lesterbanks.com/2020/05/rizomuv-gets-texel-density-workflow-overhaul/; Blender Manual, Minimum Stretch (SLIM) unwrap: Iterations, No Flip, Importance Weights (Weight Group, Weight Factor) (RST): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/editing/uv.rst; ZBrush UV Master Control Painting 'Density' (Maxon help): https://help.maxon.net/zbr/en-us/Content/html/user-guide/zbrush-plugins/uv-master/uv-master.html

### F66. Production packing options: lock islands, pack-to-region, merge overlapping, pack groups, fixed-density UDIM multi-tile

*Impact* high · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/core/pack.js (locks, region mask, super-islands, multi-tile), src/core/engine.js (repack opts: lock, region, groups, udim), src/io/exporters.js (per-tile PNG), src/ui/textures.js (udimGrid labels), src/ui/main.js (Pack panel)`

**What:** The packing controls production users rely on beyond 'pack everything into 0-1'. Keep some islands fixed; pack into a sub-rectangle or original bounds; treat intentionally overlapping islands as one; keep groups (materials, stacks) together or on separate tiles; and pack at a fixed texel density across as many UDIM tiles as needed.

**Why:** The plan's bitmap packer supports one 0-1 atlas with a global scale search only. It cannot preserve hand-placed islands, trim-sheet regions, material groups or UDIM workflows. It also breaks intentional stacks by separating them. All of this reuses the planned BitImage/canBlit machinery at small incremental cost.

**Sketch:** All items extend the pack.js bitmap packer. (1) Locked islands: before placing movable charts, rasterise locked charts at their current transform (texel coords = uv*R + margin), padded, and blit them into the atlas; rebuild the top profile from atlas columns. Locks create overhangs, so the bottom-left profile y0 can sit above free holes. When locks exist, run the hole scan for every chart: for candidate x on the step grid, scan y upward in steps of max(1, h/4) with canBlit, then refine downward 1 texel at a time. (2) Pack To: a generic region rect [x0,x1)x[y0,y1) in UV units, with atlas bits outside it pre-set to 1. 'Original bounding box' = the selection's bbox before packing. 'Closest UDIM' groups islands by floor(centroid) tile and packs each tile separately. 'Active UDIM' = a region of one tile. (3) Merge Overlapping: AND the un-padded rasters of chart pairs from metrics.bijectivity.overlappingPairs (avoids an O(n^2) scan). Union-find overlapping charts and pack each component as one super-island whose raster is the OR of its members. (4) Groups (by material, stack, or manual tag): 'Together' packs each group into a sub-atlas first (side = sqrt(1.3*sum of raster areas)), then packs group images as super-islands. 'Separate tiles' sends group g to tile 1001+g. (5) Fixed-density UDIM: with D fixed from the TD target, skip the secant search. Keep tiles[] of BitImage(R+2p). For each chart in perimeter-descending order, try tiles in order with extent capped at R (first fit), else open a new tile. Tile t maps to (tu, tv) = (t mod 10, floor(t/10)), UDIM number = 1001 + tu + 10*tv, and UV offset (tu, tv). Charts bigger than a tile at D are scaled down individually with a note. Alternative 'N tiles' mode: secant on D until everything fits in N tiles. (6) Rotation Method: Cardinal = rotations 4, Axis-aligned = orientToAxis with rotations 2, Any = additionally try k*15deg for the 32 largest charts. Shape Method: rasterise triangles (Exact), the convex hull polygon (Convex), or the rect (Bounding Box). (7) Margin Method: convert Fraction (of final atlas), Add (UV units) or Scaled (relative to island scale) to paddingTexels up front. Exporters: OBJ vt keeps integer tile offsets; UV PNG export writes one image per tile (name.1001.png). UI: Pack panel with a Pack To dropdown plus region fields, Lock toggle, group-by dropdown (none/material/stack/manual), and a tile count readout.

**Risks:** Hole scanning with many locks can be slow at R=4096, so cap candidates and use the witness-word test. Sub-atlas group packing is less efficient than joint packing. Some DCCs mishandle UDIM tiles with negative or >10 u offsets, so validate. 'Any' rotation needs re-rasterisation per angle (a cost to budget in searchMs).

**Sources:** Blender Manual, Pack Islands: Shape Method (Exact Shape (Concave) / Boundary Shape (Convex) / Bounding Box), Rotation Method (Any / Axis-aligned / Cardinal), Margin Method (Scaled / Add / Fraction), Lock Pinned Islands, Merge Overlapping, Pack To (Closest UDIM / Active UDIM / Original bounding box / Custom Region) (RST): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/uv/editing.rst; Blender 5.0 release notes ('pack to custom region'): https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/5.0/modeling/; UVPackmaster 3 Track Groups / Stack Groups / lock groups: https://uvpackmaster.com/doc3/blender/latest/20-packing-functionalities/57-track-groups/; xatlas (multiple atlases when resolution is fixed; PackOptions): https://github.com/jpcy/xatlas

### F67. GPU ID-buffer picking with 3D-UV sync selection and selection operators

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/ui/picking.js (ID buffer, box/lasso), src/ui/app.js (faceSel, sync highlighting), src/ui/uv-view.js (grid hit-test, selection fill), src/ui/seam-tool.js (use picker), new src/core/selection.js (linked/grow/shrink/by-metric ops), src/core/engine.js`

**What:** Selection that is shared live between the 3D viewport and the UV editor. Pick faces, edges, vertices or islands in either view, see them highlighted in the other, and use standard selection operators (linked, grow/shrink, overlap, flipped, similar, by distortion). Every professional UV editor is selection-driven; the plan has only chart hover/select in UV and a raycast seam click in 3D.

**Why:** Three.js r141 Raycaster is an O(F) walk with no BVH, so hover and brush picking stutter on 100k-face meshes. An ID-buffer pick is O(1) on the CPU (one 1x1 readPixels), which enables hover highlighting, seam brushes and box select at 60 fps. Sync selection is also what makes 'unwrap selected', 'seams from selection', pins and TD operations usable.

**Sketch:** Picking: create a second THREE.Mesh sharing the position attribute, with a per-corner Uint8 colour attribute encoding id = face + 1 (r = id & 255, g = (id >> 8) & 255, b = (id >> 16) & 255, enough for 16.7M faces). Material: MeshBasicMaterial({ vertexColors: true, toneMapped: false }). Render into a 1x1 WebGLRenderTarget using camera.setViewOffset(W, H, x, y, 1, 1) (clear it afterwards), then renderer.readRenderTargetPixels(rt, 0, 0, 1, 1, buf); face = r + (g << 8) + (b << 16) - 1. Edge and vertex from the face hit: intersect the camera ray with that single triangle to get barycentrics. The nearest edge is opposite the smallest barycentric and the nearest vertex is the largest one (replaces the seam tool's three segment-distance tests). Visible-only box select: render the ID buffer at canvas resolution on drag end, read the rect, and collect unique ids. X-ray box/lasso: project face centroids with the view-projection matrix in a typed loop and test the rect or lasso polygon (even-odd rule). UV side: a uniform grid over UV triangles (cell about 2*sqrt(sum A_uv / F)) for point hit tests. Sync: one faceSel: Uint8Array(F) owned by app.js (plus cornerSel for split UV vertices when sync is off). Selecting in either view updates both: 3D tints selected faces by updating a colour attribute range, and the UV view fills selected triangles. Hovering a face in 3D outlines its island in UV and vice versa; store select mode, sticky and sync in undo snapshots as Blender 5.0 does. Selection operators, each an O(F) typed pass in core: select linked island (L), grow/shrink by adjacency (Ctrl +/-), select overlapping (bijectivity pairs), select flipped, select by distortion (faceSD above the p-th percentile), select similar islands, invert. Actions: 'Seams from selection boundary' marks cut edges between selected and unselected faces; 'Unwrap selected' re-runs the pipeline on the selection with other islands locked in packing. Contract: UVView.setSelection(faceMask), event 'selectfaces'; engine.markSeamsFromFaceSelection(mask), engine.unwrapFaces(mask, opts).

**Risks:** The render target must avoid MSAA and colour management; r141 applies outputEncoding only to the canvas, which should be verified. Rendering the ID pass needs clipping planes and turntable state kept in sync. Mobile GPUs can be slow on readPixels, so throttle hover picks to one per animation frame. Blender's 5.0 sync-select rework details were only seen as a search snippet.

**Sources:** Blender Manual (5.2), Selecting UVs (sync selection, select modes, sticky): https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/uv/selecting.html; Blender 5.0 release notes (undo now stores UV select mode, sticky and sync-select): https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/5.0/modeling/; three.js GPU picking example webgl_interactive_cubes_gpu (from knowledge): https://threejs.org/examples/webgl_interactive_cubes_gpu.html

### F68. Seam control painting (Attract / Protect / Erase) with curvature and visibility seam priors

*Impact* medium · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/core/segmentation.js (paint-weighted cost, relaxed angle), src/core/chart.js (weighted Dijkstra in cutToDisk), src/core/engine.js (setPaint), src/ui/seam-tool.js (brush modes), new src/ui/visibility.js (multi-view occlusion bake), src/io/exporters.js (project save)`

**What:** Instead of clicking exact seam edges, the user paints rough regions where seams should go (Attract) or never go (Protect, for example the face or a logo), and the automatic unwrap follows the guidance. Automatic priors can pre-fill that paint: sharp convex edges for hard surface, concave creases or occluded areas for organic models.

**Why:** The plan offers either fully automatic seams or edge-by-edge manual seams. Painting is much faster than edge clicking and keeps the auto-segmenter useful. ZBrush states Attract cannot help on tunnels or handles; routing cutToDisk's Dijkstra through the paint field fixes exactly that case. Seams placed in hidden regions directly improve perceived quality, which no current metric captures.

**Sketch:** Paint buffer: paint: Float32Array(F) in [-1, 1] (-1 Protect, +1 Attract), optional density w_f, stored in projects. Brush: take the hit point p from the ID buffer, with world radius r (default 5% of bbox diagonal). Collect faces by BFS over adjacency while |centroid - p| < r. Per dab: paint_f += s*(1 - smoothstep(0.6r, r, d))*sign, with s = 0.25, clamped to [-1, 1]. Display via per-face vertex colours (exact on a non-indexed mesh). Segmentation (xatlas-style growth cost): with a_e = (paint_f + paint_g)/2 for the crossed edge, cost += wAttract*max(a_e, 0) - wProtect*max(-a_e, 0), with wAttract = 4 and wProtect = 2 (total cost clamped >= 0). In protected regions, relax the hard angle limit: cosLimit = cos(min(89deg, angleDeg*(1 + max(-a_e, 0)))). Small-chart merging prefers removing boundaries inside protected paint. cutToDisk: Dijkstra edge weight = l_e*exp(beta*(protect_e - attract_e)) with beta = 2.5, so topology cuts route through attract areas and avoid protected ones, including on handles. Priors via 'Auto paint' presets: hard surface: attract = clamp((dihedral - 30deg)/60deg, 0, 1) on convex edges; organic: attract on concave creases (negative signed dihedral). Optional visibility prior, computed on the UI thread: render 32-64 orthographic ID buffers from Fibonacci-sphere directions at 256-512 px and count how many views see each face. Occlusion o_f = 1 - visibleViews/views; attract += 0.5*o_f, so seams prefer armpits and undersides. Add a metric seamVisibility = sum over seam edges of l_e*(1 - o_e) / seam length, reported next to seamNorm. UI: seam tool gets brush modes Attract / Protect / Erase / Density with radius (F-drag) and strength sliders, plus an 'Auto paint from curvature / visibility' button; unwrap uses the paint automatically.

**Risks:** Weights interact with the xatlas cost scale, so constants need tuning on fixtures (angle-limit relaxation can create high-distortion charts that SLIM must absorb). The visibility bake must run on the UI thread (WebGL) and be cached per mesh. ZBrush feature details were confirmed only via search snippets of the Maxon help page, and the Houdini Labs Auto UV page was not fetched directly.

**Sources:** ZBrush UV Master, Control Painting: Protect (red), Attract (blue), Erase, Density; Attract has no effect on tunnels/handles (Maxon help): https://help.maxon.net/zbr/en-us/Content/html/user-guide/zbrush-plugins/uv-master/uv-master.html; SideFX Labs Auto UV (UV Autoseam; bias seam placement toward convex regions for hard surface; curvature sensitivity): https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/labs--autouv.html; SideFX UV Autoseam SOP: https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/uvautoseam.html; xatlas chart growing cost (basis of planned segmentation): https://github.com/jpcy/xatlas

### F69. Interactive cut / weld (Stitch, Rip) with incremental local re-unfold

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/core/engine.js (incremental chart table, partial results), src/core/chart.js (local rebuild helpers), src/core/pack.js (insert with locks), src/core/worker-main.js + src/client/engine-client.js (partial-result protocol), src/ui/seam-tool.js + src/ui/uv-view.js (weld/rip commands)`

**What:** RizomUV's signature workflow: each seam cut or weld immediately re-unfolds only the affected islands, starting from their current state, so the user sees the island open up. Blender provides Stitch (weld UVs sharing vertices), Rip and Split as explicit operators.

**Why:** In the plan, toggleSeamEdge only updates the overlay and a full unwrap re-runs segmentation, topology, flattening, optimisation and packing (targeted at about 1.5 s for 7.7k faces), and the whole layout can reshuffle. Local warm-started updates touch one or two charts, keep every other island where it is, and should take tens of milliseconds for typical charts (estimate), which turns seam editing into an interactive loop.

**Sketch:** Keep per-chart locals alive in the engine and treat edits as local updates. Cut edge e between faces f,g in the same chart c: (1) rebuild the local with the new cut. Vertices along the new seam are split but keep their current UVs, so the layout stays flip-free and is a valid SLIM start. (2) Run connectedComponents on the chart's faces without crossing cuts; if the cut separates it, create two charts. (3) If the result is not a disk, run cutToDisk on that chart only. (4) Run warm-start SLIM for 6-10 iterations with Anderson; new seams relax outward immediately. (5) Placement: other charts keep their transforms. Changed charts are rescaled to the global density and inserted with the lock-aware packer (others locked), or left in place with overlap highlighted; full repack stays one click away. Weld/Stitch between charts a and b: collect shared welded vertices along e and its contiguous cut chain. Procrustes-align b onto a using those vertex pairs (closed-form theta, s, t), weld duplicates at the midpoint, and rebuild the merged local. If the merge has flips, run 3 ARAP iterations with the far side pinned, then SLIM; if still flipped, re-initialise the merged chart with LSCM. Stitch 'Limit Distance' = only weld pairs closer than d in UV. Rip = mark the selected edge chain cut and apply the cut procedure. Scheduling: queue edits and coalesce clicks within 50 ms; the worker always processes the newest cut state. Undo stores only touched charts (local.uv, transform, cut diff) rather than a full Snapshot, keeping memory O(chart) per edit. New engine ops: cutEdges(edgeIds, {live:true}), weldEdges(edgeIds, {limitDistance}), each returning a partial result {changedCharts, removedCharts, uvPatches}.

**Risks:** Chart ids change under incremental edits, so UI selections and stacks must be remapped. Accumulated local edits can drift from what a fresh unwrap would produce (offer 'Recompute all'). RizomUV's internal update strategy is not documented; the latency figures are estimates.

**Sources:** Blender Manual: Stitch (join selected UVs that share vertices, Limit Distance), Rip, Split (RST): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/uv/editing.rst; RizomUV (interactive cut/weld with live unfold), product changelog: https://www.rizomuv.com/changelog/; Rabinovich et al. 2017 SLIM (warm-start local/global): https://doi.org/10.1145/2983621

### F70. Existing-UV workflow: island extraction from imported UVs, analyse / relax-in-place / repack, lightmap UV2 generation

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `src/core/engine.js (setSourceUV, uvSource modes, lightmap preset), src/core/mesh.js or new src/core/uvislands.js (continuity-based cut), src/io/loaders.js (keep originalUV per corner), src/io/exporters.js (TEXCOORD_1), src/ui/main.js (presets, A/B)`

**What:** Many users arrive with models that already have UVs and want to check them, fix only the bad parts, repack them, or make a second, non-overlapping lightmap channel from them, as game engines do. The loader contract already returns originalUV, but the engine plan never uses it.

**Why:** The plan always discards existing UVs and re-segments from scratch, which is unacceptable when textures already exist. Island extraction reuses the planned cut-flag contract (about a 100-line pass), so every planned metric, SLIM and packer path immediately works on imported layouts. A UV2 lightmap mode serves the Unity/Unreal/three.js lightMap audience, and adds a second-channel export that v3 lacks.

**Sketch:** (1) Islands from imported UVs (Blender 'Seams from Islands'): for each welded edge shared by faces f and g, the edge is UV-continuous if both endpoints' corner UVs match (|uv_f - uv_g|inf < 1e-6); otherwise set cut[e] = 1. faceChart = connected components over non-cut edges, so buildChartLocal and computeMetrics work unchanged. (2) Analyse: compute Metrics for the original UV (flips, overlap, stretch, TD CV, coverage, bleed mip) and show them in the A/B table against a fresh unwrap. (3) Keep-island operations. 'Relax in place': SLIM starting from each chart's imported UV if it is flip-free, else Tutte; then map back with a Procrustes transform onto the original island so it does not jump. 'Repack only': keep scale or equalise TD. 'Fix only invalid charts': relax or re-unwrap only charts flagged by bijectivity or flips. Stacked islands (from similarity detection) stay stacked for UV0. (4) Lightmap UV2 preset: start from UV0 islands; split additionally at edges with dihedral > hardAngle (default 88deg); unstack overlapping or stacked islands (lightmaps need unique texels). Run SLIM and split charts whose angle error exceeds 8% or area error exceeds 15% using segmentation. Pack with rotations 4, blockAlign on, bilinear +1, and padding derived from the smallest lightmap resolution R_min: paddingTexels_at_R = ceil(p_min*R/R_min) with p_min = 2 texels. (5) Export: GLB with TEXCOORD_0 (original) and TEXCOORD_1 (generated) via a BufferGeometry 'uv2' attribute; OBJ holds one channel, so warn. Contract: engine.setSourceUV(uv) builds the cut from continuity; UnwrapOpts.uvSource = 'generate' | 'imported'; opts.channel = 0 | 1; a UI switch 'UV source: generate / keep imported' plus a Lightmap (UV2) preset.

**Risks:** Imported UVs can be non-manifold in UV (the same corner used twice, zero-area UV faces), which needs sanitising. Unity and Unreal defaults (88deg, 8%, 15%) are from memory and were not re-verified this session. Three.js r141 GLTFExporter uses 'uv2' for TEXCOORD_1, which should be confirmed on the vendored build.

**Sources:** Unreal Engine docs, Unwrapping UVs for Lightmaps (Generate Lightmap UVs, Source/Destination lightmap index, Min Lightmap Resolution) (from knowledge, not fetched): https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/unwrapping-uvs-for-lightmaps-in-unreal-engine; Unity Manual, Generating Lightmap UVs (Hard Angle, Angle Error, Area Error, Pack Margin) (from knowledge, not fetched): https://docs.unity3d.com/Manual/LightingGiUvs-GeneratingLightmappingUVs.html; Blender Manual, Seams from Islands / Lightmap Pack (Share Texture Space, Pack Quality, Margin) (RST): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/editing/uv.rst; UVPackmaster 4, Split Overlapping Islands: https://uvpackmaster.com/doc4/blender/latest/35-aligning-functionalities/

### F71. Mip-bleed padding analyser + mip-level / texel-footprint viewport shader

*Impact* medium · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/core/bake.js (or metrics.js section): rasterised id map, distance transform, mip mixing; src/ui/textures.js (bleed-test texture, mipmapped grids); new ShaderMaterial in src/ui/app.js (mip-level heat mode); src/ui/main.js (bake-readiness row)`

**What:** A validation feature I did not find in the surveyed tools' docs as an exact metric. It simulates baker dilation and the mip chain to report the mip level at which neighbouring islands start bleeding into each other, per chart, and recommends padding. A viewport shader colours the model by the mip level the GPU actually samples. This relates to Unreal's texture-resolution view modes, but I did not verify those.

**Why:** The plan checks 'padding vs resolution' with a rule of thumb. This computes the exact safe mip level from the actual layout (for example 'safe to mip 3 = 256 px on a 2048 map'). It turns the most common real-world UV complaint (seam bleeding at distance) into a measurable, fixable number, with a Fix button that sets paddingTexels = 2^k.

**Sketch:** Core, in metrics.js or a new src/core/bake.js. (1) Rasterise un-padded chart ids into Int32Array(R^2) at the target resolution with the pack.js conservative rasteriser. (2) Simulate baker dilation: a two-pass (forward/backward) Chebyshev distance transform that also propagates the nearest chart id, stopped at padding p texels, giving a dilated id map. (3) For mip m = 1..log2(R), with block size b = 2^m: per block store the first non-empty id and a mixed flag (two or more distinct non-empty ids). Compute level m from level m-1: mixed if any child is mixed or children hold different non-empty ids. Total cost O(4/3 R^2) with Int32Array per level. (4) Bilinear/trilinear footprint: chart c is clean at level m if no mixed block lies in the 3x3 block neighbourhood of any block containing an un-dilated texel of c. maxCleanMip_c is the largest such m, found in one pass by propagating per block the minimum level at which it becomes dirty. Report metrics.bleed = { perChart: Uint8Array, safeMip: min over charts, safeMipPx: R >> safeMip, recommendedPadding: 2^targetMip }. Also flag island pairs closer than 2p texels. (5) Viewport shader (three r141 ShaderMaterial, extensions: { derivatives: true }): lambda = log2(max(length(dFdx(vUv*R)), length(dFdy(vUv*R)))). Colour lambda < -1 red (magnified, too few texels on screen), -1..+1 green (about 1 texel per pixel), and lambda > 1 as one blue step per level. Add an R slider and an optional fixed reference distance. (6) Texture modes: build the checker and colour grid with full mipmaps (LinearMipmapLinearFilter, max anisotropy) and add a 'Bleed test' texture that fills each island with a saturated unique colour, dilated by p, so bleeding shows when zooming out. UI: a bake-readiness row 'Padding safe to mip k (N px)', a target-mip dropdown, and a Fix button that sets paddingTexels = 2^targetMip and repacks.

**Risks:** Real bakers differ (some dilate unlimited, some use edge padding), so the dilation mode should be a parameter. Block-mixing is a conservative bleed model (it ignores anisotropic filtering and non-power-of-two maps). At R=8192 the id map is 256 MB of Int32, so cap analysis at 4096 or use Int16 ids.

**Sources:** xatlas padding / bilinear options (context for margins): https://github.com/jpcy/xatlas; Blender Manual, Pack Islands Margin Method (Scaled / Add / Fraction) (RST): https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender-manual/raw/branch/main/manual/modeling/meshes/uv/editing.rst; OpenGL/WebGL mip level selection, lambda = log2(rho) with rho from UV derivatives (OpenGL ES 3.0 spec section 3.8.10, from knowledge): https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf

### F72. Flatten from Points: geodesic polar (log-map) islands on geodesic Voronoi cells with distortion-driven source insertion

*Impact* medium · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* medium · verified · *Target* `new src/core/geodesic.js (multi-source Dijkstra, DEM, optional VHM log map), src/core/engine.js (mode 'points'), src/ui/seam-tool.js or picking.js (source point placement), src/ui/main.js (mode UI)`

**What:** A point-driven unwrap: the user clicks island centres on the surface (or they are sampled automatically). Each vertex joins the nearest source's geodesic Voronoi cell and gets polar coordinates (distance and direction) around that source. This is how Houdini's UV Flatten from Points works; its automatic mode adds sources farthest from existing ones or where distortion was too high.

**Why:** It is an intuitive art-direction control ('one island centred here, V pointing along this direction') for decals, organic blobs and scanned or photogrammetry surfaces, which neither v3 nor the plan offers. The log map is radially undistorted, so it is a near-isometric start for SLIM on round patches (fewer SLIM iterations than a Tutte disk start). Distortion-driven source insertion is also a principled automatic segmentation for smooth organic meshes where normal-deviation growth over-segments.

**Sketch:** Sources: user clicks (ID-buffer pick, snapped to the nearest welded vertex) or automatic selection. Furthest Point Sampling: start at the vertex farthest from the area centroid, then repeatedly add the vertex with the largest geodesic distance to existing sources, using multi-source Dijkstra on welded edges (MinHeap from math.js, O(k E log V)). Distortion Based: after an unwrap, add a source at a vertex of the max-SD face in each chart whose sdP99 exceeds the threshold (default 1.5), repeating up to Additional Points (default 0, max 64). Islands: geodesic Voronoi from multi-source Dijkstra with 3D edge lengths. Each face goes to the source that owns the majority of its vertices (ties go to the lowest distance). Cells that are not disks go through cutToDisk; Voronoi boundary edges become cut flags (the Houdini 'Seams Group'). UV per cell, Fast mode = Discrete Exponential Map: process vertices in Dijkstra order. For vertex q reached from processed neighbour p: u_q = u_p + F_p(q - p), where F_p projects the offset onto p's tangent plane and expresses it in p's frame. p's frame is the source frame carried along the path by minimal rotations that align consecutive normals. Average over up to 3 processed neighbours weighted by 1/|q - p|. Accurate mode = Vector Heat Method log map: solve a vector heat flow with the complex connection Laplacian (split into a 2n x 2n real SPD system, solved with solvers.pcg) at t = h^2 (h = mean edge length), plus scalar heat for the radial term, with UV = r*(cos phi, sin phi). Orientation: rotate each island so an optional 3D direction maps to +V (reuse Align Rotation Geometry). Scale: optional per-source uniform scale multiplier (maps to the TD multiplier). Finish with SLIM from the log-map start if it is flip-free, otherwise from Tutte, then pack. Engine op: unwrap({ mode: 'points', sources: Int32Array, method: 'fast' | 'accurate', autoSources: { method: 'fps' | 'distortion', count, threshold } }).

**Risks:** Dijkstra distances on edges are metrically biased (zig-zag on irregular meshes), so Fast mode shows directional artefacts; mitigate with the SLIM polish. The log map can flip past the cut locus of large cells, so keep cells small or fall back to Tutte. VHM needs a correct complex connection Laplacian, which is subtle to implement and test (take formulas from the paper). Houdini's exact internal algorithm is not documented beyond 'geodesic normal coordinates', so the DEM/VHM choice is mine.

**Sources:** SideFX Houdini, UV Flatten from Points SOP (geodesic normal coordinates / surface log map; Voronoi-cell islands; Method Fast (default) / Accurate / Only Seams; Point Selection Method Furthest Point Sampling / Distortion Based; Additional Points; Distortion Threshold; V Direction Attribute; Uniform Scale Attribute; Seams Group; Island ID): https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/uvflattenfrompoints.html; Schmidt, Grimm, Wyvill 2006, Interactive Decal Compositing with Discrete Exponential Maps, SIGGRAPH (from knowledge): https://doi.org/10.1145/1141911.1141930; Sharp, Soliman, Crane 2019, The Vector Heat Method, ACM TOG (log map) (from knowledge): https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~kmcrane/Projects/VectorHeatMethod/
