## Texture-space features in the browser: texture transfer and re-baking onto the new atlas, UV-space map baking (position, normals, tangent-space normals, AO, curvature, ID, texel density), seam-aware dilation (JFA, push-pull, adjacent-faces, seam erasure), PNG16/EXR export, and glTF material round-trip on three.js r141 without a build step

### F73. GPU UV-space texture transfer (re-bake old-UV textures onto the new atlas), with sRGB-correct filtering, supersampling, tiling and material merging

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `new src/gpu/baker.js (UVApp.GPUBaker); CPU fallback in new src/core/texops.js (transferTexture, rasterizeUV); src/io/loaders.js must additionally return materials/maps/faceMaterial`

**What:** Render the mesh into the new atlas with a vertex shader that places each vertex at its NEW uv (gl_Position = vec4(uvNew*2-1,0,1)) while passing its OLD uv as a varying; the fragment shader samples the original texture at the old uv. Because the mesh topology is unchanged by unwrapping, there is an exact per-triangle correspondence, so no ray casting or cage is needed. The GPU's implicit mip LOD (derivatives of vOld in atlas raster space) and anisotropic filtering prefilter the source correctly when the new atlas is smaller than the old one.

**Why:** Neither v3 nor the v4 plan can keep an imported model's textures. v4 loaders return positions plus originalUV only, and exporters write geometry only, so re-unwrapping a textured asset destroys its look. This makes the toolkit usable on real assets: re-atlas, merge N materials into 1 texture set (fewer draw calls), change resolution. It is GPU-fast (a 4K atlas plus 2x supersampling is a few draw calls, well under 1 s) and has a pure-JS fallback for worker/node tests.

**Sketch:** New classic script src/gpu/baker.js (UVApp.GPUBaker). Build a non-indexed BufferGeometry with attributes position (real 3D, required by three for draw count), uvNew, uvOld (and uvOld2 for TEXCOORD_1 maps). Set mesh.frustumCulled=false, material.side=THREE.DoubleSide (mirrored/flipped UV triangles have reversed winding and must still rasterize), depthTest=false, depthWrite=false, blending NoBlending. Use RawShaderMaterial (GLSL ES 1.0, so WebGL1 works). VS: `attribute vec3 position; attribute vec2 uvNew, uvOld; uniform mat3 srcUvXform; uniform vec4 tile; varying vec2 vOld; void main(){ vOld=(srcUvXform*vec3(uvOld,1.)).xy; vec2 p=uvNew*tile.xy+tile.zw; gl_Position=vec4(p*2.-1.,0.,1.); }`. srcUvXform = source texture.matrix after texture.updateMatrix() (this is where KHR_texture_transform offset/repeat/rotation lands in r141). FS: `precision highp float; uniform sampler2D src; uniform vec4 factor; varying vec2 vOld; void main(){ gl_FragColor = texture2D(src,vOld)*factor; }`. Coverage mask: second pass with the same VS and gl_FragColor=vec4(1) into an RGBA8 target (or MRT via WebGLMultipleRenderTargets on WebGL2). Color space: on WebGL2, r141 uploads sRGBEncoding RGBA8 textures as SRGB8_ALPHA8, so sampling returns LINEAR values; give the render target texture encoding=THREE.sRGBEncoding too, so it is also SRGB8_ALPHA8 and the GPU re-encodes on write, and blending/MSAA resolve happen in linear. On WebGL1 do manual decode/encode in the shader (exact piecewise sRGB). Keep normal/ORM maps LinearEncoding. Supersampling: render at S x R (S=2 default, 4 'high'), then coverage-weighted box downsample: c = sum(rgb_i*m_i)/sum(m_i), coverage = sum(m_i)/S^2, done in linear space and re-encoded. Set src.anisotropy = renderer.capabilities.getMaxAnisotropy() and minFilter LinearMipmapLinear. Preserve the source wrapS/wrapT (tiled UVs outside [0,1]); r141 auto-resizes NPOT to POT on WebGL1 when needed. Sub-texel slivers: first draw the triangle edges as THREE.LineSegments with the same material, then draw the filled triangles over them (a cheap conservative-coverage approximation); any remaining holes are handled by dilation. Large atlases: if S*R > capabilities.maxTextureSize, split into T x T tiles with tile=(T,T,-tx,-ty). Integer pixel tile boundaries rasterize consistently. Read each tile with renderer.readRenderTargetPixels and stitch. Row convention: framebuffer row 0 is v in [0,1/H). For glTF (flipY=false) image row y maps to v=y/H, so write rows unflipped; for three/OBJ (flipY=true) flip rows. Keep one `vDown` flag per project. Material merge: for every material group, draw its faces with its own src/factor into the same atlas. baseColor = decode(tex)*baseColorFactor. ORM target: R = 1+strength*(ao-1), G = roughTex.g*roughnessFactor, B = metalTex.b*metallicFactor. Emissive = tex*emissiveFactor. A missing map becomes a 1x1 white texture times the factor. Output factors are then 1. Auto resolution ('Match source detail'): for each face, r_f = sqrt(A_oldUV,f/A_newUV,f); R_new = nextPow2(R_old * areaWeightedPercentile95(r_f)), capped at maxTextureSize/S; also report the area fraction whose new density >= old. CPU fallback in pure JS (src/core/texops.js transferTexture): edge-function rasterizer at texel centers (x+0.5)/W with the top-left rule; per-triangle affine map J = d(oldUV*srcRes)/d(newUV*dstRes); lod = log2(sigma_max(J)) via math.svd2; trilinear sample from a box mip pyramid of the source ImageData passed in as a Uint8ClampedArray.

**Risks:** sRGB handling differs between WebGL1 (shader decode) and WebGL2 (SRGB8_ALPHA8), so it needs a test that bakes a gradient on both paths and checks equal bytes within +/-1. Alpha-tested textures: coverage and texture alpha must be kept separate. readPixels of 8K RGBA is 256 MB, so tile and stream. Implicit LOD is wrong at chart silhouettes (derivatives across triangle edges), a minor effect fixed by dilation. The LineSegments conservative trick is approximate; true conservative rasterization needs CPU-side triangle expansion. Timings were not measured in this session.

**Sources:** three.js r141 examples/jsm/misc/ProgressiveLightMap.js (UV-space render: gl_Position = vec4((uv2-0.5)*2.0,1.0,1.0); ping-pong float targets) https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/r141/examples/jsm/misc/ProgressiveLightMap.js; Local vendor/three/three.min.js r141 internal-format selection: RGBA+UnsignedByte+sRGBEncoding -> 35907 (SRGB8_ALPHA8) on WebGL2 (read in this session); Local vendor/three/GLTFLoader.js r141: texture.flipY=false (l.2733), baseColor/emissive assigned sRGBEncoding (l.533, 3124), custom texCoord warning (l.2853); Hasselgren, Akenine-Moller, Ohlsson 2005, Conservative Rasterization, GPU Gems 2 ch.42 https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems2/part-v-image-oriented-computing/chapter-42-conservative-rasterization

### F74. Jump Flood Algorithm dilation (GPU) with exact Euclidean feature transform, push-pull gutter fill and a mip-bleed safety analyzer (CPU)

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/gpu/dilate.js; new src/core/texops.js (featureTransform, pushPull, mipBleedReport); bake-readiness checks in src/ui/main.js`

**What:** Gutter/margin generation for every baked map. JFA computes, for every empty texel, the nearest covered texel in O(log N) full-screen passes. The resolve pass copies that texel's value within `padding` px (Blender's 'Extend'). Push-pull fills all remaining texels with a smooth multiresolution extrapolation, so no black background can bleed into any mip level. A CPU analyzer computes, per mip level, how many texels of each chart get contaminated by other charts and reports the highest safe mip level for the current packing padding.

**Why:** The v4 plan has 'padding vs resolution' only as a bake-readiness toast heuristic, and v3 has nothing. This adds actual dilation (needed by every texture feature) and an exact, testable answer to 'how many mips are safe with this padding?'. GPU JFA dilation of a 4K map with 16 px margin is about 6 passes; exact CPU EDT at 4096^2 is linear time (roughly 1 s in JS, not measured).

**Sketch:** GPU (src/gpu/dilate.js). Seed target: RGBA8 (renderable everywhere) encoding the texel coordinates exactly: R,G = x high/low byte, B,A = y high/low byte, 0xFFFF = no seed. Init pass: covered ? encode(gl_FragCoord.xy-0.5) : none. Decode in GLSL ES 1.0: x = floor(r*255.+.5)*256.+floor(g*255.+.5). Use NEAREST filtering and sample at texel centers. Jump pass (ping-pong two RGBA8 RTs), uniform k: for (i,j) in {-1,0,1}^2, s = decode(texture2D(seeds,(gl_FragCoord.xy+vec2(i,j)*k)/res)); if valid, d = dot(s-fc,s-fc); keep the argmin. Schedule for bounded margin p: k = nextPow2(p), k/2, ..., 1, then one extra k=1 pass ('JFA+1', which reduces JFA errors). For full-texture Voronoi fill start at k = N/2 (12 passes at 4096). Resolve: covered ? color : (dist <= p ? texture2D(color,(s+.5)/res) : fallback). For ID/label maps always copy (never interpolate). CPU exact version (src/core/texops.js featureTransform(mask,w,h) -> Int32Array nearest index): Felzenszwalb-Huttenlocher 1D lower-envelope squared EDT on columns then rows, carrying the argmin site index (store v[] parabola sites and z[] boundaries; f(q)=0 for seeds, +INF otherwise). This is O(wh) and is the reference in node tests (compare with brute force on 64x64 random masks). Push-pull (fill beyond margin): pull: w_{l+1} = min(1, sum of 2x2 child w), c_{l+1} = sum(w*c)/sum(w) (skip if sum(w)=0). Push: c_l = w_l*c_l + (1-w_l)*bilinearUpsample(c_{l+1}), with w=coverage (0/1 at level 0, fractional after supersampling). On GPU it is a mip chain of RTs; on CPU about 1.33*N^2 operations. Mip safety analyzer (src/core/texops.js mipBleedReport(labels,w,h,levels)): labels = Voronoi chart id per texel after dilation (from the feature transform). For level L build label pyramid lab_L(x,y) = unique id if the 2^L x 2^L footprint has one label, else -2 (mixed). For each chart A and each covered texel of A, take the 2x2 bilinear footprint at level L (coordinates (x+.5)/2^L-.5). It is contaminated if any footprint texel is -2 or != A. Report contamination fraction per level and safeLevel = max L with fraction < 0.1%. Worst-case analytic bound for gap g texels between charts: safe L <= floor(log2 g) - 2 (a 2x2 bilinear footprint at level L spans 2^(L+1) texels and must stay within g/2). Show both in bake-readiness, and suggest packer padding = 2^(Ldesired+2). Parameters: padding default = max(4, packer padding/2) px; margin unlimited for 'push-pull' mode.

**Risks:** Numeric JFA error rates for JFA+1/1+JFA come from the paper's claims and were NOT verified in this session; they are visually harmless for dilation. highp float in fragment shaders is not guaranteed on some old mobile GPUs (mediump breaks coordinate decode above 1024), so fall back to CPU EDT when renderer.capabilities.precision is low. The bilinear-footprint bound assumes box-filtered mips (GPU generateMipmap is usually box, but implementation-defined). The pull-push weights here are a simplified box variant of Gortler et al. The FH and pull-push details are from knowledge, not re-read in this session.

**Sources:** Rong & Tan 2006, Jump flooding in GPU with applications to Voronoi diagram and distance transform, I3D '06 https://doi.org/10.1145/1111411.1111431 ; project page https://www.comp.nus.edu.sg/~tants/jfa.html; Felzenszwalb & Huttenlocher 2012, Distance Transforms of Sampled Functions, Theory of Computing 8:415-428 https://theoryofcomputing.org/articles/v008a019/; Gortler, Grzeszczuk, Szeliski, Cohen 1996, The Lumigraph (pull-push hole filling) https://doi.org/10.1145/237170.237200; Blender manual, Render Baking - margin (Extend) https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/cycles/baking.html

### F75. Seam-aware 'Adjacent Faces' margin: fill gutter texels with texture continued from the neighbouring face across the UV seam (Blender 3.1+ default)

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/core/texops.js (adjacentFacesMargin), called from the bake pipeline after GPU readback; uses mesh.js adjacency`

**What:** Instead of smearing boundary texels outward, each empty gutter pixel near a chart is mapped across the nearest UV boundary edge into the UV triangle that is adjacent in 3D (the other side of the seam), and the texture is bilinearly sampled there. Bilinear and low-mip filtering at the seam then read texels that genuinely continue the surface signal, which strongly reduces visible seams without changing any interior texel.

**Why:** The v4 plan has no margin generation at all, and plain Extend/JFA (finding 2) still leaves filtered-colour mismatches at every seam. Adjacent Faces gives near-seamless bilinear and mip-1/2 results for free (no optimization), is the production default in Blender, and uses mesh adjacency the kernel already has (mesh.faceEdges, cut flags), so it is a natural pure-JS core feature.

**Sketch:** src/core/texops.js adjacentFacesMargin({img, w, h, ch, uv, mesh, faceIdMap, margin}). (1) Rasterize the triangle-id map Int32Array(w*h) (-1 empty) with the same texel-center/top-left rule as the bake. (2) Dijkstra over empty pixels using an 8-neighbourhood chamfer metric with integer costs 2 (orthogonal) and 3 (diagonal), as in Blender's `distances[8] = {2,3,2,3,...}`. Seed the heap with empty pixels adjacent to covered ones and expand while dist < 2*(margin+1). Store per pixel the direction to its parent so the source face f can be found by following parents. (3) For empty pixel q (texel center in UV units) with source face f: over f's three UV edges (a,b) compute t = clamp(dot(q-a,b-a)/|b-a|^2, 0, 1). Take the closest edge that q is outside of (cross((b-a),(q-a)) sign opposite to the face's orientation). (4) Find the 3D-adjacent face g across that edge: the edge id is mesh.faceEdges[3f+k]; the other incident face comes from the edge->faces adjacency, and its corners holding the same welded vertices give uv a',b' (orientation reversed: the other half-edge runs b->a). Point on the other edge: e' = uv_g(A) + t*(uv_g(B)-uv_g(A)). Offset perpendicular into g by the signed distance d of q from edge (a,b), scaled by |b'-a'|/|b-a| so the extension is isotropic: p' = e' + d*s*nrm_in(g), with nrm_in pointing towards g's third vertex. (5) If p' lies inside g (or anywhere covered in g's chart), set img(q) = bilinear(img, p') (sample only covered texels; renormalize weights by coverage). Otherwise treat the pixel p' lands in as the new start and repeat, up to maxPolygonSteps = 3 as in Blender. (6) Pixels still unassigned use Extend: JFA/feature-transform nearest copy from finding 2. (7) Boundary edges with no 3D neighbour (open borders) also use Extend. Complexity O(P log P) for P gutter pixels. Run in the worker on the readback Float32/Uint8 image. Tangent-space normal maps: apply the 2x2 similarity R (rotation plus mirror sign) that maps edge direction (b-a) to (a'-b') to the sampled xy components before writing, or run the margin on an object-space normal bake and convert afterwards. This avoids the problem that made Blender consider reverting the default to Extend for tangent maps. Tests: a cylinder with one seam and a texture that is linear in 3D angle should give near-zero bilinear mismatch within the margin; compare the seam D_total (finding 4 metric) for Extend vs Adjacent.

**Risks:** Blender reports lookup failures with several adjacent UV edges at roughly 30-60 degrees (issue #95249) and dilation into island interiors in some cases (#119393). Keep the chamfer distance, validate against true Euclidean distance, and always finish with an Extend pass. The perpendicular scaling by edge-length ratio is my recommendation; Blender's exact offset scaling was not fully confirmed from the source summary. Non-manifold edges (more than 2 faces) need a deterministic choice.

**Sources:** Blender source/blender/render/intern/texture_margin.cc (read in this session) https://github.com/blender/blender/blob/main/source/blender/render/intern/texture_margin.cc; Blender commit 449db0ab1e3 'Baking: new method to generate margin, based on adjacent faces' https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender/commit/449db0ab1e3; Blender manual, Render Baking - Margin Type (Adjacent Faces vs Extend) https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/cycles/baking.html; Blender issue #96977 (tangent-space normal maps and margin default) https://projects.blender.org/blender/blender/issues/96977 ; #95249 (45-degree lookup failures) https://developer.blender.org/T95249

### F76. Seam erasure by sparse least squares on texels (Seamless, Liu et al. 2017)

*Impact* medium · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* medium · verified · *Target* `new src/core/seamerase.js (uses solvers.js TripletBuilder/CSR/pcg, mesh.js edges); exposed as engine op 'eraseSeams' in the worker`

**What:** An optional 'Erase seams' post-process for baked/transferred maps. Along every seam edge pair (e1,e2) the bilinear reconstructions from both sides are forced to agree by minimizing the analytically integrated squared difference D = integral over [0,1] of |B(e1(g),p) - B(e2(g),p)|^2 dg = p^T M p. This is quadratic in texel values p. Deviation and smoothness terms pick a pleasant solution from the null space of M. Only texels in bilinear cells touched by seams are variables.

**Why:** This goes beyond both v3/v4 (no seam handling) and beyond margin tricks: after erasure the rendered signal is continuous across seams at the bake resolution (paper Table 1: D_total drops from about 1e-4..3.7 to about 1e-7..1e-11). It is essential for displacement/position maps (cracks) and gives visibly seamless AO/normal/colour. It reuses the existing solvers.js CSR + PCG, so it is feasible in plain JS. The paper's Python/SciPy runtimes were 25-431 s at 256-2048 px. A typed-array JS PCG over only seam texels should be far faster (my estimate, not measured).

**Sketch:** src/core/seamerase.js (after metrics in load order). Texel lattice: node (i,j) sits at uv ((i+.5)/W,(j+.5)/H) (WebGL texel centers), so for a uv point x=u*W-.5, y=v*H-.5, cell=(floor x, floor y), s,t=fract. Seam pairs: for every non-boundary welded edge whose two incident faces have different corner UVs (a cut edge), e1(g)=lerp(uvA_f1,uvB_f1,g), e2(g)=lerp(uvA_f2,uvB_f2,g) with the same 3D endpoints A->B. Collect the g values where e1 or e2 cross integer x or y lattice lines, sort and merge into intervals [g_i,g_i+1]; within each interval both sides stay in fixed cells. Instead of symbolic monomial integrals, use 3-point Gauss-Legendre per interval: the integrand is a degree-4 polynomial, so 3-point GL (exact to degree 5) matches the paper's closed form. At each node g_q build the 8-sparse vector m = [bilinear weights of e1(g_q)] minus [weights of e2(g_q)]; accumulate w_q*(len/2)*m*m^T into a TripletBuilder. Normalize: M_total = (1/L) sum over edges of l_e*M_e (l_e = 3D edge length, L = total). Energy (paper weights): E = w_change*(1/|T_in|)*sum_{i in T_in}|p_i - p0_i|^2 + w_gin*|grad_in p - grad_in p0|^2 + w_C1*E_C1 + w_gout*|grad_out p|^2 + w_seam*p^T M p. Use w_change=1e4 ('local'; 1e2 for 'global' gradient-domain mode), w_gin=w_gout=100, w_C1=100, w_seam=1e10 (paper, direct LU). E_C1 = integral of (grad B(e1)·n1/|e1| + grad B(e2)·n2/|e2|)^2 with interior-pointing perpendiculars; grad B is linear in g, so 2-point GL is enough. grad_in/grad_out are forward differences on lattice edges among inside texels / edges touching outside texels of seam cells. Variables: texels of all bilinear cells touched by seam edges; all other texels are fixed and moved to the RHS. Solve A p = b per channel with the SAME matrix (build the preconditioner once) using solvers.pcg. Because PCG with a 1e10 penalty is ill-conditioned, use w_seam in the 1e6-1e8 range, a block-Jacobi or IC0 preconditioner, warm start p=p0, and stop when D_total(after)/D_total(before) < 1e-4. Report D_total before/after (this also becomes a seam-quality metric in metrics.js). Apply to linear data only (decode sRGB first). For normal maps run on object-space normals (the paper did this, since tangent frames differ across half-edges) and renormalize afterwards.

**Risks:** The paper used a direct sparse LU with w_seam=1e10 (1e6 for the Lemon due to numerics). The lower penalty plus PCG recommended here is my adaptation and may leave small residuals, so validate with D_total. Nullity shrinks at low resolution, so changes spread and colours can shift globally. Tangent-space normal maps are not directly supported. Memory for 4K textures with long seams (about 100k variables times 3 channels) is fine, but assembly must stay in typed arrays.

**Sources:** Liu, Ferguson, Jacobson, Gingold 2017, Seamless: Seam erasure and seam-aware decoupling of shape from mesh resolution, ACM TOG 36(6):216 (paper text read in this session) https://www.cs.toronto.edu/~jacobson/images/seamless-seam-erasure-and-seam-aware-decoupling-of-shape-from-mesh-resolution-siggraph-asia-2017-compressed-liu-et-al.pdf; Project page https://cragl.cs.gmu.edu/seamless/ ; ACM DL https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3130800.3130897; Iwanicki 2013, Lighting Technology of The Last of Us (sample-point seam penalty, cited by Liu et al.)

### F77. Correct tangent-space normal-map transfer and bake with a synced tangent basis (MikkTSpace or JS Lengyel fallback) exported as glTF TANGENT

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/core/tangents.js; transfer shader in src/gpu/baker.js; exporters.exportGLB adds tangent + material maps`

**What:** A tangent-space normal map is expressed in a frame derived from UV gradients. After re-unwrapping, copying texels (finding 1) is WRONG: every chart rotation, mirror or scale changes T/B. The fix is to decode with the old frame into object space, then re-encode with the new frame, per pixel on the GPU. The new tangents are then exported in the glTF so every renderer uses exactly the baking basis.

**Why:** Without this, the headline texture-transfer feature silently breaks normal maps (lighting flips on rotated or mirrored islands, which the packer's rotations make common). Exporting TANGENT removes renderer-dependent MikkTSpace mismatches, and a built-in angular-error self-check turns an invisible failure into a number.

**Sketch:** Tangents (src/core/tangents.js, pure JS): for each triangle, dP1=p1-p0, dP2=p2-p0, (du1,dv1),(du2,dv2); r = 1/(du1*dv2-du2*dv1) (skip if |det|<1e-20); T=(dP1*dv2-dP2*dv1)*r; Bt=(dP2*du1-dP1*du2)*r. Accumulate angle-weighted per key (weldedVertex, chartId), so seams split. Gram-Schmidt T' = normalize(T - N*dot(N,T)), w = sign(dot(cross(N,T'),Bt)). Output Float32Array tangent4 per corner. Optional exact MikkTSpace: vendor three's inline-base64 mikktspace WASM wrapper converted to a classic script (allowed: inlined WASM plus pure-JS fallback); it requires non-indexed input, which matches the kernel's triangle soups. Compute old tangents from uvOld and new tangents from uvNew with the SAME algorithm. Transfer shader (RawShaderMaterial, same UV-space VS as finding 1, plus attributes normal, tanOld(vec4), tanNew(vec4) passed as varyings). FS: `vec3 t = texture2D(srcN,vOld).xyz*2.-1.; t.xy*=normalScale; vec3 N=normalize(vN); vec3 To=normalize(vTo.xyz-N*dot(N,vTo.xyz)); vec3 Bo=cross(N,To)*vTo.w; vec3 o=normalize(To*t.x+Bo*t.y+N*t.z); vec3 Tn=normalize(vTn.xyz-N*dot(N,vTn.xyz)); vec3 Bn=cross(N,Tn)*vTn.w; gl_FragColor=vec4(vec3(dot(o,Tn),dot(o,Bn),dot(o,N))*.5+.5,1.);`. The source texture must be LinearEncoding, and the RT must be linear RGBA8, or RGBA16F/PNG16 for 'high' quality. Downsample by averaging decoded vectors and renormalizing. Dilation copies vectors (Adjacent Faces with 2x2 UV rotation, see finding 3). Export: geometry.setAttribute('tangent', new Float32BufferAttribute(tanNew,4)). r141 GLTFExporter writes TANGENT, and GLTFLoader then does NOT apply its derivative-tangent normalScale.y flip, so viewer and bake agree. Green-channel convention: glTF normal textures are +Y up in glTF UV space (v down). Provide a 'flip green (DirectX)' toggle for the source map. Self-check (validation feature): for a random 1% of covered texels, decode the source with the old frame and the result with the new frame to object space. Report mean and 95th-percentile angular error in degrees (target < 2 deg; red toast > 5 deg). Node test: a planar quad with a known bumped object-space normal field plus a mirrored and a 90-degree-rotated island, error < 1 deg on the CPU fallback path.

**Risks:** Exact MikkTSpace sign/orientation conventions between three.js (vTBN, flipY) and glTF were NOT verified here, so the self-check and a mirrored-island fixture are mandatory. The Lengyel fallback differs from MikkTSpace on degenerate or very skewed UVs; exporting TANGENT hides this for glTF, but OBJ exports cannot carry tangents (warn users baking normal maps for OBJ targets). Per-pixel versus per-vertex tangent normalization must match the viewer's shader (three normalizes per fragment).

**Sources:** glTF 2.0 specification, Meshes - TANGENT attribute / MikkTSpace recommendation https://registry.khronos.org/glTF/specs/2.0/glTF-2.0.html; donmccurdy/mikktspace-wasm (MikkTSpace as WASM; three.js inlines it as base64) https://github.com/donmccurdy/mikktspace-wasm ; three.js PR #23802 https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/23802; Local vendor/three/GLTFLoader.js r141 l.2899-2959: without a tangent attribute, material.normalScale.y *= -1 (derivative tangents); Local vendor/three/GLTFExporter.js r141 l.1362-1390: attribute 'tangent' exported as TANGENT (validVertexAttributes regex); Lengyel 2001, Computing Tangent Space Basis Vectors for an Arbitrary Mesh https://terathon.com/blog/tangent-space.html

### F78. UV-space G-buffer map baking: position, object-space normal, chart/material ID, island mask, texel-density and distortion maps (MRT on WebGL2, multi-pass on WebGL1)

*Impact* medium · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/gpu/baker.js (bakeGBuffer), src/ui/uv-view.js (display new layers), src/io/exporters.js (per-map export), src/ui/app.js (live texel-density shader mode)`

**What:** One UV-space rasterization writes several data maps at once: world/object position, normals, chart ID, material ID, coverage mask, per-face texel density ratio and per-face distortion. These are useful as deliverables (Substance/Blender-style mesh maps, ID masks for painting), as inputs to the AO/curvature bakers, and as 2D validation views that match exactly what a texture will see.

**Why:** v4 plans exportUVPNG (wire/chart/heat/checker styles drawn from UV lines on a 2D canvas). This bakes real data maps at texel accuracy with correct gutters, in float precision where available, reusing the same rasterization as texture transfer. Texel density and distortion become paintable/exportable layers, and ID maps with exact integer IDs are usable for masking in other tools.

**Sketch:** src/gpu/baker.js bakeGBuffer({res, maps:[...]}). WebGL2 + EXT_color_buffer_float: new THREE.WebGLMultipleRenderTargets(res,res,4); set each texture[i].type=FloatType (position/normal) or UnsignedByteType (ids), minFilter/magFilter Nearest, generateMipmaps=false. RawShaderMaterial{glslVersion:THREE.GLSL3}: VS `in vec3 position; in vec3 normal; in vec2 uvNew; in float chartId; in vec4 faceData; out vec3 vP; out vec3 vN; flat out float vId; flat out vec4 vFace; void main(){vP=position; vN=normal; vId=chartId; vFace=faceData; gl_Position=vec4(uvNew*2.-1.,0.,1.);}`. FS `layout(location=0) out vec4 oPos; layout(location=1) out vec4 oNrm; layout(location=2) out vec4 oId; layout(location=3) out vec4 oFace;` with oPos=vec4((vP-bbMin)/(bbMax-bbMin),1), oNrm=vec4(normalize(vN)*.5+.5,1), oId: R,G = (id+1) as 16-bit split over two bytes (exact up to 65535), B = material id, A=1, oFace=vFace. On a non-indexed soup 'flat' is free: faceData is duplicated per corner. faceData: r = clamp(log2(TD_f/TD_target)/4+.5,0,1) with TD_f = sqrt(A_uv*W*H/A_3d) texels/unit; g = per-face stretch from metrics (e.g. SD/4 or L2 mapped by log); b = flip flag; a=1. WebGL1 fallback: one pass per map into RGBA8; position/normal as 8-bit, or two passes packing 16-bit (hi byte, lo byte). Common settings: DoubleSide, depthTest off, frustumCulled=false, no MSAA (IDs and normals must not be averaged across triangles). Display ramps in the UV view and 3D preview: diverging blue-white-red for density (+/-2 stops), viridis-like for distortion; chart ID colour = HSV(fract(id*0.618034), 0.65, 0.95). Also add a live per-pixel texel-density shader for the 3D viewport (no bake): `float tdx = length(dFdx(vUv)*texSize)/max(length(dFdx(vWorld)),1e-9);` (similarly for y; show min/max). This needs extensions:{derivatives:true} on WebGL1. Readback: readRenderTargetPixels(rt, x,y,w,h, Float32Array) for FloatType (r141 accepts FloatType on WebGL2 / OES_texture_float). Store the bbox in a JSON sidecar/report so position maps are decodable. All maps go through dilation (IDs: nearest copy only) and exporters (finding 9).

**Risks:** EXT_color_buffer_float is missing on some mobile/older Safari, so keep the 8-bit/16-bit-split fallback. MRT with non-raw ShaderMaterial conflicts with three's injected pc_fragColor output in GLSL3, so use RawShaderMaterial (I believe this is what the r141 example does; not re-verified in this session). Sub-texel triangles vanish without conservative coverage; dilation hides it for continuous maps, but ID maps can lose tiny charts (report 'charts smaller than 1 texel').

**Sources:** Local vendor/three/three.min.js r141: WebGLMultipleRenderTargets, glslVersion/GLSL3, EXT_color_buffer_float checks, readRenderTargetPixels float/half-float rules (read in this session); three.js r141 example webgl2_multiple_rendertargets (RawShaderMaterial + layout(location=N) outputs) https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/r141/examples/webgl2_multiple_rendertargets.html; Sander, Snyder, Gortler, Hoppe 2001, Texture Mapping Progressive Meshes (stretch metric used for distortion map) https://hhoppe.com/tmpm.pdf

### F79. Ambient occlusion and bent-normal bake: GPU multi-direction depth-map visibility accumulated in UV space, with a pure-JS BVH ray-traced fallback

*Impact* high · *Effort* L · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `new src/gpu/ao.js; new src/core/bvh.js and src/core/bakeao.js (worker op 'bakeAO'); exporters (ORM R channel / occlusionTexture)`

**What:** AO map baking on the new atlas. GPU path: for K directions on the sphere, render an orthographic depth map of the whole mesh, then rasterize the mesh in UV space. Each texel projects its world position into that depth map, tests visibility, and accumulates V*max(0,n·d) and max(0,n·d); AO = sum(V*ndl)/sum(ndl) (a cosine-weighted estimator). The same accumulation of V*ndl*d gives a bent-normal map. CPU path: a BVH plus cosine-weighted hemisphere rays, range-limited, deterministic and testable in node.

**Why:** Neither v3 nor v4 bakes anything. AO is the most requested 'unwrap for lightmap/AO' deliverable. It also fills the glTF occlusionTexture, which r141 exports with texCoord:1, enabling a zero-loss 'keep original UVs, add AO atlas on TEXCOORD_1' workflow. The GPU path should handle 100k-triangle meshes at K=256 in seconds; the CPU path works in the worker and in tests.

**Sketch:** GPU (src/gpu/ao.js). Directions: Fibonacci sphere d_k: z=1-2(k+.5)/K, phi=k*2.39996323 (golden angle), with a random global rotation per bake to avoid banding. Defaults K=64 preview / 256 normal / 1024 final; depthRes=1024 (2048 final). Bounding sphere (c,r). Depth pass per k: OrthographicCamera(-r,r,r,-r,0,2r) at c + d_k*r looking at c. Custom RawShaderMaterial writes linear depth z = (distance from the camera plane)/(2r) packed into RGBA8 with three's packDepthToRGBA (ShaderChunk 'packing'), or into a FloatType RT when available. DoubleSide (backfaces of open meshes must occlude). Accumulation pass: rasterize the mesh in UV space again (VS outputs gl_Position=uvNew*2-1 and varyings vP, vN; this is portable and needs no float G-buffer). FS: ndl = dot(N,d); if ndl<=0 output zero. p' = vP + N*(1.5*2r/depthRes) (normal-offset bias); project with lightViewProj; zMap = unpack(texture2D(depth, uvL)); 3x3 PCF; V = mean(zp' - 1e-3 <= zMap). Output vec4(V*ndl, ndl, 0, 1); for the bent normal write rgb = V*ndl*d into a second RT (MRT on WebGL2). Accumulate with CustomBlending(AddEquation, OneFactor, OneFactor) into a HalfFloat/Float RT. With no float RT: read back each direction's RGBA8 result and accumulate in a Float32Array in JS (about 5-15 ms per 1024^2 readback), because 8-bit running means stall once increments drop below 1/255. Spread K across animation frames (8-16 directions per frame) with progress and cancel. Final: AO = acc.r/max(acc.g,1e-6) (texels with acc.g=0 get AO 1); bent = normalize(accB.rgb). Optional falloff: count as occluded only if (zp - zMap)*2r < maxDist. This is biased, because the depth map stores the occluder nearest the light (farthest from p). CPU (src/core/bvh.js + src/core/bakeao.js): binned-SAH BVH (12 bins, leaf <= 4 tris) flattened to Float32Array bounds (6 per node) + Int32Array (child/offset,count); iterative traversal with an Int32Array(64) stack; slab test with precomputed invDir; Moller-Trumbore any-hit with tMax = maxDist (default 0.25*bbox diagonal) and early exit. Per covered texel: p,n from barycentric interpolation of the UV raster; cosine hemisphere sample r=sqrt(u1), phi=2*pi*u2, local=(r cos phi, r sin phi, sqrt(1-u1)) in the Duff ONB; u from the R2 sequence (alpha1=0.7548776662, alpha2=0.5698402910) plus a per-texel hash Cranley-Patterson rotation. Origin p + n*1e-4*diag. Progressive 4 -> 16 -> 64 spp in tiles posted from the worker. Denoise with a 5x5 cross-bilateral filter weighted by exp(-|dn|^2/0.1) and position distance, restricted to the same chart id. Tests: unoccluded plane AO=1; a floor texel touching an infinite perpendicular wall gives cosine-weighted AO = 0.5; the inward face of a closed cube gives AO ~ 0.

**Risks:** The depth-map method is infinite-range AO with only a heuristic falloff, so interiors of closed rooms go black; offer the CPU range-limited mode. Shadow-map acne/peter-panning needs tuning (normal offset 1.5 texels, PCF). JS BVH ray throughput (I estimate 1-5 M rays/s) was NOT measured, and the CPU BVH details are from knowledge. geo-ambient-occlusion notes float-texture dependence and Safari issues, hence the RGBA8 packing and readback fallback. Thin double-sided geometry self-occludes unless the normal offset is large enough.

**Sources:** wwwtyro/geo-ambient-occlusion (AO by averaging visibility over many shadow maps from random directions; resolution 512, bias 0.01, 'several hundred' samples; needs float textures) https://github.com/wwwtyro/geo-ambient-occlusion; three.js r141 ProgressiveLightMap (UV-space accumulation via ping-pong FloatType/HalfFloatType targets, mix(old,new,1/window)) https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/r141/examples/jsm/misc/ProgressiveLightMap.js; Duff et al. 2017, Building an Orthonormal Basis, Revisited, JCGT 6(1) https://jcgt.org/published/0006/01/01/; gkjohnson/three-mesh-bvh (reference for BVH ray queries in JS/GLSL; WebGL2) https://github.com/gkjohnson/three-mesh-bvh

### F80. Curvature, cavity and edge-convexity maps from mesh geometry (per-vertex discrete curvature in the worker, rasterized in UV space)

*Impact* medium · *Effort* S · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/core/curvature.js (worker op 'curvature'); baking via src/gpu/baker.js scalar pass`

**What:** Bake a signed mean-curvature map (convex bright, concave dark; the basis of edge-wear and dirt masks in texturing tools), an optional Gaussian-curvature map, and a dihedral-angle edge-convexity mask for low-poly hard-surface meshes. Curvature is computed on the welded mesh in the pure-JS kernel, so UV seams never create fake curvature, then interpolated across triangles by the UV-space rasterizer and dilated.

**Why:** Adds a cheap, high-value map with no GPU requirement that is unit-testable against analytic spheres and cylinders. It reuses mesh.js welding/adjacency and the UV-space rasterizer. It also doubles as a geometry diagnostic (seams belong in high-curvature creases; the segmentation cost could later consume it).

**Sketch:** src/core/curvature.js meanCurvature(mesh, positions, {smoothIters=2, scale='auto'}) -> Float32Array per welded vertex. Cotan Laplacian: for each triangle with corners i,j,k, add 0.5*cot(angle at k) to edge ij, and likewise for the other edges. Laplace vector L_i = (1/(2A_i)) * sum_j (cot a_ij + cot b_ij)(x_j - x_i), with A_i = mixed Voronoi area (Meyer: Voronoi area for non-obtuse triangles; A_tri/2 at an obtuse vertex, else A_tri/4), or barycentric A_tri/3 for simplicity. Signed mean curvature H_i = -0.5*dot(L_i, n_i) with area-weighted vertex normals n_i (sign chosen so a sphere with outward normals gives +1/R; verify in test). Gaussian K_i = (2*pi - sum of incident angles)/A_i (boundary vertices: pi - sum). Clamp cotangents to [-1e3,1e3] for slivers. Multi-scale: smooth H with smoothIters rounds of the uniform 1-ring average. Normalize to a scale-free value: h = H*s with s = k*meanEdgeLength (k=4 default, UI 'radius') and map value = 0.5+0.5*clamp(h,-1,1). Edge convexity for hard edges: for each interior edge with face normals n1,n2 and centroids c1,c2, theta = acos(clamp(dot(n1,n2))), sign = sign(dot(n1-n2, c1-c2)) (convex positive); per-vertex max |theta| with sign gives an edge-wear mask. Alternatively rasterize edges with theta > 20 deg as thick lines in UV space (width 2-4 px) for crisp wear masks. Upload as a float vertex attribute aScalar (duplicated per corner; welded values are continuous across seams), bake with the G-buffer shader (out = vScalar) into RGBA8 or 16-bit gray, then dilate and optionally seam-erase (finding 4). Detail curvature from an existing normal map (optional GPU pass): kappa ~ (n_x(x+1)-n_x(x-1) + n_y(y+1)-n_y(y-1))/2 in tangent space, only where all neighbours share the chart id. Tests: icosphere subdiv 4, radius R: mean |H*R - 1| < 3%; plane H=0; open cylinder radius R: H = 1/(2R) +/-5%; cube edges convex positive, inside of a box negative.

**Risks:** Cotan curvature is noisy on irregular triangulations (CAD/scan meshes), so smoothing and a user radius are required. Mean curvature is resolution-dependent near sharp features. Sign conventions must be tested. Per-vertex values under-resolve large triangles on low-poly meshes (hence the edge-convexity line mode). Formulas are from well-known literature and were not re-read in this session.

**Sources:** Meyer, Desbrun, Schroder, Barr 2003, Discrete Differential-Geometry Operators for Triangulated 2-Manifolds https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-662-05105-4_2 (formulas from knowledge, not re-read in this session); Blender manual, Render Baking (bake types and margin context) https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/cycles/baking.html

### F81. Lossless image export: pure-JS PNG (8/16-bit, gray/RGB/RGBA, no premultiply) and OpenEXR (half/float, NONE/ZIP) writers using CompressionStream('deflate') with a stored-deflate fallback

*Impact* medium · *Effort* S · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `new src/core/imagecodecs.js (encodePNG, encodeEXR, zlib); src/io/exporters.js uses it for map downloads`

**What:** Browser canvas.toBlob can only write 8-bit, premultiplied-alpha PNG/JPEG/WebP. That destroys RGB under alpha=0 (dilated colour under cutouts), rounds normal/position data, and cannot write high-precision position or height maps. Small hand-written encoders give 16-bit PNG and float EXR with native zlib speed through CompressionStream (main thread and workers), plus a pure-JS stored-block fallback so node tests and old browsers still work.

**Why:** The v4 exporter plan only has canvas-based UV PNG styles. These encoders make baked maps production-grade: 16-bit normal/height/AO without banding, float position/curvature EXR for DCC tools, exact non-premultiplied alpha, and deterministic byte output (testable in node by inflating with zlib). They run in the worker (the encoding is DOM-free) with no npm dependencies.

**Sketch:** src/core/imagecodecs.js (DOM-free; place in core so node tests run it). zlib(bytes): if typeof CompressionStream==='function' use `new Response(new Blob([bytes]).stream().pipeThrough(new CompressionStream('deflate'))).arrayBuffer()` (RFC1950 zlib, exactly what PNG IDAT and EXR ZIP need). Else stored deflate: header 0x78 0x01; blocks of <=65535 bytes: byte (final?1:0) (BTYPE=00), LEN u16 LE, NLEN=~LEN u16 LE, raw bytes; trailer Adler-32 big-endian (a=1,b=0; a=(a+byte)%65521; b=(b+a)%65521). PNG: signature 89 50 4E 47 0D 0A 1A 0A; chunk = length u32 BE, type, data, CRC32(type+data) (table polynomial 0xEDB88320). IHDR: width, height u32 BE, bitDepth 8|16, colorType 0 (gray) / 2 (RGB) / 6 (RGBA), compression 0, filter 0, interlace 0. Add an sRGB chunk (1 byte, 0) only for colour maps; omit it for data maps. Scanlines: one filter byte per row; choose per row among None/Sub/Up/Average/Paeth by minimum sum of |signed bytes| (standard heuristic); bpp = channels*(bitDepth/8); 16-bit samples big-endian. IDAT = zlib(all filtered rows); IEND empty. EXR (scanline, single part): magic bytes 76 2F 31 01; version 02 00 00 00; attributes as name\0 type\0 size(int32 LE) value: channels 'chlist' (for each name sorted alphabetically, e.g. A,B,G,R: name\0, int32 pixelType 1=HALF/2=FLOAT, uint8 pLinear 0, 3 reserved zero bytes, int32 xSampling 1, int32 ySampling 1; then a terminating \0), compression 'compression' 1 byte (0 NONE, 3 ZIP), dataWindow 'box2i' (0,0,w-1,h-1), displayWindow 'box2i' same, lineOrder 'lineOrder' 1 byte 0 (INCREASING_Y), pixelAspectRatio 'float' 1.0, screenWindowCenter 'v2f' (0,0), screenWindowWidth 'float' 1.0; header terminator \0. Offset table: ceil(h/linesPerBlock) uint64 LE absolute file offsets (linesPerBlock 1 for NONE, 16 for ZIP). Block: int32 y of first line, int32 dataSize, data = for each line, for each channel (alphabetical), w little-endian samples. For ZIP, if the compressed size >= raw, store raw bytes with dataSize = raw length. EXR row 0 is the TOP line, so flip WebGL readback rows. Float32 to half: standard bit conversion (sign, exponent re-bias with clamp, mantissa>>13, overflow to 0x7C00). Export presets: baseColor PNG8 RGBA sRGB; normal PNG8 (game) / PNG16 (film) linear; AO/curvature PNG8/16 gray; position EXR FLOAT RGB + bbox JSON; ID PNG8 RGB plus a JSON id table. Tests: inflate with node zlib and compare pixels; parse the EXR header back; CRC of a known 1x1 PNG.

**Risks:** Only 'deflate' is needed (Baseline 2023). Very large images (8K RGBA16 is about 512 MB raw) need chunked streaming into the CompressionStream writer rather than one Blob. EXR compression enum values RLE=1 and ZIPS=2 are from knowledge (NONE=0 and ZIP=3 were confirmed). PNG filter/chunk details are from the spec, not re-read in this session. Some viewers ignore the sRGB chunk.

**Sources:** MDN CompressionStream() - 'deflate' is ZLIB (RFC 1950), 'deflate-raw' is RFC 1951; Baseline widely available since May 2023 https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/CompressionStream/CompressionStream; OpenEXR File Layout (magic 20000630, version 2, required header attributes, offset table, scanline blocks, NO_COMPRESSION 1 line / ZIP 16 lines, UINT=0 HALF=1 FLOAT=2, alphabetical channels) https://openexr.com/en/latest/OpenEXRFileLayout.html; W3C PNG Specification (Third Edition) https://www.w3.org/TR/png-3/; Local vendor/three/GLTFExporter.js r141 l.974-1060 (images re-encoded through a 2D canvas via drawImage/putImageData + toBlob)

### F82. glTF material round-trip contract: keep textures through import -> unwrap -> rebake -> export (with verified three.js r141 exporter quirks) plus a zero-loss 'AO/lightmap on TEXCOORD_1' mode

*Impact* high · *Effort* M · *Feasibility* high · verified · *Target* `src/io/loaders.js (extended return contract), src/io/exporters.js (scene-based exportGLB + patchGLBImages + exportOBJWithMTL), docs/ARCHITECTURE.md sections 5.2/5.3 contract update`

**What:** A concrete IO spec so materials survive the pipeline. The loader keeps per-face material index, all maps and factors, texture transforms, UV0/UV1 and normals. The unwrap produces a new UV set, and the user chooses one of two modes. (a) Lightmap mode keeps the original UVs and textures and writes the new atlas as TEXCOORD_1 with baked AO. (b) Re-atlas mode replaces UVs, transfers every map into one merged material and exports it. A post-export GLB image patch swaps in lossless PNGs.

**Why:** The v4 contract (loadFile returns positions+originalUV; exportGLB(positions, uv, name)) loses materials, normals, textures and multi-material structure. This spec turns the toolkit from a UV lab into a usable asset tool, and it avoids six silent r141 exporter pitfalls found by reading the vendored source.

**Sketch:** Loader extension (src/io/loaders.js): return {positions, normals, uv0, uv1|null, tangents|null, faceMaterial: Uint16Array(F), materials:[{name, type, color, opacity, metalness, roughness, emissive, emissiveIntensity, maps:{map, normalMap, normalScale, roughnessMap, metalnessMap, aoMap, aoMapIntensity, emissiveMap, alphaMap}, side, transparent, alphaTest}]}. For each map keep texture.matrix (call texture.updateMatrix(); it encodes KHR_texture_transform offset/repeat/rotation), flipY (false for glTF), encoding, wrapS/T. geometry.groups gives faceMaterial after toNonIndexed. Mode A 'Lightmap/AO atlas': geometry keeps uv (original); set uv2 = new atlas; material.aoMap = baked AO CanvasTexture. r141 exports occlusionTexture with texCoord:1 and uv2 as TEXCOORD_1, so the original textures stay untouched and it is lossless. Mode B 'Re-atlas': the transfer shader (finding 1) bakes baseColor, ORM, emissive and normal (tangent-space finding) for all material groups into one atlas. New MeshStandardMaterial{map, normalMap, roughnessMap: orm, metalnessMap: orm (same texture object), aoMap: orm, emissiveMap, color: white, metalness: 1, roughness: 1, emissive: (1,1,1) only if an emissive bake is non-zero}. Set geometry uv = new, uv2 = uv (same BufferAttribute, needed for aoMap in r141), tangent = tanNew. Reset texture offset/repeat/rotation to identity so no KHR_texture_transform is written. Exporter pitfalls to guard: (1) swap any ShaderMaterial/heat/checker preview material for the export material (r141 returns null for ShaderMaterial); (2) never pass render-target textures (no image data); read back into a canvas first; (3) DataTexture must be RGBAFormat Uint8 AND flipY=false, because r141 flips via ctx.translate/scale but putImageData ignores transforms, so a flipY=true DataTexture would export upside down; prefer CanvasTexture (drawImage honours the flip); (4) canvas premultiplies alpha, so the dilated RGB under alpha=0 texels is lost, which gives mip halos on alpha-tested assets; fix it with (5) the GLB image patch: after GLTFExporter.parse(scene, cb, {binary:true, maxTextureSize}), parse the GLB (12-byte header, JSON chunk 0x4E4F534A, BIN chunk 0x004E4942). For each images[i].bufferView replace the bytes with encodePNG output (finding 9), rebuild BIN with 4-byte alignment, update bufferViews byteOffset/byteLength and buffers[0].byteLength, and re-pad JSON with spaces; (6) emissive colour >1 is clamped, so bake emissiveIntensity into the texture. OBJ export: write an .mtl (map_Kd, map_Bump/norm) plus PNGs; warn that tangent-space normals are not portable without tangents. Round-trip test (in-app 'Verify export' button): reload the produced GLB with GLTFLoader and compare material slots, texCoord indices, image sizes, and a rendered view (finding 11).

**Risks:** Changes the fixed v4 interface (ARCHITECTURE.md 5.2/5.3), so it must be done as an explicit contract revision. Multi-UV-set materials (texCoord != 0 other than AO) are unsupported by the r141 loader (it warns). KHR_materials_* extensions (clearcoat, transmission, etc.) need per-extension map transfer or will be dropped. Skinned/morph meshes: the kernel flattens world transforms, so skinning would be lost; scope this to static meshes and warn. The GLB patch does not cover .gltf (non-binary) exports, whose images are data URIs and need a separate replacement path.

**Sources:** Local vendor/three/GLTFExporter.js r141 (read in this session): ShaderMaterial unsupported l.1131-1135; aoMap written with texCoord:1 l.1247-1252; uv->TEXCOORD_0, uv2->TEXCOORD_1 l.1362-1365; metalness/roughness via buildMetalRoughTexture l.1172-1179; DataTexture must be RGBAFormat and is drawn with putImageData l.1001-1027; flipY applied via ctx transform l.994-999; webp re-encoded as png l.1100; emissive clamped to <=1 l.1196-1204; Local vendor/three/GLTFLoader.js r141: flipY=false l.2733; TEXCOORD_1->uv2 l.1855; uv2 auto-copied from uv for aoMap l.2974-2976; custom texCoord warning l.2853; KHR_texture_transform l.1390-1404; HTML Living Standard, CanvasRenderingContext2D.putImageData is not affected by the transformation matrix https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/canvas.html#dom-context-2d-putimagedata; KHR_texture_transform extension https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_texture_transform

### F83. Bake QA: multi-view render diff (PSNR/SSIM), mip-distance diff and seam-visibility score comparing original vs re-baked asset

*Impact* medium · *Effort* S · *Feasibility* high · **unverified** · *Target* `new src/ui/bake-qa.js; hooks into src/ui/main.js A/B compare and benchmark; metrics recorded in exportReport`

**What:** A validation panel that renders the original model (old UVs + original textures) and the re-baked model (new UVs + transferred textures) from a fixed set of cameras and at several distances (forcing mip levels 0, 2 and 4). It computes PSNR, SSIM and a seam-band error (screen pixels within 2 px of projected seam edges vs elsewhere), and shows a difference heatmap. It extends the v4 A/B compare from UV metrics to what the viewer actually sees.

**Why:** The v4 plan validates UV geometry (stretch, bijectivity, TD CV) but has no way to validate textures. This gives an objective, automatable pass/fail for texture transfer, dilation mode (Extend vs Adjacent Faces vs seam erasure), atlas resolution and padding. Its output can go straight into the benchmark CSV and the JSON report.

**Sketch:** src/ui/bake-qa.js. Views: 14 directions (6 axis + 8 cube-diagonal) at a distance fitting the bounding sphere, rendered at 512x512 into RGBA8 RTs with unlit MeshBasicMaterial (map only). Normal-map checks use a fixed directional-light MeshStandardMaterial with identical lighting for both. Use identical sampler settings (LinearMipmapLinear, same anisotropy). Mip stress: for each view also render at 1/4 and 1/16 viewport scale (then upscale nearest for display), which forces roughly mip 2 and 4 on the same content; this is where gutter bleeding shows. Metrics on readPixels in sRGB-decoded linear luminance: PSNR = 10*log10(1/MSE) over pixels covered in both renders (alpha mask from a separate coverage pass); SSIM on 8x8 windows (step 4) with C1=(0.01)^2, C2=(0.03)^2 on [0,1] values, averaged. Seam band: draw seam edges (cut edges) as 1-px lines into an ID target, then dilate 2 px in screen space to form a mask. SeamError = mean |diff| inside the mask / mean |diff| outside (1.0 means seams are no worse than anywhere else; flag > 2). Also compute texture-space D_total from finding 4 on each baked map. Heatmap overlay: |diff| x 8 with a ramp in the 3D view (toggle). Report per mip scale: {psnr, ssim, seamRatio}. Toast thresholds: PSNR < 30 dB at mip0 or < 26 dB at mip4, or seamRatio > 2, gives a suggestion (increase resolution via 'Match source detail', switch margin to Adjacent Faces, raise packer padding to 2^(L+2)). Add the rows to the A/B snapshot table and the benchmark CSV. Keep everything deterministic (no random rotations in QA renders) so CI screenshots are comparable.

**Risks:** Screen-space metrics depend on view selection and lighting. Sub-pixel rasterization differences between old and new UV renders produce a small nonzero baseline error even for a perfect transfer, so calibrate thresholds by baking with identical UVs (expected PSNR > 40 dB, my estimate). SSIM windowing and thresholds are conventional choices, not tuned in this session.

**Sources:** Wang, Bovik, Sheikh, Simoncelli 2004, Image Quality Assessment: From Error Visibility to Structural Similarity (SSIM) https://doi.org/10.1109/TIP.2003.819861; Liu et al. 2017 Seamless (seam discontinuity D_total as a quantitative seam metric) https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3130800.3130897
